# Rim Prison — design

Total conversion of RimWorld 1.7 into a prison management sim in the spirit of Prison Architect (PA), built on
RimWorld's pawn simulation (needs, moods, traits, health, social, jobs) instead of replacing it. The player is the
warden: builds the facility, hires staff, sets the regime and policies, and deals with what the inmates do.

The mechanics are PA's, the world is RimWorld's. "The state" is the penal authority of the settled worlds, out on the rim: it has no prisons of its
own here, so it sends its convicts (pirates, raiders, slavers, yayo and flake smugglers, deserters, plain thieves) by shuttle and pays the warden for
each. Its shuttle plays the part of PA's road with its buses and trucks (see "The state shuttle"); its marshals bring back who escapes or reoffends.

Design rule: **every system must feed the tension loop.** Needs → mood → tension → misconduct → security response →
punishment/reform → reputation and money. Nothing is decoration.

## Core loop

1. **Money in**: intake grant per prisoner + daily per-prisoner payment by security level; prison-labor exports;
   program completion bonuses; grants (objectives); parole bonuses.
2. **Money out**: staff wages (daily), supply orders (materials, food, furniture parts), program costs, fines
   (deaths, escapes, failed inspections).
3. **Build** cells, canteen, kitchen, showers, yard, workshop, classroom, visitation, solitary, a medical ward (hospital beds; no zone of its own), staff rooms (the staff room zone) and offices (no zone of their own: staff-only or staff-room cells).
   Materials are bought (Supply), brought by the state shuttle and unloaded on the shuttle pad (or in the Deliveries zone), built by workmen (vanilla construction).
4. **Intake** the state shuttle lands daily on the warden's pad; the new inmates walk down its ramp, guards meet them and walk them to a holding area
   (to their cells without one); the released and paroled board the same shuttle on its way out.
5. **Regime** (hourly schedule per security level) moves inmates between cells, canteen, showers, yard, work,
   classes, visitation. Prisoners walk on their own through doors their access allows (PA model; escape = leaving the
   perimeter / map edge).
6. **Needs** unmet → mood drops → **tension** builds (per inmate, spreads socially, amplified by traits/gangs).
7. **Misconduct** (fights, vandalism, contraband, intimidation, escape attempts, riots) → guards respond →
   **punishment** (solitary, lockdown, privilege loss) per player policy.
8. **Reform** programs + work + good conditions → parole, lower reoffending → reputation → better grants & intake.
9. **Report card** daily: safety, health, conditions, reform, security, finances. Inspections audit it.

## Systems

### Crimes (the rim's, and the generic ones)
`RimPrison.CrimeDef`s are drawn by security level (`SecurityLevelDef.crimes`: listing a crime twice doubles its weight at intake); the gang is drawn from the crime (`typicalCrimes` triples the weight of a gang for a crime it lists). The generic ones (theft, fraud, vandalism, drug possession and
dealing, burglary, assault, armed robbery, arson, manslaughter, murder, racketeering, kidnapping, capital murder) stay; the rim's are added with sentences that fit the level: minimum security levy evasion, poaching, desertion, yayo
possession, smokeleaf growing; medium mechanoid salvage theft, wargear dealing, go-juice smuggling, flake trafficking, caravan banditry; maximum piracy, raiding a settlement, slaving, sedition, organ harvesting. The share of violent and of
drug crimes at each level is the old one (`violent` and `drugRelated` drive weights and smuggling chances elsewhere). Gangs got the crimes that fit them. Sentences, life chances and flags: `Defs/Security.xml`.
The shares of violent and drug crimes are the old ones, the sentences and gangs are not quite: with the rim's crimes weighted in, the weighted mean sentence midpoint is about 7% longer at medium and maximum, the life chance at maximum 0.123 (was 0.114), and the maximum level's gang mix moved (Ravens 42%, was 35%; Brotherhood 33%, was 39%; a Saints-Ravens rival pair is about 14% likelier). Worked out from the defs in the review of the shuttle commit, not re-measured in play.

### Prison zones (PA "rooms")
Painted per cell (MapComponent byte grid + overlay + designators): Cell block (implicit via prisoner beds),
Holding, Canteen, Kitchen, Shower, Yard, Workshop, Laundry, Classroom, Library, Chapel, Garden, Mailroom, Visitation, Hearing room, Solitary, Common room,
Staff room, Morgue, Burial plot, Deliveries, Exports, Staff-only, a wing zone for each security level (minimum, medium, maximum, protective custody, death row, supermax, psych) and the supermax yard
(`Defs/PrisonZones.xml` and the defs of the systems that own the rest). There is no Infirmary, Office or Garbage zone: the medical ward is vanilla hospital beds, the offices are staff-only or staff-room cells, and nothing in the prison has garbage to put away. Zones decide where regime activities happen and what
prisoners may enter. Zone validity checks (canteen needs tables + serving counter, shower needs showers, ...) show
problems in the zone inspector.

**The rooms a prison cannot run without** (`PrisonDiagnostics.EssentialZones`): canteen, yard, showers, holding and a kitchen with a stove (the deliveries zone is not one: crates land on the pad). The Rooms screen
says "essential, not painted" beside these (and plain "not painted" beside the rest), and the overview's to-do band and the "prison incomplete" alert list them while they are missing.

**The morgue** (`Zones/Morgue.cs`, `RP_Morgue`, staff-only): without one the body of an inmate or a staff member lies where it fell (vanilla's stockpile presets do not take corpses, so nobody hauls it) and rots in front of everybody,
with vanilla's corpse moods. With one painted, `WorkGiver_HaulToMorgue` (Hauling work: guards, cooks, workmen, janitors; `JobDriver_HaulToMorgue`) carries the body in. Only the prison's own dead (`Morgue.Ours`:
the colony's faction, a prisoner of the colony, an inmate with a security level; people only: the penitentiary's dogs are not) are carried; a raider's is vanilla's business, and so is a body already in storage that takes corpses
(`Morgue.Settled`: vanilla's dumping stockpile or 1.7's corpse stockpile; the guards would carry it out and vanilla's hauling back, for as long as both stood). A full morgue answers once a tick (`Morgue.Full`). The overview lists
uncollected bodies while there is no morgue, and bodies that have lain outside a morgue for over an hour (full, out of reach, or forbidden: vanilla forbids a body outside the home area). 

**A death inside: the dead go home, the family writes, the plot** (`Story/Condolences.cs`, `Zones/Burial.cs`; gap analysis 4, 1.3). At every lift-off `ShuttleSystem.Depart` calls `CondolenceSystem.Ship` after the exports: every body of the
prison's own (`Morgue.Ours`) that lies in the morgue is loaded (the exports rule: in the zone at lift-off counts), destroyed, and written in the chronicle (`RP_Chr_BodySent`, `stats.bodiesSent`). One that is not in the morgue stays where it is (no
morgue, no shuttle for it). *Family:* an inmate's is whoever of `Relatives.Of` is still in touch (`CanVisit`); a member of staff has one unless they were a callout officer (`CompStaff.leaveAtTick >= 0`, who had no family letter from the
prison before either). When there is a family a `Condolence` (kind, name, relative's label, staff, due tick: `Story.condolences`, scribed) is queued for one to three days later, then `CondolenceSystem.Hourly` delivers it as a letter to the warden:
`RP_Mail_Condolence` (a neutral letter, a chronicle line) or `RP_Mail_CondolenceBitter` (a negative letter, a bigger line, `MailKindDef.reputation` -1). Both are `MailKindDef`s whose worker never sends them to an inmate
(`MailKindWorker_Condolence.CanSend` is false, weight 0): the kind is the data (label, chronicle line, importance, reputation), the delivery is the system's. **Bitter** is decided at the death, in the kill's prefix: a killing by staff
or neglect (the two deaths that open a state inquiry, `DeathAftermath.Notify_Died`; for staff, a death with no hand in it from an illness or starvation, `StaffDeaths`) puts the pawn's thing id in `Story.bitterDeaths` (scribed, capped at 200;
removed when the body goes or is buried). A murder by another inmate is not bitter (decision: "custody killing" is read as a killing by the prison's own). The staff-death letter that asks about paying the family stays as it was (it
is the warden's decision about the family; the new letter is the family's answer once the body is home). *No family:* with a **burial plot** (`RP_BurialPlot`, staff-only zone on bare ground) a workman, and only a workman
(`WorkGiver_BuryBody`, Hauling, `JobDriver_BuryBody`: carry, dig for 360 ticks, lay in), buries the body: a vanilla `Grave` (1 x 2, the prison's) is built where the body is laid and `Notify_HauledTo` gives it the grave's name,
`RP_Chr_Buried` is written, `stats.buried` counted, and any `FormerInmate` whose news is still to come and whose `friendIds` has the dead one gets `graveName`, so that their news (`FormerInmates.Deliver`) is `RP_FormerNews_Grave` (it comes
first, is a neutral message, and does not cheer the friends still inside). The grave takes two cells of the plot with soil under them (the grave's own affordance: a floor does not do) and nothing built on them. Decision: a vanilla grave
in a plot zone, not a sub-area of the morgue: it shows, it carries a name, and nothing new is scribed. **A plot that cannot take a body does not hold it** (`Burial.Wants`: no family and a plot painted; `Burial.Wanted`: and `Burial.Capacity` > 0): `Capacity` counts the graves that fit side by side (never one over another) on soil, inside the grounds
(`PrisonGrounds.Allows`: a grave is a new building, and what stands outside the grounds only works), on ground an on-duty workman can reach. `Ship` keeps only as many wanted bodies as there are graves (the oldest first); the rest go on the shuttle like a body with a
family does, with no letter if nobody would write, and the warden gets one message (`RP_BodiesSent` and `RP_BodiesNoPlot`) saying the plot could not take them. So a full plot, a plot on a floor or stone, a walled-off plot, a plot outside the grounds or no workman on
duty never clogs the morgue, and a prison without a plot never does. Only a body that is *wanted* and lies in the plot is settled (`Morgue.Settled`): a body in the plot that the plot will not bury (a guard with a family killed there, the last site taken mid-job) is carried
to the morgue by the guards like any other. A burial that falls back to another site (`TryBury`) picks one the workman can reach (`FindSite(hauler)`). Raiders' and animals' bodies stay vanilla's. *Layouts:* both prisons have a plot of bare soil, the inherited facility's
east of the deliveries outside the south wall, the penitentiary's behind the morgue; the showcase check no longer exempts it. **Not built:** a memorial at the grave, a cost for the grave.

**The danger map** (`Security/Danger.cs`; gap analysis 4, 1.4). `PrisonMap.incidents` (`IncidentMark`: cell, `DangerKind`, tick; 30 days, at most 400, trimmed when the next is made, scribed, null-pruned) is where **seen** violence happened.
`MisconductUtility.Record` writes one when the misconduct was seen (`seen`: loud, or found by staff, or watched) and its def has a `danger` kind (`RP_Fight`, `RP_Ambush` fight; `RP_AssaultStaff`, `RP_HostageTaking` assault; `RP_Riot` riot,
the ringleader only: a riot joined is not another start); a fight with a weapon in the hand (`equipment.Primary`) is a stabbing **only where the weapon was seen** (a guard, camera or tower watching, or evidence: a fight is loud and always marked, a shiv drawn in a blind spot is
known to nobody and the mark stays a plain fight, `DangerMap.Note`); the cell is where the violence was (`DangerMap.SceneOf`: the one set on, for an assault or a social fight, else where the one who did it stood). Only **violent** deaths are marked
(`DangerMap.Violent`: a blow from another pawn, or a wound, bleeding out or infection from one; illness, old age, hunger, thirst, cold and heat are not violence), where there is a body (`DeathAftermath.Notify_Died` and `StaffDeaths.Notify_Died`; executions are not deaths in custody).
A member of staff who dies of an inmate's blow later (bled out, festered) is a killing, not neglect: `CompStaff.assaultedTick` (scribed, set when an inmate's blow lands) within a day keeps the bitter letter off (`DeathAftermath.RecentlyAssaulted`). A pawn who is resurrected loses
the note of fault (`CondolenceSystem.ClearFault`, `Notify_Resurrected`), so a later death is judged on its own. The hidden-information rule holds by construction: an ambush nobody saw (`seen` false: no guard, camera or tower covering it) is not recorded;
a murder record is not marked (its death is). Weights: fight 1, assault 2, stabbing 3, riot 3, death 4; a mark counts less as it ages (down to 0.3 at 30 days, `DangerMap.Fade`). `DangerOverlay` (`PrisonMap.DangerShown`, toggle with the
other overlays, icon of the melee attack) glows every cell within 3 of a mark in the colour of the worst one near it, deeper where more and worse, rebuilt when a mark is made and once an hour, with a letter on each mark (F, S, A, R, D;
only with the camera zoomed in). The Deployment tab (its patrol list is a scroll view, so the danger section never cuts guards off the list) lists `DangerMap.Hottest(pm, 3)`: marks grouped by connected patch of a painted zone (`ZoneGrid.PatchAt`; ground no zone covers: a block of eight cells), each named by its zone and
the average of its marks' cells, with what happened ("2 fight(s), 1 stabbing(s)") and a jump button. Each patch has a "post a guard here" button (see the guard posts under Security). Not built: camera advice on the map, a per-wing total in the weekly report.

**Meal bills** (`Labor/Kitchen.cs`, `KitchenBills`): a stove in the kitchen zone gets a standing target-count meal bill by itself (`10 + 4 x cell beds` meals, the menu's recipe, bulk from 24 beds), whoever built it: when it is built, when the
kitchen is painted under it, and every hour. A stove is billed once (`PrisonMap.billedStoves`), so a bill the warden deletes stays deleted; a bill left as made grows with the beds, one the warden edited keeps their target
(`PrisonMap.cookBills`, the target the prison last set). The starter facility's stoves get theirs through the same code (`PrisonBuilder.AddCookBill` only adds fuel). A stove that has a bill of the warden's is remembered too, so deleting it
keeps it deleted; a save from before stoves were billed keeps its stoves as they are (`PrisonMap.stovesRemembered`: some were left unbilled so that only inmates cook), and what the prison remembers of stoves and bills that are gone is forgotten. From 24 beds a bill left
as made goes bulk. A bill swapped for another recipe (a menu change, the bulk step: `KitchenMenuPolicy.Replace`) is a bill of its own with its own number and keeps the memory of its target (a clone has no number until `InitializeAfterClone`, and two with one number are one
bill to a save; `Ensure` renumbers such bills in an old save). With the fine menu and nobody on the staff at Cooking 6, a new stove's bill is for the simple meal and a message says why; changing the menu to fine with nobody who can cook it swaps nothing (`KitchenMenuPolicy.Apply`) and says so, and every hour the prison's own bills follow the cooks (`KitchenMenuPolicy.Follow`, from `Ensure`): simple when the last cook who can make the fine meal has gone, fine again when one is on the staff, with a message each way. "Somebody on the staff" is the cook role's people on site or at home (`KitchenBills.Cooks`: a cook off shift is still the prison's cook, a guard who has the skill is not who cooks); a bill the warden made or edited is theirs and is left alone (only `cookBills` follow). Staff respect an inmate cook's claim on a stack of the kitchen's raw food as they respect a
colleague's (`Patch_ReservationManager_CanReserve_InmateClaimsInKitchen`: vanilla does not make a colonist respect a prisoner's reservation; a physical-interaction reservation, which an inmate chewing holds, is vanilla's own test in `CanReserve`).
### The grounds, and buying land (`Core/Grounds.cs`, `UI/LandTab.cs`, `UI/GroundsOverlay.cs`, `Core/GroundsDebug.cs`)
As in Prison Architect the prison owns a plot, not the map. `PrisonGrounds` (scribed on `PrisonMap.grounds`) is a rectangle of owned cells, the rectangle the game started with (what dev mode goes back to) and the number of
parcels bought. **Building and painting are allowed inside it only; pawns walk anywhere** (the road, the map edge, commuting staff, visitors). The refusals are Harmony postfixes that only look (the designators ask every frame
the cursor moves): `GenConstruct.CanPlaceBlueprintAt_NewTemp` (the whole footprint must be inside, and for the shuttle pad its hull and ramp too), refusing **only the warden's own placements**: the test is asked from `Designator_Build` (walls, floors, furniture, copy, replace) and
`Designator_Install` (install, reinstall), which count their ask (`Patch_Designator_Place_CanDesignateCell_Grounds`, a finalizer undoes it), and nothing else is refused: a raid's siege blueprints, a mech cluster, a prefab, a pod launcher, a trap or firefoam popper put back, and a building auto-rebuilt
after it was destroyed (it asks the same test with no designator, and is not the warden's click) all go on as in vanilla. God mode is judged by `DebugSettings.godMode`, so it is exempt for an install too. `Designator_ZoneAdd.IsZoneableCell`
(vanilla's growing zones, stockpiles and fishing zones all ask it) and `Designator_PrisonZone.CanDesignateCell` (Rooms; taking paint off outside is allowed). The text is "Outside the prison's grounds -
buy the land first (Office, Land)." (a dash, not a colon: the game capitalises the letter after ": "). A refused click (`Designator.FinalizeDesignationFailed`) is told to the grounds when the dragger's reason is our text (walls, floors and zones are dragged), or, for a designator
with no draw style (a bed, the shuttle pad, an install: clicked once, vanilla keeps the reason with the cell, not the dragger), when asking it again about the cell under the mouse gives our text (`Patch_Designator_FinalizeDesignationFailed.RefusedForGrounds`): the
overview's to-do band gets a line for a day (or until land is bought) that opens the Land tab, and the learning helper teaches `RP_Concept_Grounds`. The line asks for land only when it can be bought: while special measures run it says the state sells none, and with no strip left to sell there is no line.
**The shuttle always finds somewhere to land** (`ShuttleSystem.SearchSite`): a site on the grounds first (hull, ring and ramp inside, unpainted ground first), and when nothing there is clear (the plot is full, the pad is roofed, cut off or burnt, the grounds were shrunk) the nearest clear ground
just beside them (`FindSiteBesideGrounds`: outward from holding, else from the grounds' edge nearest to it; landing is not building, and the state's shuttle is not the warden's). A site found beside the grounds is kept like any other (`KnownFallbackSite` asks `SiteFits` without the grounds), and the
pad's alerts (no pad, a pad that cannot be used) say what is wrong; no new letter. A pad
already outside after a shrink keeps working (shrinking never deletes anything).

*Where it starts.* The inherited facility and the state penitentiary own `PrisonBuilder.Footprint` (the walls plus the road, pad and deliveries south of the gate) and **12 cells** on each side; a new facility owns a centred 56 x 56 square and the same margin, so 80 x 80.
All clamped to the map less **10 cells** along its edge: vanilla's no-build edge (`GenGrid.InNoBuildEdgeArea`), so no land is sold that cannot be built on (on a pocket map or a layer that ignores the edge, vanilla's no-zone edge, 5 cells: `PrisonGrounds.Margin`). The Land tab's plan outlines that land. The start is set by `ScenPart_PrisonStart.PostGameStart` after the builder has run. A save from before land has no grounds
(`defined` false: `Contains` is true everywhere, nothing is for sale, and the Land tab says so); dev mode's first move there defines them from the whole map.

*Buying.* The Land tab (Office, next to Grants and Bureaucracy; `Tab.Land`) shows a plan of the plot, and for each side a parcel: a strip **15 cells** deep along the whole side (cut short where the edge margin begins; none at it), the price, and Buy, off with the reason
(`Parcel.blocked`: the edge, special measures, not enough money). Pointing at a parcel draws it on the map. Price = cells x $2 x (1 + cells owned / 12,000) x the difficulty's land lever, rounded to ten: the first
strip of the inherited facility (it starts with 88 x 82 cells, so a strip is 82 or 88 x 15) costs about $4,000 at the neutral difficulty (the penitentiary's, 144 x 146 owned, about $12,000), each purchase makes the next dearer, and the difficulty (`PrisonDifficulty.Levers.land`, shown in the New prison pane) scales it from 60% (peaceful) to 150% (losing is fun). `PrisonGrounds.TryBuy` spends from the
ledger (`RP_Land`), moves the side at once, writes a chronicle line, counts `landParcels`/`landCells` (Reports, Statistics), clears the to-do line and marks the note learned. **Special measures forbid it** (`SpecialMeasures.Restricted`: the state sells no land while it
is looking over the books: only while the measures run, `SpecialMeasures.Running`; the state's chief in post after they failed does not close the sale, so a full prison under a chief can still get room). **The price is fixed when the game starts**: `PrisonGrounds.landFactor` (saved) is the difficulty's land lever at `SetStarting`
(grounds saved without it take the lever in force when they first load, or when dev mode first defines them), so Options, Change storyteller cannot cheapen land; the Land tab shows the fixed figure. **Buy is off, with the reason, when the purchase would leave less than tonight's wages** (`BlockedFor`: the staff's payroll, what the midnight settlement pays; the
prison is never put into debt by land). Not built: land as a grant reward, or a neighbour's land offered by a story beat.

*On the map.* The border is the outermost ring of owned cells in yellow, the land outside is dimmed while the Architect or Rooms screen is open or a build or zone tool is in hand, and the toggle sits with the other overlays (bottom right): it hides the line only, the dimming is the building warden's sign of
where the land ends and stays. Each is a `CellBoolDrawer` (a mesh built once and rebuilt only when `PrisonGrounds.version` changes; on a 400 map the dimming mesh is about 150k quads, so a purchase or a dev click while building costs a short hitch, by design); the parcel pointed at in the Land tab is an outline of its cells
(`GenDraw.DrawFieldEdges`, the cell list kept while the same one is pointed at), not a pass over the map when the pointer moves. The penitentiary's road runs from the map's south edge and leaves the starting grounds: it is dimmed, and cosmetic (nothing rebuilds there by itself).

*Dev mode.* In the Land tab: each side by -10, -5, -1, +1, +5, +10 for nothing (clamped to the map's edge margin on the way out and to 10 cells on the way in), the whole map, the starting grounds. The debug actions menu ("Rim Prison": each side +/-10, every side +/-10, whole map,
starting grounds) does the same. The New prison dialog in dev mode has a row of four fields (north, east, south, west) for the land owned beyond the facility, at the bottom beside Start (`NewPrisonOptions.groundsMargins`, handed to the start through `PrisonGrounds.PendingMargins`, which
every `new Game()` clears, so margins from a start that never used them cannot leak into the next game).
*By design, for dev mode and the edges.* Moving a side back to the start (`SetBackToStart`) takes the plot back to the starting rectangle and zeroes `parcelsBought`; what was paid stays paid, and the statistics' land counters (`landParcels`, `landCells`, the Land tab's "bought so far") are lifetime history, so buying again pays again. Any dev move on a save from before land defines the grounds from the whole map
(less the edge): that save is limited for good, and "Starting grounds" cannot go back to undefined. Shrinking under buildings is harmless: what stands outside stays and works, blueprints and frames outside are still built, a pad outside still takes the shuttle (`ProblemOf` does not look at the grounds), a known landing site outside is kept. What the grounds do **not** stop, by design: roofs (`Designator_AreaBuildRoof`
builds wherever there are supports), smoothing walls and floors, painting (`Designator_Paint*`) and filling in, which change what is already there; a gravship landing (Odyssey, not a blueprint); another mod's placement code that skips `GenConstruct.CanPlaceBlueprintAt` (most call it, and are covered if they ask from a build designator).

### Doors
`CompPrisonDoor` on every door: mode **Open / Staff only / Locked**, plus *Cell door* (follows lockup).
`Building_Door.PawnCanOpen` postfix: inmates pass a door only if it is not staff-only/locked and the regime currently
grants access to the zones on either side (cell doors open only outside Sleep/Lockup). Staff pass everything not
Locked. Reachability cache is cleared on regime/door changes. A guard who has an inmate by the arm (a supermax shower or yard walk, a supermax inmate
fetched back to the cell, a parole escort) opens the doors on the path of that walk to that inmate, and only those: `CompInmate.leg.doors` is the set of
doors the guard's own path from where the inmate stands to where they are going crosses (the inmate's own cell door and the door of the cell they stand in
among them, never another inmate's cell door; a staff-only door on the path is in it, one that is not on it is not), planned on the main thread when the
walk begins and again when it turns for home, and read on path threads (see Supermax below).

### Regime
24 slots × security level. Activities: Sleep, Lockup, Eat, Shower, Yard (free time), Yard alone (supermax), Work, Programs, Visitation.
Inmate AI (think tree inserted at `Humanlike_PostDuty`) resolves activity → target zone → job. Needs can override
within the current zone (toilet in cell, drink, sleep if exhausted).

**The regime pulled** (`Regime/RegimeFeedback.cs`, `Regime/RegimeOrders.cs`, `Events/Demands.cs`, `Story/Inspector.cs`, `UI/RegimeTab.cs`, `Keyed/Regime.xml`; gap analysis 6, W1). Three things in the Regime tab so that the schedule is a lever the warden touches, not a setting made once.
- **The strip** under each level's row: yesterday, hour by hour, as bytes on `PrisonMap.regime` (not saved: a reload loses a day, and the tooltip of an hour with no numbers says so). `PrisonMap.OnHourChanged` calls `RegimeFeedback.Sample` at the hour that ends a slot, before the regime moves anyone (where the turf is sampled): per level, the inmates in sample, the share in place (`RegimePlacement.InExpectedPlace` for the slot they were in, as the compliance grade reads it), and for the last hour of a run of meals the hungry inmates who had not eaten in it (`MealTally.Eaten` notes the meal; a hunger striker is not counted). Misconducts are noted as recorded (`MisconductUtility.Record`, the hour they happened in). The day's last hour closes the day into `Yesterday`. The tab draws bytes only; a tooltip's text is built for the hour under the mouse.
- **Demands that name the timetable**: the outdoors need (`RPVanillaDefOf.Outdoors`) joins `DemandSystem.CandidateNeeds` (joy was already there). A demand about either (`TimetableNeed`) is put for one level (`WorstLevel`: the biggest share low on the need, two inmates at least, not a level that has eight of the slot already) and asks for the next hour of free time ("a second yard hour"; the hour alone in the yard for a level that never leaves its cells without a guard); the letter carries the level (`note`) and the number of hours a day (`amount`), and the promise (`InmateDemand.slotLevel/slotActivity/slotTarget/slotRan`, saved with defaults) is judged on the schedule: the hours of the slot that ran in the last 24 hours before the deadline (`TallySlotHour`, from the hour pass, reading `PrisonGame.SlotAt`, so a day's orders count), against the target, whatever the need's average is. Every other promise is judged on the average as before. A riot's or a hostage-taker's grievance can be about these needs too: the promise is then made for the worst level the same way (and the warden is told what was promised).
- **Today's orders** (`PrisonGame.dayOrders`, saved with defaults, per level): the yard called off (free time, and the supermax yard hour, are lockups), early lockup (from 17:00 or now, everything but sleep, meals and showers), a second shower (the next hour of the day that is not sleep, a meal or a shower). One-day overrides read by `PrisonGame.SlotAt`, which every reader of the timetable that matters now goes through (`ActivityAt`, `ConfinedAlone`, funerals' service hours, the yard sanction, the HUD's now/next); the timetable itself is never edited, the painter shows the hours an order changed. Given and withdrawn in the tab (one "Today..." button per row opens a menu of the three; a tag beside the level's name shows what is in force and its tooltip when it lapses), with a chronicle line each; the days a level was given an order that keeps it in (yard off, early lockup) are kept for a week (`PrisonGame.orderDays`, saved), and a level so ordered on more than three of the last seven days is a finding for the inspector (`orders:<level>`) and a line of the weekly report; `RegimeOrders.Sweep`, run by the hour pass after the day's last hour is sampled, ends every order stamped with another day (so a day that was slept through still ends them) with a "back to the regime" line; it interrupts nobody itself: `OnHourChanged` reads each inmate's previous-hour slot under the old orders before the sweep and queues those whose slot differs now through the staggered interrupt queue.
- **The inspector** writes down a level whose timetable keeps its inmates in their cells (lockup and sleep) more than 20 hours of the day (`InspectionReport.LockedLevels`; not supermax or death row, not a level nobody is in, the timetable and not today's orders): only the warden can put it right, so it is a finding like the others, with the Regime tab as its to-do line.

**The adjudication sheet** (`Security/Adjudication.cs`, `Security/Punishment.cs`, `Story/Trials.cs`, `UI/AdjudicationUI.cs`, `Keyed/Adjudication.xml`; gap analysis 6, 5). Serious offences are decisions, not a table.
- **Policy** `PrisonGame.adjudicate` (Policy tab, saved, off by default: an old save is unchanged). `Sanctions.Apply` hands a seen offence the sheet takes (`Adjudication.Takes`: severity 3 and over, not a mark, not murder - it has its trial) to `Adjudication.Queue`, which writes a case with the evidence as it was on the spot (`CourtUtil.CameraOver`, a guard who sees the cell by `TensionUtility.GuardWhoSees` or a manned tower, staff who found it directly, the weapon in hand, the serious offences already on the file); otherwise `ApplyPolicy` is the old body. A restraint after an adjudicated offence no longer sends them to the table's hole (`CompInmate.PolicyHoursForLatest`), only the few hours for acting out.
- **The sheet** (`AdjudicationBook` on `PrisonGame`, saved with defaults): `Adjudication.Morning` runs at the first morning hour (6:00, `SheetHour`). What was listed and left (no letter, no window) takes the policy's sanction; then, if no sheet is out and none went today, yesterday's cases (oldest `MaxPerSheet` = 6, the rest wait) go on one `RP_Decision_Adjudication` letter. Not urgent, so the day's budget (`StoryLetters`) holds it like any other (a held sheet still counts as out). The letter's options open the window (`Dialog_Adjudication`, also from the Policy tab) or give the policy's sanction to all; unanswered it is the policy's sanction for all, after `SheetAnswerHours` (22: under a day, so it has timed out before the next morning's pass). An open window does not hold the day's sheet back (only a letter does); the book is the game's, so whichever home map's pass comes first writes the one sheet for every map, and a case is decided on the map its inmate is on (`Adjudication.HomeOf`). Answered cases are kept 30 days.
- **The answers** (`Adjudication.Answer`; one that cannot be given falls back to the policy's sanction and says why): *policy* - `Sanctions.ApplyPolicy`; *loss of remission* - `remissionEarned` goes back onto `sentenceDays` and is zeroed, `cleanDays` zeroed (nothing earned: refused); *days added* - 1 to 30 (`MaxDays`, suggested 5/10/15 by severity) onto `CompInmate.daysAdded`; with no officer and no camera, a jailhouse lawyer anywhere in the prison appeals at once (`ReputationBehavior_Lawyer.CourtCosts` to the prison, `appealsFiled`) and the days are struck out; without a lawyer they stand and the cause shown is "unfair" (`RP_Cause_Unfair`, more tension); a lifer or death row has none to add to; *charge* - an assault on staff (`MisconductDef.chargeDays` set) with a weapon, the officer still here.
- **The term** `CompInmate.TermDays` = `sentenceDays + daysAdded` (-1 for life): release (`ReleaseSystem.SentenceServed`), parole eligibility (`WantWorker_Parole`, `Grading`, `DrugTesting`'s next-event date), the parole texts and scene, `ParoleLife`, the old-age file, the register's bar, the file's bar (with "(n added)"), the chronicle, the jailhouse lawyer's appeal (`CutTerm` takes the days added first, never below the days served) and the appeal event. `ResetForNewStay` clears `daysAdded`. Remission's last-day rule reads the term.
- **The charge** reuses `Trials` with an `offence` on `MurderTrial` (saved; murder when absent, so an old save's cases are murder cases). `Open` is `ReadScene` (the prosecution's file as the scene is: witnesses in sight, the camera, the weapon, the record) plus `Register` (dated from now, listed, merged into an open case); the sheet keeps the scene as it was on the day (`AdjudicationCase.scene`, saved) and registers it on a charge. Remand, assembly, witnesses got at, the court day, the odds are untouched. What differs: a conviction adds `chargeDays` (10 to 20 for assaulting staff; no life, no maximum security) and an acquittal leaves the offence on the file; the words (`Trials.Key`, "_Charge" variants); a later killing by the accused turns the case into the murder case.
- **Inspector**: the days added in the last 7 days over `WeekLimit` (60) are a finding (`added`, Policy tab as its to-do line), gone when the week falls to the limit, as the orders' habit is.
- Not in: an "unfair" cause in `TensionUtility.HourlyUpdate` (the string exists only for a days-added case nobody saw; the model had none to reuse), the state inquiry's notice, `Grading`'s score.

### Inmates
`CompInmate` on humanlikes: security level (Min, Med, Max, Supermax, Protective custody, Death row), crime, sentence
(days), days served, reputation traits (Volatile, Instigator, Snitch, Gang leader, Escape artist, Ex-cop, Tough,
Fearless, Model prisoner, Addict, Celebrity, Notorious...), gang, tension, suppression, misconduct record,
punishment state, program record, parole state, family (visitor count), contraband stash.
Personality = RimWorld traits + backstory + reputations; they drive misconduct type and likelihood.

### Needs (inmates; staff keep vanilla)
Vanilla: Food, Rest, Comfort, Beauty, Outdoors (≈ freedom), Room size. Vanilla Joy enabled for inmates (recreation).
New: Hygiene (shower), Bladder (toilet), Family (visits/phone), Exercise (weights bench in the yard or common room, free time), Literacy
(library books read in the cell; only for those who can read). Unmet needs give thoughts. Both new needs start gentle: a slow fall,
exercise gives a mood drain and the "restless" tension cause only below 0.2, literacy never drains (a library is a gain, not a duty).
Exercise is not for death row (cell meals); literacy is filled by a book left at the cell door by the library clerk (a trusted minimum
security inmate with a book cart, see Needs/Library.cs; several clerks never go to the same inmates, and the cart stops at the cells' doors in order) and helps the education programs' exams (up to 10 points).
Lifting makes violent inmates Tough, but only up to a quarter of the prison; a weight plate off the bench hits harder than a fist but not as hard as a shiv; a donated book may hide a drug (never a tool or a phone: nothing screens books), which the stash and the debt hook treat as the post's.

### Tension, suppression, misconduct
Tension 0–100 per inmate, from mood, unmet needs, crowding, rival gang presence, instigators nearby, recent
punishments perceived unfair. Suppression from guard presence, CCTV, recent harsh punishments; high suppression
lowers misconduct but raises tension slowly (PA trade-off). Misconduct roll per inmate per hour picks an act weighted
by personality: argue, fight, vandalize, steal tool, hide contraband, deal drugs, intimidate/extort, assault staff,
escape attempt (alone or planned group: tunnels with spoons, fence climb, guard uniform), riot (zone-wide).

**Gang turf.** A gang holds a connected patch of a yard, common room or canteen (`gangTurf` on the zone def) after a day in which
60% or more of the inmates seen there were its members (three or more at once); it keeps it down to 40%, and loses it below that,
to a gang that reaches 60%, or when nobody of it is left inside. Presence is counted in one pass over the inmates in the hour
pass and judged in the daily settlement; claims are saved per map (`PrisonMap.turf`), the day's tally is not. A claim follows its patch
through a repaint (to the patch that has most of the cells its own had; merged patches keep the claim with more of them), quietly, and a war's
stake moves with it. Rival members on held turf
get +1 tension (under the 1.2 of one rival member nearby) and +3 fear, holders -0.6 tension and -2 fear, dealers deal at 0.8 instead of
0.5, leaders recruit at 0.2 instead of 0.1. A gang war is fought over a patch (`StoryArc.stake`, picked when it starts): attacks prefer
it and then turf of either gang, a blow on the victims' own ground is a raid (counted net of the holders' answers, per raiding gang, in
any order), and a patch raided twice more than answered goes to the raiders when their war ends, if they still have someone inside (a truce
settles nothing; the end of another war touches none of these raids). Hidden gangs stay hidden: the overlay, rooms line, intel
tab, chronicle and letters name a holder only when its gang is known, and a hidden gang's turf is not shown at all.

**Gang structure and a way out** (`Security/GangRanks.cs`, `Security/GangExit.cs`, `Defs/GangStructure.xml`, keys in `Keyed/GangStructure.xml`).
*Ranks.* `CompInmate.gangRank` is member, lieutenant or leader; the leader is also the old `gangLeader` flag, which the rest of the code reads, and
`GangRanks.SetRank` keeps the two in step (never write the flag alone). In the daily pass a gang with no lieutenant makes its most-tenured violent member
one (`CrimeDef.violent`; one new lieutenant a day, a second for a gang of six or more inside). The rank shows only where the gang does (the file's chip,
the inspect line: a known gang's). *Succession.* When a leader leaves for good (`StorySystem.Notify_Leaving`, called last: release, transfer, death, escape)
the gang's lieutenant leads, with a chronicle line once the gang is known (naming them only if their own membership is known too). Two lieutenants contest
it in `RP_Arc_Succession` (`SuccessionArc`: both wound up, no leader meanwhile; 18-42 hours on they settle it where they meet, a fight on the record, or, when they keep
apart for three days, the stronger takes it; the loser is a member and the winner's enemy; if one leaves the other leads). A second word of the same
departure, or a contest already running, does nothing. A contest that cannot be held (a contender is cast in another story) is no fight: the more tenured takes the post, and the line does not tell of one.
*One leader per gang.* The post is held by one: an arrival is never rolled leader of a gang that has one or whose post is being fought over, a contest that finds a leader by the time it settles
ends without a crown, and someone back from outside (`Reoffenders.Return` -> `GangRanks.Notify_Returned`: rearrested, or a fugitive brought back) keeps the rank only if there is nobody in it: a leader
leads a gang with no leader and no contest, a lieutenant is a plain member (the daily pass picks one again). The daily pick leaves out a member who wants out of the gang and an enemy of the leader (the
loser of a contest). A leader taken off the wing by the warden (a challenge's option) still leaves the gang to drift, as before.
*The wish to leave.* A member who is not the leader gets doubts (`CompInmate.gangDoubtTick`) from a birth letter (`Wants.Notify_FamilyNews`), a mentor pairing
(`Mentors.Start`, the newcomer), any program passed (`Wants.Notify_ProgramPassed`: the vocational programs of the other task count too) and a friend killed while their
gang is at war (`GangExit.Notify_Died`); the daily pass rolls the doubt once (`GangExit.DoubtChance`, 50%, stale after three days) into the goal `RP_Want_LeaveGang`
(priority 2: it puts aside a lesser goal in progress, which says so). A doubt is not rolled at the event itself so the goals the event forms (a birth's family goal) are
undisturbed. *Hidden:* while the gang is not on the file `Wants.Quiet` hides the goal and silences its lines; the session is what tells. *Steps.* (1) A session with the
chaplain (ministry) or a counsellor (counselling): `WorkGiver_GangExitSession` (candidates in `InmateCandidates.ExitSession`) and `JobDriver_GangExitSession` (a
talk of 1,200 ticks): the inmate confides, so their gang is revealed (not the others') and the renouncement is set six hours on (`InmateWant.step/stepTick`). (2) The renouncement
(`GangExit.Renounce`, from `WantWorker_LeaveGang.Fulfilled`): they leave the gang (`gang` null, rank and tattoo cleared; a gang war's cast drops them, and a leadership challenge they were in is
void, `GangChallengeArc.Valid`), `exGang`/`exGangTick` mark an ex-member, and if the old gang has anyone inside who can reach them (`GangExit.Reachable`: free, on their wing, and the leaver not
in protective custody) its green light goes on (`exMarked`), else nothing is said or asked: a threat at once (tension, fear, a memory, the debts' constants), a beating `DebtSystem.BeatingDelayDays`
later and every `RepeatDays` days after, three at the most (`GangExit.Hourly`: the highest-ranked old-gang member who can reach them, `DebtSystem.CanBeat`, goes for them: a
recorded `RP_Fight`, the unwatched preferred until it is half a day overdue, never in protective custody; the line names the enforcer only if their gang is known). (3) The warden's
decision letter (`RP_Decision_LeftGang`, urgent, answered in a day, otherwise "nothing"): protective custody, another wing (`GangExit.OtherWing`: a level they could be housed at, not above
their own in danger, with a bed and none of the old gang on it; disabled when there is none), or nothing. Out of reach (custody, a wing without the gang, death row) the daily pass ends the
green light with a line. *The ex-member:* for `GangExit.HatedDays` (30) days the old gang's members whose own gang is on the file hold `RP_ExMember` against them (-40 opinion; a hidden member's grudge would point them
out in the Social tab, so theirs shows in tension and the beating only), the old gang's leader does not recruit them back (`GangUtility.TryRecruit`, as `Mentors` does not), another gang may, and the
green light outlives that, they are left out of turf presence (`GangTurf.Sample` skips them: they are not one of the people counted there), and near the old gang or
on its turf they are tense and afraid as on a rival's (`GangUtility.TensionFromGangs`, `GangTurf.TensionTerm/FearTerm`, never named and never a "rival gang": "trouble on the wing"; the
enforcer's own cause is the same). The file shows an "ex-" chip while it lasts.
*Tattoos.* `GangDef.tattooChance` (Saints 0.4, Ravens 0.6, Brotherhood 0.3) is rolled at intake into `CompInmate.gangTattoo`; the end of a frisk (`JobDriver_Frisk`, which is also the holding search of
a new arrival) reveals the gang of a marked member the warden does not know, with its own chronicle line (`GangUtility.Notify_Frisked`). Not built: a leader who wants out (a leader leaves by
release, challenge or the warden), a contest over a "rival claim" other than two lieutenants, recruits being marked.

**Inmate economy.** `StorySystem.debts` is a ledger of who owes whom (thing ids and names, amount, due tick, reason: drugs on credit, a
lost card game, protection; `Economy/InmateDebts.cs`). A dealer sells to a broke buyer who owes nobody on credit (double the price, due in
three days); three or more inmates at a common-room table, or huddled anywhere else on free time (within five cells in one room), may play cards out of the guards' sight (the best hand takes a stake from each
of the others, and what a loser cannot cover, two or more, becomes a debt); a gang member sells a frightened or vulnerable inmate of the
wing, or a newcomer (admitted in the last four days, in no gang: fear is rare in a quiet prison), protection for a fee every three days (less fear an hour, other extortionists turned away; paid again and again, or it lapses; it is dropped,
and paused meanwhile, when the protector is in the hole, in protective custody, on death row or on another wing). A debt is paid
from the debtor's cash (what they have once it is due, in full early when they can) and escalates when overdue on a daily schedule: a
threat (tension, fear, a bad memory), two days later a beating (the creditor, else a gang mate, in the debtor's room: a recorded `RP_Fight`
with the debt as its cause, rate-limited to one in three days per debt, never while the debtor is in protective custody, written off after
the third), then the debtor's own answer (they ask for protective custody, arrange a smuggled delivery that pays it, or turn informant).
`RP_Arc_Debt` asks the warden at the beating: move the debtor (a free bed in another wing, else another cell), pay it off from their own
cash, or let it run. A debt can become a goal (`RP_Want_Debt`, kept secret until the debt is known) and, once they are beaten for it, a
grudge (the first beating is a setback and the goal is retried; a goal given up after the allowed setbacks turns into the grudge). Debts are judged daily and
only the beating is tried hourly; a party leaving drops the debt (`Story.Notify_Leaving`), and so does a debt nobody can collect: the creditor's side cannot
get at the debtor (protective custody, death row, another wing) for three days, or it has been overdue for three weeks (the creditor writes it off, both are free
to owe and lend again). A delivery smuggled in to pay a debt counts at its market value (part-paid if it falls short; never for a protection fee). Hidden facts
stay hidden: the warden learns of a debt only by a snitch's tip (ranked after tunnels, contraband, gangs and rings), a search turning up the notes, a beating, or
the debtor asking for custody; until then no chronicle line, file chip, intel line, goal, tension cause ("owing money") or mood and opinion thought shows it
(the memories read as generic, "a bad feeling", "a good night", "at ease", until the debt is known; `Economy/EconomyThoughts.cs`). A known protection debt
reveals its creditor's gang, since only a gang sells protection. A fee's renewal is not another debt in the statistics.


**Suppression made legible.** An inmate's `suppressed` (0-100: the hole adds 2 an hour served, a cell served for want of a solitary bed 1, a
confinement sanction 0.5, the warden's firm word 20; it wears off 5 a day) costs them programs, joining a riot and (halved relief) recovery above 50
and cuts their misconduct; the desk says so. The overview's wing table has an "under the thumb" column (the inmates above 50 per level, the mean in its
tooltip) and the weekly report a line naming the wings; the Programs screen's status line says suppression is why a class has nobody (the inmates it
would take but for it); a policy row's sanction tooltip gives the rate an hour, what its hours put on a clean file and the days that file takes to clear; the file's bar
reads "suppressed 72 - clear in 5 days" above the line (the policy tooltip counts from the end of the sentence: the hours served wear off part of it). **The lid comes off:** at midnight (after the new day's budget of letters opens) a wing (security
level, three inmates or more) with a quarter of its inmates (two at least) about to fall through 50 within the day (held, and no more than 55: the inmates, not the mean) and
mean tension over 60 gets a budgeted, not urgent decision letter that names the day the spike begins by the calendar (the budget may hold the letter back), and from a day after the letter, for a day, a riot weighs x2 in the misconduct picker and a rioter's
pull to join one is x2 for the wing's inmates who are under the line (`Suppression.RiotFactor`); a wing is written to at most once in 5 days. The one saved
thing is `PrisonGame.lidTicks`, a wing's last letter tick: the gap and the date the spike runs from (the wing numbers are counted from the inmates, never kept).

### The morning after (`Security/RiotReview.cs`, `Defs/RiotReview.xml`, `Keyed/RiotReview.xml`; gap analysis 7, item 1)
A riot used to end with nothing but a chronicle line. Now it ends in a reckoning. All of it is event-driven: no pass scans the prison for it.
- **The record** (`RiotRecord`, in `PrisonGame.riots`, a `RiotBook` of the last 10; an old save gets an empty book) opens in `MisconductWorker_Riot.TryExecute` when the riot starts (`RiotReview.Open`). It holds the ringleader and the first rioters, each marked as seen or not by the one seen rule (`TensionUtility.Watched`: a guard, a manned tower or a working camera), at most 30 people. It also holds the grievance the ringleader shouts (`RiotNegotiation.Grievance`, now worked out for every riot) and what the prison lacked at the start: the nearest guard to the ringleader, whether the spot was dark, the hour's guards on the roster strip against the inmates on the wing, and yesterday's regime strip for the wing (missed meals, hours locked in), and a copy of the rumour the wing was telling (`RiotRecord.rumour`, see the rumour mill). A grievance counts as real when the inmates' average of that need is under 50%, or, for food, when the wing missed meals yesterday.
- **During the riot**, each rioter's own state (`MentalState_Rioting`, every 250 of its ticks, `RiotReview.Notify_Rioting`) keeps the record's clock and adds the rioter, seen if anything watches them now. Anyone pulled in by the spread goes into the record of the rioter who pulled them. A pawn in no record, such as an assault's hostage-taker who riots in the held room, joins no record. A Harmony prefix and postfix on `Thing.TakeDamage` (`Patch_Thing_TakeDamage_RiotBill`) counts the blows a rioter deals or takes. A thing a rioter breaks adds its value to the bill (the hit points lost against all it had, times its market value). A person a rioter hurts counts as staff or an inmate hurt, and a rioter hurt by anyone counts as an inmate hurt. A member of staff or an inmate a blow kills is listed as dead, by name, unless they were a rioter nobody saw: those are a count. A blow between a rioter and staff is a sighting either way, so a rioter a guard puts down between two of the rioter's own samples is seen.
- **The end**: the record closes when no participant has been rioting for an hour. The closing reads the participants' own ticks, which work like `RiotPolice.RiotActive`'s flag but per record, so two riots on one map close separately. The hour pass at 06:00 then sends **one budgeted letter per riot** (`RP_Decision_RiotReview`, 24 hours to answer): two riots in a night means two letters, and the second waits if the day's budget says so. The letter is the incident report. It names the ringleader and the others **only where they were seen**; the unseen are a count ("and about 2 others nobody saw"). It also gives the hurt and the dead, the damage bill, the grievance and whether it holds up, what the wing was saying when it began (true or false as known then), and what the prison lacked.
- **The answers** (`RiotReview.Apply`): up to two, each given once. Amnesty must be the only answer. An answer that cannot be given says why on its button. After the first answer, an unbudgeted second step (`RP_Decision_RiotReviewMore`, urgent) offers the rest and "that is all". A letter left unanswered means no reckoning. An answer that can no longer be given when it is chosen (things changed since the letter was drawn) uses up nothing: the same step comes back with the reason, asking for "your answer" rather than a second one. "Still here" below means an active inmate on the same stay: admitted by the riot's last rioting (`RiotReview.SeenHere`; a joiner may have arrived after it began), the ringleader by its start (`RingleaderHere`). One released and back since is on another stay.
  - *Restrict the wing* for 1, 2 or 3 days: the yard is called off and lockup comes early each day (`RegimeOrders.Restrict`: a `DayOrders.untilDay` that `Sweep` carries over at midnight, -1 by default). Each day counts as a day ordered in, so the existing habit finding of the inspector counts it. While it runs, the wing's riot odds are halved (`RiotReview.WingFactor` in `Suppression.RiotFactor`). Its suppression comes after the fact, not up front: 15 for each day served, credited at midnight by `Sweep`, and pro rata for part of a day (a restriction given at 23:00, or one lifted early, `RegimeOrders.Credit` from `DayOrders.creditFromTick`). So answering late, or restricting and lifting at once, buys almost nothing. Once a wing is held down past the line (50), it is shut out of programs, and the letter says so. A restriction is the yard order and the early lockup together, so lifting either ends it: it is billed for the days it ran, and what is left is today's order only. A second shower given beside it is never a restriction on its own (`DayOrders.Confining`). Its cost, 5 tension a day on each inmate of the wing, lands when it ends, as the lid's does (`Notify_RestrictionEnded`, from the sweep, or at once when the warden lifts it, for the days it ran).
  - *Ship the ringleader out*: `InmateUtility.TransferOut` (its cost, reputation, the payment lost, and the gang's succession). Only for a seen ringleader who is still here.
  - *Charge the seen not yet sanctioned*: each seen participant still here who was not dealt with for this riot goes on the adjudication book as a case (`Adjudication.Queue` with the officer's evidence, dated to their riot record, or to the riot's end when there is none). **What "charge what was seen" means when the policy already sanctioned the riot:** a riot is loud (`RP_Riot` and `RP_RiotJoined` are `loud`). Every rioter's own misconduct record is therefore seen, and the sanction policy, or the adjudication sheet when the warden adjudicates the serious, deals with them as the riot starts. Charge skips anyone already dealt with in the riot's window, from its start to the last tick anyone was seen rioting (`RiotReview.DealtWith`). That is anyone with a case for a riot offence on the book, waiting or answered, or with a seen riot record that was punished or that the policy gives something for. When nobody is left, the button says why ("everyone seen was sanctioned or put on the adjudication sheet for this riot already"). So nobody is charged twice for one riot. Charge matters when the policy gives nothing for a riot, or for a seen participant whose riot left no record. Amnesty does not undo the sanctions given at the start, and its line in the letter says so. The other option, making the riot's own records follow the seen rule (an unseen rioter not recorded or sanctioned at riot time), was not taken. It would change the misconduct record, the sanctions, the chronicle, the riot-start and negotiation letters (which name the ringleader to negotiate with and point at every rioter) and the existing riot checks. That is not a small change, and it is not safe without running the game.
  - *Make restitution*: the bill is split between the seen participants. Each pays their share out of their cash as far as it goes (never more than they have, as debts are paid) and gains 4 tension, and the ledger takes the money as `RP_Restitution`.
  - *Meet the grievance*: a promise (`DemandSystem.Accept`), as a negotiation makes one. It is made to the ringleader only when the ringleader was seen; otherwise the promise has no spokesperson, so the chronicle names nobody when it is kept or broken. An open promise is swept aside by it, as by a negotiation, and the letter says so when one is open.
  - *Amnesty*: 10 tension off each inmate of the wing, 2 reputation lost, and the wing's riot odds rise by half for 5 days.
- **A negotiated riot** (`RiotNegotiation.Negotiate`, or a hostage crisis that a riot started, conceded) marks its record. The report says so, and "meet the grievance" shows as done already.
- **Intel** lists the riots, newest first: when, the wing, who (only a seen ringleader is named), and where each stands (still going, report due, waiting, no reckoning, or the answers given). The report is in the tooltip, and a button reopens a letter that is still open.
- **The seen rule is about evidence, not knowledge.** The riot-start chronicle line and the negotiation letter name the ringleader and point at every rioter, because a riot is loud and the warden has to know who to negotiate with. These came before this feature and stay. The report, the Intel list and the answers name only who staff or a camera saw, because those are the people the prison can act on: charge, bill or ship out.
- Kept: the last 10 records. A record whose restriction is not billed yet is not trimmed while another can go, so the tension still lands.
- Saved: the record's fields with defaults (`deadIds` is new in round 2), `DayOrders.untilDay` and `DayOrders.creditFromTick`. All are in the saveload snapshot (`riots=`, and the regime's orders).

### Security
Guards (`RP_Security` work type): patrol zones, escort intake, shakedown cells, frisk inmates leaving workshop/
kitchen/visitation, respond to alerts, subdue (arrest/melee), take to solitary. Armed guards later. Metal detectors
frisk automatically; dogs sniff drugs; CCTV raises suppression. Lockdown button: all inmates to cells.

### Seen, and the dark (`Security/Tension.cs`, `UI/Overlays.cs`, `Security/Danger.cs`; gap analysis 5, 2.1 and R11)
**One seen rule.** `TensionUtility.Watched(pawn, pm)` / `SeenAt(cell, map, pm)` is the only reading of "seen" and "watched": a guard in line of sight within 10 cells, a manned tower, or a working camera, however little (`WatchersAt(..) > 0`).
The sanction, the danger map's weapon, the psych count, the complaint, the court, the gang war's pick (through `MisconductUtility.Record`, `DangerMap.Note`, `GangWar`; the court's and the complaints' camera over a spot and staff witness read the dark too, `CourtUtil.CameraOver` / `InSight`) and suppression, contraband, stash, brewing, garden, debts, smuggling and the gang exit (through `Watched`)
all call it; `Watchers` is the same count as a number (fear uses the size as seen; suppression the size as if lit), `GuardsNear` the guards alone (the visit, the exercise plate, the contraband pickup). **The two readings were not the same before:** the record counted any watcher (`Watchers > 0`: one camera is enough) and `Watched`
wanted a whole one (`>= 1`: a guard, a tower or two cameras). Following "what a guard or a working camera sees is seen", the stricter reading is gone: **one working camera now makes an inmate watched**, so a lone camera now stops the contraband pickup, the stash, the garden and the debt collection it did not stop before
(and so does the ring's courier, the gang exit, the mental-state pick). A saved game has no state in it for this.
**What one powered camera over an inmate now stops that it did not** (a guard in sight or a manned tower always did; by day or lit, a camera does not see in the dark): the contraband pickup (`Contraband.cs`), the stash tuck-away and the cellphone call (`Stash.cs`), the kitchen fruit and the mash (`Brewing.cs`),
the garden (sowing, and finding the patch: `Garden.cs`), the debt collection beating (`InmateDebts.cs`) and the card game (`InmateDebts.Unwatched`), the gang-exit enforcer (`GangExit.cs`), the ring's courier and dealer handover (`SmugglingRing.cs`), the dealer's deal and the extortionist's extortion (`ReputationBehaviors.cs`),
riot spread (`MentalStates.cs`) and a tunnel being noticed (`Intel.SomeoneToNotice`; that is, it is noticed when a camera sees the cell). It also makes the misconduct record, the danger map's weapon, the psych count and the court's reading *seen* (`MisconductUtility.Record`, `DangerMap.Note`, `GangWar`). **Unchanged:** suppression and fear use the count as a number (a camera is half a watcher), as before.
**The dark.** A cell where `map.glowGrid.GroundGlowAt(cell)` is under `TensionUtility.DarkGlow` (0.3, vanilla's `PsychGlow.Lit` line) **and** that `DarkMatters` (in a painted zone that is not staff-only, or unzoned indoor ground: the free-time zones, the corridors, a wing's corridor; not the room of a cell, not a staff room,
not open ground outside) is dark: a guard on foot sees it only from within `DarkSight` (3) cells and a camera not at all; a manned tower has a searchlight and sees its whole radius (`TowerSees`). Lit cells are as before. Daylight outdoors is bright, so this is nights, a blackout (the lamps go off with the power) and unlit indoor corners.
A door blocks light, so vanilla gives its cell no glow, by day too: **a cell with a door always counts as lit** (it does not read its neighbours' glow), so an inmate in a doorway is seen as one in the room beside it.
**The dark never changes the odds.** Suppression (`HourlyUpdate`: `watchers * 0.25`, which `MisconductChancePerHour` reads) is taken from the **lit** count, the watchers as if the cell were lit (`WatchersAt(.., ignoreDark: true)`, asked only for a dark cell), so a dark corridor
costs the guards their sight but not the suppression. The blackout's own effect stays (the misconduct chance doubles: `HourlyRoll`) and does not stack with a second one from the dark; a blackout's cameras were off already (a camera with no power is not `Working`), so what the dark rule adds to a blackout is the guards' shorter sight and nothing else.
Fear (`FearSystem.Hourly`, safe when `Watchers >= 1`) still reads the count as it is seen, dark or not.
**Overlays.** The coverage overlay counts each cell as the rule does and shades a dark one blue (`CoverageOverlay.DarkAt`; rebuilt as it was: when a guard has moved three cells or a camera or tower went on or off after at least 250 ticks (looked at every 60), else every 1000 ticks, so the glow can be up to 1000 ticks (about 17 seconds at normal speed, 24 game minutes) behind dusk; a camera going off shows within a check); the danger map's hottest patches carry `unlit` when more than half of the walkable cells of the patch are dark now (`DangerMap.Unlit`;
the Deployment tab says "- unlit" after the place and the tooltip says what it means; the flag is kept per patch for `DangerMap.UnlitKeepTicks` (250) ticks, not worked out on every draw). The coverage overlay shades **the zones only** (every zone inmates are held in or sent to, not the staff-only ones): a corridor is not drawn, so a dark corridor is not shown blue, though it counts for the rule. **The builder** (`PrisonBuilder.LightTheFreeTime`, the last pass of both layouts) puts a standing lamp, on the nearest free cell that sees it, wherever a free-time or corridor cell has no lamp within 8 cells in sight (a lamp's glow is over 0.3
to about ten), so a fight in a lit room is seen at night as by day. Checks: `Testing/DarkChecks.cs` (one rule across the readings, the dark, the tower, the overlay, the danger list, the lamps; glow forced through `TensionUtility.TestGlow`, which a game never sets). Keyed strings: `Languages/English/Keyed/Dark.xml`.

### Guard posts (`Security/GuardPosts.cs`, `Defs/GuardPosts.xml`, `Languages/English/Keyed/GuardPosts.xml`)
The warden puts a guard at a spot, by the hour. `RP_GuardPost` is a spot like vanilla's (1x1, standable, `WorkToBuild 0`, so `Designator_Build` puts it up at once; in the prison menu, no cost). `CompGuardPost` holds what the warden sets, saved:
`fromHour` / `toHour` (0-23, to the hour and not in it, past midnight allowed; the same hour in both, which a new post has, is all day) and `assigned` (the guard named for it, else any). `WorkGiver_ManPost` (Warden, priority 4: **just under the tower (5)**, above the
patrol 1, under every other piece of a guard's work, and under the emergencies; a built tower keeps its guard and the posts take the guards no tower needs, which the post's description and the Deployment text say as "not on a tower") hands an open post (hours cover now, the state's chief not in post: `SpecialMeasures.ChiefInPost` suspends them all) to a guard `GuardPosts.CanTake` says: on duty (the roster,
on site, awake, on the timetable's *work* hours: not a break, not the commute hour, not on strike or walking home) and the one the post wants: the guard named if on duty and `Free` for it (no job, a patrol, a wander, or standing at *this* post: an escort, a search, a rescue,
pad duty or a tower is work, so a post is filled by another; a guard on another post is not free for this one, since they never leave theirs: counting them as "someone else will go" starved posts), else the nearest free guard (an incumbent keeps the post).
A guard named for another open, unmanned post they can reach is not sent to a post that does not name them (and is not counted as the nearer guard for it), so the post that names them is the one they man. A post the guard is forbidden from (an allowed area that excludes it) is no one's to take for them.
`ShouldSkip` skips the scan when the map has no posts. `JobDriver_ManPost` (`RP_ManPost`) walks there and stands watch; it reserves the post, ends when the post is gone (the guard is released),
when its hours or the guard's shift end, or the chief arrives (checked twice a second). **What outranks it**: the job asks the think tree every `GuardPosts.OverrideCheckTicks` (vanilla's own `expiryInterval` + `checkOverrideOnExpire`), so an
emergency (restrain, armed response, rescue), a search, an escort, a meal or the pad takes the guard off the post, and the same check hands the post on when the named guard comes free. A patrol also ends at its two-second look when a post wants the
guard (`GuardPosts.PostFor`, the same test). A guard standing on the post is a watcher by the rules a guard is (`PrisonMap.Guards`, `TensionUtility.GuardsNear`): nothing in the tension code knows about posts. Deployment: a "Guard posts" block in the left
column (place and jump, two hour menus, the guard menu, and who stands there or why nobody is sent) and a "post a guard here" button on each danger patch (`GuardPosts.Place`: within `NearRadius` of the patch's cell, the cell itself or the nearest standable one with nothing built on it, in the patch's room first; the same radius is the "posted" test, so a patch shows posted once its post is up).
A named guard who is dead or let go is written as no reference on save and shown as "name (let go)" until then; any free guard goes.
Checks: `Testing/GuardPostChecks.cs` (real ticks on throwaway guards, the post put up as far from the real guards as the map allows); the post's hours and guard are in the saveload plan's snapshot.

### A protected care detail (`Security/CareDetail.cs`, `Keyed/CareDetail.xml`; gap analysis 6, item 8)
Care and trouble compete for the same guards. On a riot night every guard was on the one who was down or berserk, the confined went unfed and the injured lay (the documented limit, see the showcase's hunger in *Needs*); the reserve makes that a staffing choice.
**Policy** (Deployment, "Care detail"; saved on the map: `PrisonMap.care`, one `Scribe_Deep` with a default of null that loads as an empty one): `size` 0-3 guards (default 0, which changes nothing: no member, no queue, every hook returns at its first test) and `pins`, the named guards' thing ids in the order named (never more than the size; lowering the size drops the last names).
**Who**: each hour (the supermax slot, `SupermaxSystem.SlotOf`), and when the policy, the roster (`Roster.Version`, `StaffCommute.StaffVersion`) or a member's standing changes, or a place is empty, at the map's 250-tick beat (`CareDetail.Tick`, after `RefreshGuards`), the reserve is picked from the guards who can serve (`CareDetail.Why` is null: on the roster and not the warden, not suspended (`CorruptionSystem`), not on leave (`StaffCareers.Available`),
not held hostage, **not on strike** (no silent exemption: a striker takes no work but the fire and the injured, and is no reserve), on site (not at home) and on the timetable's work, on their feet). Named guards first; then those not on a tower or a post: on the hour those already on care work first (a care job in hand: `IsCareJob`), then the ones already in it, the idle, the rest, by id;
between hours the members stay and only a vacancy is filled. **A floor**: the automatic pick never takes the last guard on duty who could answer the trouble (on duty, not drafted or in a mental state: `OnDuty`), so at least one guard stays outside the reserve whatever its size (the tab says a place was left empty for it: `Floored`); a named guard is the warden's own choice and may be the last (the tab's red line says so).
Named guards are held to the filters the pick applies to the others (`CareDetail.Unfit`: `Why`, and on a tower or post, drafted, in a mental state, or with no care work to do): one who is not fit is listed unavailable with the reason and the place is filled from the others, and is back in at the next beat once fit (a post holds a guard for up to an hour; chosen over ending the post job when they become a member, so that a naming never pulls a guard off a post the warden set). A named guard who cannot serve keeps the name and is listed "not available - Name (suspended)", and the automatic pick fills the place until they can; one who is dead, let go or no longer a guard loses it. (The description says pins are dropped when they cannot serve, off shift included; a day guard's name would then be gone every evening,
so only *gone* guards lose it, and the rest is reported.) `CareDetail.IsReserve(p)` is the set's membership **and** `Why(p) == null` read at the moment of asking, so a shift ending, a suspension or a strike takes a guard out of the reserve at once, not at the next beat (no phantom reserve).
**What the reserve does**: a reserve guard takes no restraint, armed response or alarm (`GuardUtility.CanRespondTo`: `CareDetail.Holds`, so `WorkGiver_RespondToTrouble`, `AlarmInmate` (the mail trip's end condition) and `Kin.Hesitates`, which no longer counts them as someone who could go), no punishment carry, no search (`WorkGiver_Frisk`, `WorkGiver_SearchVisitor`, `WorkGiver_SearchCell`: their `ShouldSkip`), no tower and no post (`CanTake`), unless an
inmate **acting out** (`GuardUtility.ActingOut`) is within `NearTroubleRadius` (8 cells, in a straight line) of them or of the inmate they are tending (`PatientOf`: the target of the care job in hand); a calm inmate who owes a restraint or a carry is held however near (it is no danger, and a cell block is full of them). **Never held, wherever it is**: an escape (`MentalState_Escaping`: an emptied prison is not a staffing trade-off) and trouble aimed at staff
(`CareDetail.AimedAtStaff`: an assault on a member of staff, a hostage-taker, an inmate whose attack job is on a member of staff). The warden's own order (`forced`) always works. They are given a care job **first**: `Patch_JobGiver_Work_CareFirst`, a prefix on the non-emergency work node, asks the queue and the existing givers (rescue to a bed, the cell meal, `WorkGiver_FeedInjuredInmate` then vanilla's `FeedPrisoner`, the walk back of a supermax inmate left outside: `CareDetail.CareJobFor`, by kind then nearest) and
gives their own jobs; the rescue is an emergency giver and is theirs anyway; a patrol ends at its two-second look for care that waits for them (`JobDriver_Patrol`, as for a post). It makes no one better at medicine: the doctors still do that (the givers' own work types and capacities apply: a guard with the warden's work off is offered nothing). A care job already begun is not cut short by trouble: `Patches/CareJobs.xml`'s `casualInterruptible false` is the protection, and the cook-delivery cap (`CookMayDeliver`) is untouched.
**Release** (a button; "Call the reserve back" while it holds): empties the reserve for the rest of the hour (`releasedSlot`; the next hour's pick fills it again; not saved, so a load brings the reserve back) and says in a message which care now waits for whoever is free, by kind.
**The queue** is one per map, not a search per guard and inmate: one pass over the inmates every 250 ticks (and when asked for and older), each inmate by the guard-independent tests the givers themselves start with (`WorkGiver_RescueInmate.CareCandidate`, `WorkGiver_DeliverCellMeal.MealCandidate`, `WorkGiver_FeedInjuredInmate.Candidate`, `SupermaxSystem.ReturnDue`: the givers' `JobOnThing` / `HasJobOnThing` were cut at those points, nothing else changed), no path search anywhere;
the queue also leaves out the meals and feedings no one could bring (an inmate in an aggressive mental state, a cell that is not secure, a forbidden one: the givers' `ShouldTakeCareOfPrisoner` gates), so the waits it reports are waits staff could have ended; "served" is per kind (a meal in hand serves the meal request, not the same inmate's feeding or carry).
The dispatch asks the giver, which decides with its own tests, reservation and way there, about the few requests of the queue: not those someone is on already or the guard cannot reach (the cached reachability test), at most `MaxTries` (4) in one look, and once `MaxKindFailures` (3) of a kind of food errand have found nothing for this guard the rest of the kind waits (`RetryTicks`); each failure is remembered per guard and request for 120 ticks. A request keeps the tick it was first seen (`CareRequest.firstTick`) while it stays, and is "served" while a member of staff has a care job for that inmate; the tab shows the oldest unserved with its wait. **Not saved**: the dispatch does not read ages, so the waits are rebuilt after a load and count from there (the tab says so in its tooltip).
Within the 250-tick beat the response givers read a hash set (`members`) and the tests above; `PrisonMap.Of` is cached.
**UI**: a "Care detail" block in the Deployment left column after the posts: the size with - and +, who is on it (named ones marked), who is named and not available and why, a muted note when a place was left empty for the floor (or a warning when there are not the guards for it), a red warning when every guard on duty is in the reserve (only the warden's naming does that), the oldest waiting care, a "Name a guard" menu (one place each) and the release button. **Not done**: a minimum-service agreement for a strike (the description's "needs an explicit agreement": the decision here is that a striking guard is no reserve), a hire of care staff, doctors in the reserve, nobody's care guaranteed (no staff, no food or no safe route: the queue says what waits).
Checks (`Testing/CareDetailChecks.cs`, `EvCareDetail` after `EvHireFit`, each says in its summary how it can fail): a walled supermax unit (a hungry inmate in a cell, a downed one in the corridor, two guards, two inmates owing a restraint sixteen cells away; the unit's regime is the lockup all day so that no yard walk takes the inmate out of the cell at the hour the run is at) run on real ticks, the pick limited to the check's own guards (`CareDetail.OnlyThese`; the real staff are neither picked nor struck, a strike reached the live prison) - reserve 1: the reserve guard never restrains, the other does, and care completes beside it;
reserve 0: the control, both guards are drawn to the restraints and no care starts first; a reserve of 0 offers everything the same as with the hooks switched off (`CareDetail.Disabled`), the real work nodes' answers (emergency and normal) included, the refactored givers still give the meal and the carry to the same inmates, and naming the guard changes the table; the radius (far and near, calm and acting out, an escape, an assault on staff, near their patient, forced, the searches); the floor (three on duty fill two places of three, one on duty none, a named last guard is in); named guards who are drafted, in a mental state, on a post or with no care work are listed and replaced, and back once fit;
**the care first**, through the real `JobGiver_Work.TryIssueJobPackage`: the shut-in inmate is due a yard walk (the regime is the yard alone), which the scan ranks above a cell meal (escort 145, meal 70), so the scan alone (`Patch_JobGiver_Work_CareFirst.Off`) gives the reserve guard the walk, and with the care first on they get care, the guard outside the reserve gets the same either way and the emergency node is left alone; a reserve guard put on a patrol is called off it within 240 ticks for the care that waits; suspended, struck, off shift, at home and gone guards are reported and never assigned; the pick prefers care work, the release, one queue for forty questions.
The saveload plan saves with the reserve guard carrying a meal across the corridor (`SeedCareDetailForReload`, last of the seeds) and the reserve off its default (size 2, two names, `care=` in the snapshot): the guard is given the meal by the care first and started on it by hand (the carry begins at once; **no game tick is run for the seed**, the first version ticked up to 1500 and moved the parole hearings, the supermax walks, the shuttle and the danger marks that the seeds before it had recorded; the inmate is confined by a lockup, so the supermax seed's all-day yard regime does not take them out of the cell; a premise the live prison does not give is told in the log, not failed). After the load, with no tick, the job and the carry are there; after the supermax check (which ticks thousands) one meal has been eaten and nothing is left in hand or on the floor (`FinishCareDetailAfterReload`). The showcase plan keeps a reserve of one and logs the longest care wait of the run (and of it, while an inmate was acting out or owed a restraint): told, not failed.

### Evacuating a wing and accounting for its people (`Security/Evacuation.cs`, `Security/EvacuationWork.cs`, `UI/EvacuationUI.cs`, `Defs/Evacuation.xml`, `Keyed/Evacuation.xml`; gap analysis 6, Astra 8)
A fire that reaches a wing is no longer only a door that opens (`PrisonMap.FireAt`, which still opens a burning cell's door for everyone and is not what an evacuation uses): the warden can empty the wing to a place that is safe, watch the roll call, and send everyone back.
