**Saved state** (per map, `PrisonMap.evac`, a `Scribe_Deep` that loads as none when absent): up to two **assembly points** (`AssemblyPoint`: the zone and the patch's first cell, as kept patches are named, and the levels it takes; a repaint that moves the first cell is followed through the cell, one that removes the patch leaves the point "gone") and the **evacuation** that runs, if any (`EvacuationState`: the wing zone, the phase Out/Back, drill or not, the start and last-fire ticks, the per-wing time of the last fire letter, and one `EvacEntry` per inmate: id and name, status, point, second/fallback/escort flags, destination cell, the escorting guard's id, arrival tick, why they are missing). A point dropped on load (its zone gone) moves the later points' indices in the lines with it. Routes, door passes, the pawns and the planning queue are never saved: after a load the first tick plans every inmate again and the guards' own jobs (which are saved) make their walks again.
**The points**: a yard, a kept room (a patch of any shared zone inside the walls) or the holding patch (`EvacuationSystem.MarkableZone`), marked from the Deployment tab; never visitation (the room the public comes into, with its door to the outside and visitors at the tables; a point saved on it is dropped on load like one whose zone is gone); the default levels are every level but supermax, and a yard that names a level (the supermax cage) takes that level only. A level no point takes goes to the survival fallback. **Supermax** (any level that is escort-only) is only ever sent to a yard of its own (`AssemblyPoint.TakesAlone`: a general yard the warden ticked supermax on does not count), one inmate per patch and nobody else in it with them (`BarredPatches`: one who is never with anyone else is not sent to a patch anyone on the roll call has been given, and nobody else to a patch one of them has been given - a cage the warden ticked other levels on is theirs alone; they are planned first, so the cage is theirs before anyone else's), and never to the general fallback; with no cage free they wait in the cell, listed "route blocked". The second point is where one of each pair that must not stand together goes (a keep-apart pair or a feud: in order of id, the one whose partner or enemy is already at the first point - a feud is two-sided, so both would otherwise go), and protective custody and the old gang's green light, when a point takes their level; with one point only they stand together and the roll call and the drill letter say so (`SeparationFailures`).
**The wing** (`EvacuationSystem.WingAt`) is the wing zone, with the solitary cells beside or inside it: a cell of `RP_SolitaryZone` (a zone of no level) belongs to the wing painted within two cells of its room (`HoleWing`, remembered for the tick). So the hole is evacuated with its wing (by escort, its sentence untouched) and a fire in the hole is a fire in the wing.
**Starting**: never by itself. `EvacuationSystem.Beat` (the map's 250-tick beat) looks for a fire in a wing with people in it and sends a decision letter (`RP_Decision_WingFire`, `WingFireDecision`: evacuate, or leave it to the staff; the evacuation is disabled with the reason when no point takes the level of anyone standing in it - the people's levels, not the wing's - or one runs already) at most every four hours a wing (`wingLetterTicks`), also while an evacuation of another wing runs; the Deployment tab has "Evacuate the <wing>" for each wing with a fire, and "Drill" for any wing. One evacuation per map at a time, but a real fire takes over from a drill (`Start` resets the drill and says so; the tab keeps the burning wings' buttons while a drill runs). A drill is refused during a lockdown of the level or a riot.
**Who moves how** (`EvacuationSystem.Start`): the people standing in the wing's cells are moved, those whose bed is in the wing but who are elsewhere (work, class, yard) are on the roll as "outside" and stay where they are (accounted for). Minimum, medium and protective inmates **walk**: planning gives them a point, a cell of it that nobody else has, a route and a pass (`CompInmate.evacDoors`, a set made on the main thread and never changed, read in `Patch_Building_Door_PawnCanOpen` next to the escort leg's; the door verdict itself is untouched) for the doors on that route and no other, and their job is a `Goto` from the new think tree `RP_InmateEvacuation` (over the regime's own tree, so that not even a patient's bed pulls them back). Maximum, supermax, the hole, death row, the downed, and a walker who has no way they may walk alone (another level's wing or a staff corridor between them and every safe place: `TurnToEscort`) **wait where they are** (a `Wait` job, or a few steps away within the room from a fire that comes beside them, `AwayFromFire`; the supermax guard's fetch is off for them, `SupermaxSystem.DueFor`; the compliance reading leaves them be, `RegimePlacement`) for a guard: `WorkGiver_EvacuateInmate` (Warden, emergency, priority 192: under the riot response 200 and the carry to solitary 195, over the rescue 190 and every routine care job; the care reserve's guards take it too) gives `RP_EvacuateInmate`, the supermax escort's walk (`EscortWalk`, new `EscortKind.Evacuation`: the guard holds the inmate, the leg's pass opens the route's doors and ends the moment the guard stops holding them; one guard, one inmate); a downed inmate is carried (`RP_CarryEvacuee`, a carry job of count 1, by a guard or, from the doctors' version of the giver, a doctor, and put down at the place; the existing rescue leaves a downed evacuee alone until they are at the point, `EvacuationSystem.Keeps`, and then takes them only to a medical bed outside the wing and away from fire (or their own bed, which is in the wing and so not while it is emptied: never another bed, which vanilla's carry would make theirs), `BedAllowed` in `WorkGiver_RescueInmate.BedFor`, when they are listed "in a bed outside the wing", `EvacStatus.Rescued` - only while they lie in such a bed, down or resting for their wounds (`InRescueBed`); one in a bed of the wing, or up again, is not rescued and is given their place again. Vanilla's warden carry to a bed and its escort of an inmate who cannot reach their bed by themselves (`WorkGiver_Warden_TakeToBed`), which know only their bed in the wing, make no job for an evacuee while the wing is emptied (`Patch_WorkGiver_Warden_TakeToBed_Evacuees` on `TryMakeJob`; round 2 only took them off the giver's list, `VanillaGiverLists.TakeToBedCandidate`, but the work scan finds a warden giver's pawns region by region round the guard and reads the list only when that finds none, so the downed inmate laid at the point was carried back to their bed in the wing and the supermax inmate in the cage escorted back to their cell, both then listed "rescued"). One already at their place when planned (down at the point, moved on within the patch) is arrived, never carried from their cell to the same cell. A guard lost mid-walk puts the inmate back to waiting for the next; any walk or carry that ends without getting there (at whichever toil, whoever ended it) is not offered again for a few seconds (`Notify_GuardDone`). After a load the walk is made again by the job and the inmate's own wait is ended so that they follow it at once (`EnsureLeg`). Those outside the wing stay out of it: an hour that would send them to their cell in it (lockup, sleep) is spent where they are (`OutsideJob`), and one who walks into it anyway is moved out with the rest.
**The routes** (`EvacRoutes`): one breadth-first walk over the cells (staggered: `PlanPerTick` = 3 inmates a tick), through walkable cells and doors that are not locked or held by rioters, **have no outside or unknown side (a perimeter door is on no route)** and no staff room open to the outside (an outdoor or map-edge staff area) on either side, are not into a staff side (a walker only; a guard's walk may) and are not another inmate's cell (their cell door), never across a cell of another level's wing (a walker only), and never through or beside fire (the cells round a fire are out). The doors on the shortest such route are the pass. A route keeps each cell's step, and is planned again only when it becomes invalid (checked each beat, over the route's cells still ahead of where the inmate has been seen on it, not the cells walked - a fire spreading behind them through the wing they left is no reason to stop them: fire on a cell ahead or beside it, a door still ahead locked or held since, the place on fire or beside it, the point repainted away or burning in its own cells), the inmate's job is cut once and they start on the new one. A route that the doors refuse right after it was planned (`CanReach` fails in the same beat) is "route blocked", not planned again in a loop. No route for an inmate is "route blocked" (tried again every `RetryTicks`, listed; no place is held for them); after two hours unreachable they are listed as missing (`missingOffMap` false) and stay missing while there is no way, but **still in the evacuation's hands** (round 6): kept from the regime (`Involves`, `RegimePlacement`), the supermax walks (`SupermaxSystem.DueFor`) and vanilla's carry to a bed (`BeingEmptied`), waiting where they are (a step away from a fire beside them), and tried again quietly every `RetryTicks` - a way found (the warden unlocks the door, the fire moves) takes them out after all; they hold nothing up (not `InProgress`), and the all-clear sends them home with the rest. One missing because they left the map is listed again when they are back (their pass and walk end when they go, so no door opens for them when they come back). A downed inmate who is blocked or missing for want of a way may be taken by the prison's rescue to a bed outside the wing (`Keeps` leaves them to it: no guard of the evacuation carries one with no way, and the rescue's carry needs no route of its own); the carry to solitary with a punishment owed waits for the all-clear (`WorkGiver_TakeToPunishment.Candidate`: the hole beside the wing is part of it). A carry planned again on the way (the way ahead burns, the place is gone) keeps the inmate in the guard's arms and turns for the new place (`Replan`, `JobDriver_CarryEvacuee` re-paths on a changed target B); only with no place at all is the inmate put down, and then not on a cell in or beside a fire (`SafeDropCell`).
**The roll call** (Deployment tab, `EvacuationUI`): arrived (with those outside the wing and the fallback ones counted; those the rescue put in a bed outside the wing on a line of their own), being carried, waiting for an escort, on the way, route blocked, missing (not on the map, or blocked for two hours), then the names of those not at a place, and the warden's "All clear". One beat does it all (`EvacuationSystem.Refresh`): a dead or vanished inmate is missing, a released one leaves the list, one in an aggressive mental state or escaping leaves it too (the riot response takes them; the walk ends - at once, from a postfix on `MentalStateHandler.TryStartMentalState`, not on the next beat), a downed walker becomes a carry (and walks again by themselves once up, when next planned).
**Survival fallback**: with no assembly point that takes their level and can be reached, the nearest safe room of the prison they may enter (a patch of the yard or a common room, not kept from their level, no fire in it, nearest first, at most four tried; supermax only a yard of its own) is their place, logged (`[RimPrison:evac]`) and listed "fallback"; with none either, route blocked. Nobody is sent into or through fire.
**All-clear**: when no fire has burned in the wing for an hour (`ClearAfterTicks`, judged on the beat) or on the warden's button - **never while the wing burns** (round 6: the button is greyed with the reason, and a drill whose wing has caught waits at the points for the fire's end or the warden's real evacuation) - everyone is planned again to the cell near their own bed (those missing for want of a way among them; a cell that still burns - one outside the wing - waits where they are until it is out, holding the end up to the cap; a destroyed bed: `InmateUtility.TryReassignBed` gives them the wing's free bed, else they stay and are listed "no bed, staying"), the passes and walks end as each gets there, and the evacuation is over when nobody is on the way any more or after three hours (`BackCapTicks`): missing, rescued and staying never hold it, and one with no way back (route blocked) holds it only for two tries at the way (`BackBlockedHoldTicks`) unless their own cell burns, then is left where they are, listed in the message and the chronicle line that say how it went, and the regime's to see in. **A rescued inmate stays in the rescue's bed until well** (the doctors' patient; the all-clear does not move them and they hold nothing up; well again, the regime takes them to their own bed, which is still theirs); one rescued who is up again at the all-clear walks home with the rest. Home is their own cell (`AtGoal`), not any bed. **A fire in the wing after the all-clear** (one that caught again) turns it back out (`BackOut`, on the beat): the way home is called off, everyone on the way back, waiting, blocked, staying or already home is given a place out again (a vanilla carry to a bed in the wing is put down), the last-fire tick is now, and no letter is sent (the evacuation runs; a drill's letter was sent at its all-clear, and a drill whose wing caught on the way back is a real evacuation from then on); a message says so. **Bed owners never change by the evacuation's hand** otherwise (other movers - feud and debt moves, cell requests, reclassification - are not gated, and the hourly move to a bed when they have none still runs): while an inmate is on the roll call (anything but dropped, `HoldsBed`) the hourly move out of a wrong wing (`CompInmate.Hourly`) and the daily review (`Cellmates.ReviewWould`, `ReviewWouldForGrade`) leave them where they are - round 2's runs had the hourly move take a minimum inmate living in the supermax unit to a free minimum bed mid-evacuation, and their way home went there.
**Drill**: a "Drill" button for a chosen wing without a fire (a wing on fire has the real button), once a day (a rolling day from the start of the last, `lastDrillTick`: the calendar's `GenDate.DaysPassed` turns over at the hour the game began, and a 3 am drill whose walk back ran past it let a second one start), not during a lockdown of the level or a riot; the same process, a memory "evacuation drill" (-2 for a day, `RP_EvacuationDrill`) for each inmate it moved (on arrival), ended when everyone is at a place or stuck (at least a quarter of an hour in, at most two hours), then the letter that names the slowest arrival, every route that was blocked, who was not at a place and any kept-apart pair together; then the way home as above.
**What does not change**: door modes (a mode that is Locked stays a wall for an evacuee), wing restrictions, lockdowns (a locked-down inmate's regime is unchanged and the pass only moves them), punishment deadlines (the hole's and supermax's clocks keep counting), bed owners. Escape is the existing escape: the routes reach no perimeter door.
**Performance**: planning is a few inmates a tick and only for those who need it; the beat is one pass over the roll (a hash lookup per inmate, the route's cells ahead against the fire mask - not the mask against the route - and a scan of the fires, none when there are none); the candidates for a guard are one list a tick that only an asking giver builds (`InmateCandidates.Evacuate`); nothing in the door verdict allocates or scans (the pass is a precomputed set).
Checks (`Testing/EvacuationChecks.cs`, `EvEvacuation` at the end of the events list; the unit is the supermax checks' walled one, the cells locked in every hour, the shower and cage doors staff-only, and the evacuation limited to the check's own inmates, `EvacuationSystem.OnlyThese`): the places the marking menu offers (yards, the cage, a shared room, holding; not visitation, a wing or a staff zone); what a route may pass (perimeter side, locked, staff side for a walker, another's cell, fire, a way again when it is out); a fire in the shower beside three cells with a supermax inmate, a downed inmate and a medium inmate - the letter offers and nothing starts, then real ticks until all three are at safe places (the supermax inmate in the cage, led by a guard on the leg's pass, the downed one carried, the walker on a pass for their own cell's door only), no door off a route in any pass, nobody in the shower room or in a fire, no job churn, the all-clear sends all three back, door modes, bed owners and sanctions as before; a locked door (listed blocked, then out when it is unlocked), the first point repainted away (the walker goes to the second), the second too (the fallback room, listed); the letter with no point offers nothing and with one it does, a kept-apart pair split between two points, protective custody at the second, and with one point the pair reported together; the drill (letter naming the slowest arrival as the ticks saw it and the blocked inmate, the memory for the moved and not for the one who stayed, a second drill the same day refused, and allowed a day after the first began, whatever the calendar); with no tick: a route judged by the cells ahead (a fire behind leaves it, one ahead or a door ahead locked since does not), a feud (one of the two at the second point), supermax alone (a general yard ticked for them is not theirs, one in the cage and the other blocked in the cell, none in the general yard with the cage gone), the hole (a solitary cell beside the wing is part of it, its fire a fire in the wing, its inmate escorted), and the downed (neither vanilla's carry to a bed nor the rescue while they wait, the rescue only to a bed outside the wing at the point - never a free bed, which it would claim - vanilla's warden giver itself making no carry for the downed one at the point and no escort for the supermax inmate in the cage, vanilla's again after the all-clear). In the fire run the downed inmate may end at the point or in a medical bed outside the wing (rescued), and "back" for them is their own cell or that bed; the warden's all-clear is refused while the fire burns and taken once it is put out; an arrived evacuee seen lying in a bed of the wing during the Out phase is a failure; with `AutoTest.Active` every change of a roll-call line is logged with where the inmate was and on what job (`[RimPrison:evac]`), and so is an evacuee in the arms of a job that is not the evacuation's carry. The blocked inmate of the blocked-route check goes missing after two hours and stays missing while the door is locked (no "found again" on the next beat) but still the evacuation's (its job, kept from the regime and vanilla's carry, holding nothing up), and gets out to the fallback room once the door is unlocked (round 6; rounds 3-5 expected them missing until the all-clear); the all-clear sends them home without the cap. Round 6's no-tick checks: a fire in the wing after the all-clear (a real one and a drill's: back out, those home listed again, no wing letter, no second drill letter, the all-clear refused while it burns), a cage ticked for medium not shared with supermax (either way round), a downed inmate behind a locked door (blocked, the rescue may take them, never to their bed in the wing), a carry whose point is repainted away (the guard keeps them and turns for the fallback room), the carry to solitary off while the wing is emptied; the guards' walks and carries are counted for churn. The saveload plan saves with an evacuation half done, built by hand with no tick and no regime changed (`SeedEvacuationForReload`: the evacuation started and planned by direct calls, the walker put at the point, a guard beside the supermax inmate started on the walk and one beside the downed inmate on the carry, every field off its default, the drill flag set just before the snapshot and taken off after the comparison, `evac=` in the snapshot) and after the load the guards' jobs are still on and the downed one still carried, the walk is made again (counted), everyone gets to a place, the all-clear sends them back and sanctions and bed owners are as they were; the pawns are found carried or not, and taken down with the unit and its fires whatever the check found. Tour: `RIMPRISON_TOUR=evacuation` (two assembly points marked, then a drill with the roll call: the wing the drill's own rules allow with the most people standing in it, the clock run on up to twelve hours until a wing has its people in it - at the showers' hour nobody is in a wing and no drill can start - then the walkers on the map and the roll call).
**Where the brief and the code differ**: (1) "the existing fire letter offers": the one fire letter (`RP_Decision_KitchenFire`) is a kitchen fire's, which burns in the kitchen zone and never in a wing, so the evacuation has its own letter for a fire that reaches a wing (from any source); the kitchen letter is unchanged. (2) `PrisonMap.cs:733`: the burning cell's door verdict is as it was (open for everyone while the fire is in the cell or the room outside it); the evacuation does not use it and does not undo it. (3) "Escape.cs": nothing changes there; the routes keep clear of perimeter doors by their own rule. (4) Protective custody walks (the decisions name minimum and medium only; protective inmates are not dangerous, and they are the ones sent to the second point). (5) "per-hour passes": the roll call and the fire watch run on the map's 250-tick beat (the existing per-map pass), so the all-clear comes within one beat of the hour; there is no per-tick scan. At the point an evacuee eats, uses a toilet or lies down when an urgent need comes (the inmates' urgent-needs giver, `SafeErrand`) if nothing of it is in the wing or beside a fire, and wanders only on cells away from fire. **One evacuation per map**: a second wing that catches fire while one runs gets its letter, but the evacuation is refused ("one at a time") and the letter times out to fighting the fire; the two are not merged. **Not done**: a button to release more staff from other duties (the work is emergency work, which already outranks routine care); a wing is still a zone def, not a patch (a fire in one block of a wing evacuates every cell of that zone on the map, and the all-clear waits for the last fire in any of them; the letter is now once per wing every four hours, not once per map); no cap on the Out phase (the all-clear comes an hour after the last fire, or from the warden); walkers walk by vanilla's `Goto`, so the route is the pass and the plan, not the exact path (vanilla's own fire costs keep the path off a burning cell); no per-room point (the patch is the place).

### Rooms kept for a level (`Zones/ZoneGrid.cs`, `Zones/KeptRooms.cs`, `Languages/English/Keyed/KeptRooms.xml`)
Prison Architect's sectors: a yard for Maximum, a canteen for Minimum.

**What is kept.** Each connected patch of a zone with `keepable` (yard, canteen, showers, common room, library, chapel, classroom, visitation) has a set of security levels it is kept for; none = all, the default. `ZoneGrid` holds it
per patch index beside the paint (`SetKept`, `KeptFor`, `Allows`, `AnyKept`, `AnyFor`) and saves it as `keptPatches` (zone, first cell, levels; a save from before has none). A repaint hands the levels on at the next walk of the
patches: a new patch takes those of the old patch of the same zone that holds most of its cells (a cut patch keeps them in both parts, two joined keep the bigger part's, one with no old patch under it is open to all).
**A patch is four cells or more: a remnant of one to three cells left by a repaint is in no patch, so it is open to all and loses the restriction** (and the patch it came from, painted whole again, is unkept).

**Who follows it.** Inmates only (`KeptRooms.LevelOf`; staff, visitors and guests are kept out of nothing), and only through the zone choice, **never through a door's verdict** (the door patch runs on path threads and reads
precomputed fields): an inmate of another level is not sent to a patch kept for others, and is out of place for compliance if they stand in one (`InExpectedPlace` gives that verdict *before* the class / visit / hearing
acceptance, `Sanctioned`; a zone with no patch their level may use counts as absent, as the regime's fallback does).
- Free time and the activities' zones: `KeptRooms.PickCell` (behind `RandomReachableZoneCell` and so `WanderInZones`) takes only patches the pawn's level may use, the nearest first and then the others, cells in bits too small
  to be a patch last. Nearest is the distance to the patch's nearest *cell* (an L-shaped patch is not taken over a nearer small one); the bounding box cached with the patches is only a lower bound, so a patch it rules out is not measured
  unless the nearer ones cannot be reached.
- Errands: `FixtureFinder.FindFor` (toilet, phone, shower, weights) takes only fixtures whose *use cell* is in a patch their level may use, the commissary counter must be too (`KeptRooms.AllowsTouch`: a counter's own cell is in no
  patch, so the cells beside it count), and so must the free-time joy target and the chapel pick. The urgent meal goes through the same search: vanilla's `JobGiver_GetFood` runs first and a meal it took in a forbidden patch is
  replaced by the allowed-first canteen search. For meals the cell actually used counts: a postfix on vanilla's `Toils_Ingest.TryFindChairOrSpot` (inmates only, only when a patch is kept) swaps a chair or spot in a forbidden patch of the
  same room for one in an allowed patch (none: vanilla's stays). The escort's shower (the wing's own showers are always theirs; the supermax yard rule is untouched) and the visitation spot read it too.
- **Classes** (`ProgramSystem.TryFindVenue`): a room with seats (or standing cells) in patches kept for some levels admits only inmates allowed in **every** such patch of the room, so no seat is assigned and `AttendJob` takes any
  allowed seat. Given the cohort, the room that admits the most of them is chosen (then the most places), those admitted are the attendees, and a room admitting none is passed over; named by none, a room counts when some inmate living in the
  prison is admitted (cached per map and tick). With nothing kept it is the room with the most places, as before.
- **Visits**: a visit under way whose visitor stands in (or walks to) a visitation patch that is kept since for other levels than the inmate's is **ended** (`VisitationSystem.KeepVisitInBounds`: the visitor leaves, nobody is relocated),
  and `RP_VisitTalk` is not issued into a forbidden patch. A new visit's spot is picked for the inmate's level.

**Exemptions.** Assigned inmate work (cleaning, the library clerk's round, laundry, kitchen, garden: the job is where the work is, kept room or not, and compliance reads it the same way), and the two urgent fallbacks, so nobody wets themselves
or starves for a sign on a door: the urgent toilet (`survival: true`) and the meal (when no patch their level may use has a meal to reach, or a seat for it, the nearest is theirs; a test run logs it). The survival meal is tied to the
*job*, not the pawn: the fallback marks its Ingest job (`KeptRooms.MarkSurvivalMeal`, a set of job `loadID`s cleared when the job ends, not saved: a reload drops the mark and a meal eaten across it is out of place once), and
`InExpectedPlace` reads an inmate whose current job is a marked Ingest as in place, first of all; an ordinary meal afterwards is not.

The Rooms tab lists each patch of those kinds under its row with a "kept for ..." menu (levels with a tick, "all" clears; it opens again after each pick) and a mild note when none of the levels has an inmate in the prison. Checks:
`Testing/KeptRoomsChecks.cs`.

### Class bookings (`Programs/ClassBookings.cs`, `UI/ClassBookingUI.cs`, `UI/ProgramsTab.cs`, `Keyed/ClassBookings.xml`; gap analysis 6, Astra 2)
The Programs tab's switch was run or stop; a scarce classroom and counsellor went to the fullest class (`ProgramSystem.ComparePriority`). A booking reserves hours of the day for one program so the warden, not the head count, picks who gets them. `ClassBooking` is a program, an hour mask (bit h: h:00, every day), an optional anchor (a cell of the room, resolved to a room each hour; a room that is not one any more is a miss, never an error), a priority, a miss streak, the game hour it was last taken up in (`handledStamp`, so an hour is handled once however the pass is repeated or the game reloaded) and the last five misses (hour, tick, `ClassMiss`). `ClassBookings` (`PrisonMap.bookings`, saved with `Scribe_Deep`, none in an old save) enforces one booking per program per hour (`Add` keeps only the free hours, `Toggle` refuses a held one, freeing the last hour removes the booking). Bookings are per map, with `PrisonMap.sessions`; `ProgramPolicy` (`disabled`, the waits) is the game's, one for all maps, and holds none of it.
- **The pass** (`ProgramSystem.HourlyTick`): a program with a booking for this hour gets a `Candidate` even with nobody in it, and `ComparePriority` puts booked candidates before everything (higher priority, then the older booking), then referred, then waited for by a job, then fullest, as before. `TryStart` takes the booked room (`FindVenueNow`/`FindVenue` try only the room the anchor is in) and says why it could not (`out ClassMiss`: nobody to teach, a room, a teacher, enough students, the funds, in that order, the order the code tries them in). A miss is `NoteMissed`, a start `NoteRan`; nothing is reserved when it misses, so the automatic scheduler fills the free room and teacher in the same pass as it would have. The session, its charge, its attendance and its credit are the existing ones, started and settled exactly as for an automatic class: a booking never charges, credits or refunds. What it cannot override it does not touch: attendees are still only the inmates whose regime has the program's slot now (no meal, no lockup, no confinement), the canteen is not used while the regime has inmates in it, a room kept for other levels admits only the inmates allowed in every patch of it.
- **Reasons** (`ClassMiss`): no teacher on duty (on leave, suspended, down or the teaching work off: `CanTeach`), teacher teaching another class, no room of the zone with seats, the booked room gone (rebuilt, repainted, its seats gone), the room not open to the inmates who could attend (kept for other levels), the room holding another class, the regime using the zone, no eligible inmate with the slot (a meal, lockup or other activity for them), no eligible inmate free, only one student, no funds. Three misses in a row (`WarnAfter`) put a warning on the row; nothing is ever cancelled for them; the history keeps the last five (`MaxMisses`).
- **The screen**: a Book button on each Programs row opens `Dialog_ClassBooking`: one row of 24 hours per booking (click to book or free; green: eligible inmates have the program's slot then; red: none, what they do instead is in the tooltip; an orange bar: another booked class that wants the same room or teacher, the higher priority first), the room (a float menu of the zone's rooms with seats; its note says how many of the eligible inmates it admits), the priority and the misses. `ClassBookings.Analyse` and the room notes are worked out when the booking is edited and when the game hour turns, never per frame; the Programs rows are cached as before and rebuilt when `ClassBookings.version` moves. The row shows the hours (and room) booked, the latest miss ("missed at 14:00 - no counsellor on duty") and the warning. Rescheduling is editing the hours.
- Not done: the Regime tab shows nothing of bookings (they are per program, the regime per level; the hour's clash with a meal or the work slot is the window's per-hour reading of the eligible inmates' own levels); a day mask (not asked for). A stopped program's booking is skipped while the program is stopped (the warden's own decision): no miss, no streak (`ClassMiss.Stopped` stays in the enum and its text, unused). Toggling the current hour is first tried tomorrow (the window says so).
- `Testing/ClassBookingChecks.cs` (`EvClassBooking`, last of the events list): two courses for one room and one teacher, booked and not; the session through to its credit and charge once, the pass repeated in the same hour, the other class in its own booked hour; every reason; the streak, the history of five, no cancel; one booking per hour; every reason has its keyed text. The saveload plan seeds two bookings (`SeedClassBookingForReload`, in the snapshot) one of them for the program of the seeded class under way. `RIMPRISON_TOUR=bookings` shows the Programs tab with bookings, a miss, the window and the warning.

### Custody contracts (`Intake/CustodyContracts.cs`, `Defs/Custody.xml`, `Keyed/Custody.xml`, `UI/IntakeTab.cs`; gap analysis 6, Astra 7)
Intake already pays per level and a transfer request already pays a premium for maximum-security inmates (`IncidentWorker_TransferRequest`, `PrisonDecisionWorker_TransferRequest`: spawned at once, not by the shuttle); export contracts reward production (`Labor/Contracts.cs`, one counter, cleared on met or missed). A custody contract is an obligation to *provide* something for named people: the state offers a cohort to rehabilitate and pays for admitting them and, per person, for the attendance the prison actually delivered. One kind so far, the rehabilitation contract (`CustodyContractDef`: 3-5 places of minimum and medium security, 7-day service window, 4 class sessions, fee 0.5 and bonus 1.5 times the level's intake grant, an offer every 7-10 days, the offer stands 48 h, 4 days to be admitted in).
- **Saved** (`PrisonGame.custody`, `CustodyBook`, `Scribe_Deep`, an empty book when absent so an old save has none): the open `CustodyOffer`, the running `CustodyContract` (def, map, the terms as signed, the arrival deadline and the days added to it, the members, the money paid, the outcome), the last one finished, the next offer day. A `CustodyMember` is a dossier row (level, crime, term, fee, bonus) and, once there is a person, their pawn (a reference only while pending or serving) and thing id, the stay (`CompInmate.timesAdmitted` at admission), admission and window-end ticks, credited sessions, what was paid (the payment checkpoints: fee, bonus), and its state: Pending, Serving, then exactly one of Met, Missed, Forfeited (Dropped if it never came). The state only moves forward, which is the exactly-once judgment (the export contract's clear-on-judge, per person).
- **The offer** (`CustodyContracts.Daily`, from `DailySettlement.Run` after the special measures): once the prison has a classroom (a room of the classroom zone with seats, `ProgramSystem.ListVenues`) and a counsellor on the staff books (`CanTeach`), the first pass sets the clock 7-10 days off, then an offer is sent whenever it is due and no offer or contract stands and the state's measures are not on. The letter is an ordinary `ChoiceLetter_PrisonDecision` (not urgent, so the day's budget of decisions holds it; no answer is a no). It shows the official file only - level, crime, term, fee, bonus - and no person is made for it, so nothing hidden (gang, innocence) is rolled or told. It shows what the cohort asks of the prison (`Supply`: classrooms, the largest one's seats, counsellors, how many can sit at once, class hours needed) against what it has, and the free beds against the beds needed. "Prepare capacity first" is the letter's postpone; signing is allowed with too little and says so (the window will drop the rest).
- **The cohort comes by the shuttle** (`IntakeSystem.AddCustodyCohort`, in `GenerateRoster` after the state's marshals' prisoners and before the policy's inmates): each pending member with a free bed of their level (never overcrowding) is made to the dossier row (`InmateGenerator.Generate(level, number, sex, crime)`, the term set to the row's; the rest of the file is rolled as for any inmate) or, if already made, brought again; `AdmitArrival` calls `Notify_Admitted`, which pays the fee and starts *that* inmate's window. It also restarts the inmate's own sentence clock (`CompInmate.admittedTick`, set when the pawn is made for the roster), so the sentence runs from the day they step in as the window does, and a late arrival is never released inside their window. The shuttle's own intake grant for a new inmate is unchanged and paid as always; the fee is on top. A shuttle that lifts off with members still aboard keeps them as the contract's (`ShuttleSystem.CarryOver` does not put them on `carriedOver`): pending, made once, first on the next roster.
- **Attendance and judgment**: `ProgramSystem.Complete` already credits a session only when the teacher taught half and the inmate sat half; `Notify_Attended` counts each such session (any course) inside the window. The hourly pass (`CustodyContracts.Hourly`) judges each member whose window has ended: the sessions asked, the bonus paid once; fewer, nothing. Nothing is paid early. A course that is switched on but nobody sits earns nothing because it credits nothing.
- **Leaving**: released, paroled, transferred out (`ReleaseSystem.Release`, `InmateUtility.TransferOut`, `ShuttleSystem.Register`), dead (`Patch_Pawn_Kill_Custody`), escaped (`Patch_Pawn_ExitMap_Escape`, and `EscapeUtility.Register`), or found gone, or back on a stay of their own, by the hourly pass: the bonus is forfeit (one who leaves after the end of their window is judged on what they attended instead); the fee stays and nothing is charged. A returning ex-inmate comes through the marshals and is no part of the contract; the same person is never admitted twice into it.
- **Pauses and cancellations** (stated, tested): the 4-day arrival window gains a day for each daily shuttle called off (weather, a threat: `ShuttleSystem.Cancel`) and for each roster the marshals filled before a waiting member, at most 3; a shuttle held back and then landing changes nothing; a member with no free bed of their level waits and is dropped, unpaid, when the window ends; the state's special measures withdraw the open offer and drop the cohort not yet admitted, unpaid (the admitted go on, their windows run; one aboard a shuttle that is on the ground is admitted as the state sent them), and no offer or signing while they last; nobody ever admitted means the contract is called off at no cost; one open offer and one running contract at most. The next offer is 7-10 days after one ends.
- Not done: other kinds of contract; a scheduler priority for the cohort (they take the classes they are eligible for; bookings are how the warden secures hours; the cohort may also be held by a job's hours); a Finance line for bonuses still to be judged (the Intake tab shows it).
- `Testing/CustodyContractChecks.cs` (`EvCustodyContract`, last of the events list): the definition and every text; the offer (dossier, letter, supply numbers, decline, lapse, signing); the cadence; a delayed shuttle, a part of the cohort admitted by the beds there are, the rest left aboard at lift-off and admitted by the next, the fee and the window by each admission, once; real attendance credited through `ProgramSystem.Complete` and the bonus paid once at the end of the window, never early, nothing for a course nobody sat or for a session after the window; every way out; the stated pauses and cancellations. The saveload plan seeds a book with an offer, a contract half way through and a finished one (`SeedCustodyForReload`, in the snapshot) and after the reload runs the passes to see that neither money nor people are made twice. `RIMPRISON_TOUR=custody` shows the offer's letter and the Intake tab's contract rows.

### Release preparation and supervision (`Story/ReleasePrep.cs`, `Defs/ReleasePrep.xml`, `Keyed/ReleasePrep.xml`, `UI/IntakeTab.cs`, `UI/ITab_InmateFile.cs`; gap analysis 6, Astra 3)
`ReleaseSystem.Release` used to roll the reoffence at the gate (`ParoleSystem.ReoffendChance`) and queue the rearrest, with its cause, at once: the outcome was fixed the day they walked out, and the register's news and a parolee's letters (written only by those who would stay out) gave it away. A release now opens a **supervision case** and decides nothing: the case starts at the physical departure (`ShuttleSystem.Notify_Left`, through `OutboundEntry.caseId`), and the outcome is found at the reviews that follow. Compassionate releases (a cause) are as they were (no case). Every other release opens one, an innocent inmate's too (exonerated or never found out) at a risk of nothing - never rearrested, as before, but listed, reviewed and settled like anybody else, so neither a missing row nor the board's trust coming at the gate shows who was innocent. Old saves have no cases; a release already waiting for the shuttle with the old roll keeps its `reoffends` flag and its queued rearrest, and `ReleaseSystem.LegacyReoffendRoll` (checks only) switches the old release back on.
- **Saved** (`PrisonGame.supervision`, `SupervisionBook`, `Scribe_Deep`, empty when absent): `SupervisionCase`s keyed to a stable id (`nextId`, never reused; `version` 1) in release order: the person (`pawn`, a world pawn kept only while the case is open, and the inmate number and stay), parole and recommendation, the state (Awaiting the shuttle, Reviewing, Resolved) and outcome (StayedOut, Rearrested, Voided), the ticks (released, departed, next review, end), the review count (`reviewDays`, rolls made, reviews held), the baseline risk and the weight of the causes in the state they left in, what is known (job, address, and whether an interview or paid lodging gave it), the certificate the interview rests on, the support promised and whether it was carried out (and the lodging paid), the setback (job lost, address withdrawn; Pending, Recovered, Failed), the warden's help (given, paid), the reason, and whether the rearrest was queued. Also `CompInmate.releasePlan` (what the warden promised an inmate still inside; cleared with the stay), `FormerInmate.supervisionId` and `.lodged`, `OutboundEntry.caseId`, seven counters (`PrisonStats.supervision*`). The settled cases kept are the last 60.
- **The review** (`Supervision.Hourly`, one pass over a short list; a review is held when `nextReviewTick` is due, one a day): for `ReviewDaysServed` (7) days after a sentence served, a parolee's own term clamped to 5-14 days, a daily roll of `1 - (1 - risk)^(1/days)`, so that the whole period has exactly the old single roll's chance (calibrated, not multiplied: the check runs thousands of cases through to see it). The hazard is split over the causes the person's situation allows, the same weights as `ParoleLife.PickViolation` (a new offence 1, the weekly reports 1 for a parolee, a job held and lost 1 or none found 1.5, an address held and left 0.5 or none 1.5); a case with nothing done for it is exactly the day's share, and the roll's place within the hazard picks the cause. A parolee's rearrest carries the term they broke; somebody with no terms is arrested for a new offence whatever the cause. A cause that is a job or an address *held* first shows as a **setback** (reported: a letter, a chronicle line, the register's entry updated so their next letter says so), and waits two reviews: unhelped it ends in the rearrest; the warden's help (100, once, paid whatever comes of it, from the Intake tab, which lists every setback waiting for its review first, however many cases are open, so the button is always in reach) mends it 60% of the time and the review goes on. A direct cause (a new offence, the reports, no work, no address) is a rearrest at that review, and no payment touches it. (For somebody with no terms, a helped setback that mends prevents what would have been written up as a new offence: the rearrest reason of a non-parolee is always a new offence, but the cause it came from was the lost job or address - that is the part payment can reach.) The setback letter says the deciding review is two days off, as the Intake line does.
- **What the warden decides** (`ReleasePrep`; the Intake tab's *Release planning* and the file's row): inmates within five days of release or of a hearing are listed with what is known (a job waiting, a certified trade, family) and three choices: *fund lodging* (150), *arrange a job interview* (only with a certificate from the vocational courses and no job waiting) or *leave to existing support* (nothing). A promise is a place with a handler: an administrator or counsellor handles `CasesPerHandler` (3) cases at once, a promise and an open case the warden is working both count, and a settled one gives its place back (the Intake line and the file say "handled x of y"). A promise is kept on the inmate and becomes the case's support at the release. **Lodging is paid at the departure, if the funds are there** (a bill on the new ledger line `RP_Resettlement`, once): a funded lodging makes the address known and takes the "no address" cause out of the hazard; a promise nobody funded changes nothing and the message says so. **The interview** is held at the first review after they leave and wins a job 60% of the time (a job known takes the "no work" cause out); held once.
- **The outcome, counted when it is seen**: a rearrest goes on the `Rearrest`/`returning`/marshals queue exactly as before (`Reoffenders`, `CheckRearrests`: the clawback, the board's trust, the reputation, the 50% to come back), once (`rearrestQueued`, and not at all if the same person is already queued); `stats.reoffended` goes up then and not at the gate; a recommended parolee who stays out builds the board's trust (+0.05) when the review ends, not before. `stats.released` is still counted at the departure, so the stayed-out share of the reform grade (`PrisonGrading.StayedOutShare`) leaves the releases still under review out until they have an outcome, and the Intake tab lists that cohort apart (under review, stayed out, arrested again). A case whose person is hired from the register onto the staff is closed at the hire with nothing to review (voided, nothing counted): hiring is a way out of an open setback or a likely breach, for the hiring fee - they have a job now, and the prison's own eye is on them.
- **Letters before the failure**: `MailKindWorker_Parolee` no longer needs the register to know who stays out: whoever has left under a case and has not been rearrested writes, and says how they are now (a job lost is "still looking for work", lodging the prison paid for is in the letter); the register's news waits while a setback is open; an old entry with no case is as it was.
- **Nobody to review** (Voided, nothing counted): the person died (before or after leaving), is an inmate here again (a recapture or a rearrest while waiting for the shuttle: the shuttle drops their entry, and a stale case would otherwise start later), is one of the player's own (hired from the register: `ExInmates.Hire` closes the case; or freed into the colony), or waited for the shuttle more than 20 days. A new release of somebody whose older case is still open closes the older one (one person, one open case). A pawn the world discarded (the ambulance's way out, `PrisonMap.CollectDownedReleased`) is a person still out there: the case lets go of the pawn and goes on (as it does after a load, where a discarded reference comes back as nobody); one discarded before they left is begun and its release counted (the shuttle never did), with no lodging booked.
- **World pawns** (one keep flag shared by four owners - the register, a rearrest waiting, a return waiting, an open case - so whoever lets go asks `Supervision.HeldElsewhere` first, after taking their own hold off: `ExInmates.Unkeep`, `Reoffenders.Forget`, the returns' tidy-up and the case's own let-go all do): a case under review keeps its pawn while open, within `MaxKept` (40: a prison releasing three to five a day has 25-40 under review; the oldest, nearest the end of their review, let go first; a case still waiting for the shuttle is not counted, its pawn is on the map). A case with no risk keeps nobody (the reference is only to see them leave, dropped at the departure). A case that let go of its pawn borrows the register's at the rearrest when the register still has them, so a rearrest has somebody to bring back whenever the case or the register (programs, a trade, a friend inside) kept them; past the cap and with nothing in the register, nobody. A rearrest takes the pawn over (`Reoffenders.KeepForReturn`) and **ends the register's story of them**: declined (never an applicant again), no news, no visit, the parole term over (no more letters, even once the settled case is pruned), the register's keep given up. Nobody is simulated off the map: a review is a roll on the case.
- Not done: the support is lodging and an interview only (no other kinds); the setback is for a job or an address held (somebody with none breaches the day it is missed); no alert for an open setback (a letter, and the Intake tab); the file shows the release-planning row of an inmate inside, not the case of one gone (the Intake tab has it). Hearings come round every four days once half the sentence is served, so "within five days of a hearing" lists nearly everyone past half (soonest first, twelve shown).
- `Testing/ReleasePrepChecks.cs` (`EvReleasePrep`, last of the events list): the texts; the calibration; the window and the handlers; Astra's proof (two equal cases, one controlled roll, funded lodging against an unfunded promise, nothing decided at the gate even with the old roll forced to "reoffends", every bill, count, trust change and marshal entry once); a payment that never mends a new offence; a setback, help once, mended or not; the interview; the old queued rearrest, the old release and the compassionate one unchanged, an innocent release read the same as a guilty one; a parolee's letter before a later failure (and none after the arrest, the case pruned); the cap and the pawns, the dead, the discarded, an inmate again, a second release; the register closed at the rearrest, its pawn borrowed, the hire (through the register's own `ExInmates.Hire`) and a pawn of the player's faction, the shared keep; the Intake tab's list with a setback on the newest of 21 open cases. Every check moves the clock on to day 60 (the events plan runs at day 0-3, and a throwaway "38 days into its sentence" would be admitted before the game began), works on a supervision book of its own, sets the handlers' capacity it tests (`ReleasePrep.CapacityForChecks`) and puts the board's trust back; the whole set fails if the live book (seeded for the set with two sentinel cases, one due for review with a kept pawn and one waiting whose person is gone), the trust or the clock changed. Every other check's rollback puts back a live case's pawn and keep that the cap let go of during the check. The saveload plan seeds a release in flight (a made-up inmate on the shuttle's outbound list, the entry carrying the case id), one mid-setback with help given and a kept world pawn, one whose pawn the world discarded, one settled each way and an old queued rearrest (`SeedReleasePrepForReload`, every saved field in the snapshot) and after the reload has the shuttle tell the departure (twice: begun, counted and billed once), reviews and settles the rest to see that nothing is paid or queued twice. `RIMPRISON_TOUR=release` shows the Intake tab's planning and cases and the file's row (paused while its made-up cases are in the book).

### Supermax (`Security/Supermax.cs`, `Security/SupermaxEscort.cs`, `Defs/Supermax.xml`)
`RP_Supermax` is the level above maximum: wing-only (`RP_WingSupermax` painted over its cells), cell meals, `escortOnly` and `yardAlone`
(`SecurityLevelDef` flags), danger factor 3, the highest daily payment (500), never sent by the state (the intake row is hidden and the roster
takes none; paid at the rate of 500 only when the rules sent them, see below). Its default regime is 8 h asleep and 9 h locked up, three `RP_ShowerTime` slots (07:00,
12:00, 17:00: none of them an hour any other level showers in, protective custody's is 10:00) and four `RP_YardAlone` slots (09:00, 11:00, 14:00, 16:00). **The yard is a
rota**: a patch takes one inmate at a time (about 2300 ticks with the walks), so a unit of four cells with one patch needs four yard hours to give each of them one a
day; a unit with more cells than yard patches times four misses some, and at midnight a message names who had no yard in the last day
(`SupermaxSystem.MissedYard`). The longest-without goes first (`CompInmate.yardSlot`), so the rota is fair. A saved game keeps the regime it has (the
default is for new games); the warden edits the slots in the Regime tab like any other level's.
Behind the `RP_Bur_Supermax` project (after maximum security), which unlocks the level, the wing and the yard zone; the state penitentiary has it done
and a small unit built (the north half of the segregation block: four supermax cells behind a staff-only security door, a shower room and a yard of their own).
- **Out of the cell only with a guard.** An `escortOnly` level's cell door is locked in every hour of the regime (`PrisonMap.CellDoorOpen`; a supermax owner who is
  out of the cell does not hold it open for others, and a fire in the cell or in the room outside it opens it: the one thing that does), and
  `JobGiver_InmateRegime` keeps them in the cell (or the bed at night) whatever the slot says: no canteen, class, visit or yard of their own. The cell is a
  room with one bed (`IntakeSystem.SingleCell`: a wing cell with two beds takes no supermax inmate).
  `WorkGiver_EscortInmate` (Warden, priority 145) offers a guard the inmates whose slot is the shower (and who are dirty, below 0.8 hygiene) or the yard (and
  have not had this hour's), from `InmateCandidates.Escort`, a shower with nobody else in its room (the level's own wing's showers first, then the prison's)
  or a free patch of the level's own yard (`PrisonZoneDef.yardFor`, a `ZoneGrid` patch of at least four cells; one inmate in it at a time, the one
  longest without the yard first); the guard's post is another cell of the same room as the inmate's target. `JobDriver_EscortInmate` collects them at the cell, sets `CompInmate.leg`
  (`EscortWalk`), walks to the guard's post while the inmate's own job is a walk (`LocomotionUrgency.Walk`) to the same target (never a follow job: a follower jogs and
  arrives first), waits (the shower until they are clean or another inmate comes into the room, the yard 2000 ticks, a lockdown ends either), fetches them and walks
  them back and sees them in. Not `casualInterruptible`.
- **The guard's hand on the arm** (`EscortWalk.Holds`): the pass exists only while the guard is on the escort job for that inmate, standing (not downed), spawned on the
  same map and within 25 cells (`EscortWalk.NearCells`). `CompInmate` checks it every 15 ticks and the job on every beat, and the walk is cleared the moment any of it stops
  (a fallen-behind inmate, a downed or killed or fired guard, an alarm). The inmate's own AI uses it for nothing but the walk: on a walk no toilet and no canteen errand
  (the guard has them back within the hour), a supermax inmate's toilet is only ever one in the room they stand in, and a yard wander is about the patch they were walked to
  (and a walk back to it when they are out of it), never "the zone at their feet".
- **Never outside the cell without a guard.** A supermax inmate who is out of their cell with no walk (just moved to the level, let out of a hearing, a medical bed or
  solitary, left by a guard who stopped, or after the walk was cleared) stands where they are (`EscortWalk.Stranded`; they do not walk home alone through the
  prison) and is a candidate for `EscortKind.Home`: the same work giver offers a free guard a walk to the cell (the whole way, from wherever they are, through any
  staff-only door on it: the unit door's mode never shuts them out of their own cell). A patient is not walked (they stay in the medical bed until they are well).
  The guard goes to the inmate at a jog and walks with them at the pace the inmate keeps (`JobDriver_EscortInmate.Pace`): the whole job at a walk took a guard from the far side of
  the prison two to three hours (six, from the gate at a shift change).
  **A staff strike does not leave them there**: a striker takes no new work (`Patch_JobGiver_Work_Strike`) but the fire, the injured and this walk (`SupermaxSystem.FetchJobFor`,
  asked for apart from the scan, whose first find, a restraint say, a strike throws away; `TryPlan` offers a striker no shower or yard walk). Without it a break that ended
  a walk, or a killer moved in from another wing, stood outside for the twelve hours of the walkout, starving (the meal is brought to the cell, by staff on strike).
- **A load in the middle of a walk.** The walk is not saved; the guard's job is. After a load every toil of `JobDriver_EscortInmate` finds no walk, makes it again from the
  job (`EnsureLeg`: the driver was loaded, so the first toil to ask rebuilds the walk of the phase it is in: out for the lead, the stay and the fetch, back for the way
  home) or, when it cannot (the guard is not near), ends the job, and the inmate is fetched. The hearing's escort makes its walk again on each beat of its lead and wait.
  `ParoleScene.Notify_Escorted` holds the same leg for a hearing (the doors on the way to the post in the hearing room); the guard's wait ends when the case is
  no longer this guard's (the inmate left their chair and the case is called again), not after two hours.
- **Who goes there** (`SupermaxSystem.TrySend`: the project done, a free supermax cell, not death row; otherwise every caller does what it did): the killer
  of a member of staff at the charge (`DeathAftermath.ChargeWithMurder`), a convicted murderer at the verdict (`Trials.Convict`), the hostage ringleader
  (`HostageCrisis.Charge`), whoever the warden sends (the inmate's button, the Inmates tab menu). The chronicle line says why. **The pay** is the supermax rate (500) for
  the rules' sends and the **maximum** rate (200) for the warden's own (`SupermaxSystem.DailyPayment`, from `CompInmate.supermaxWhy`: an order is not a reason; moving a
  shoplifter there would have paid six times what they are worth; a send from before the reason was kept pays the supermax rate). A staff killer the court **acquits**
  goes back to the level they came from (`CompInmate.levelBeforeSupermax`, else maximum; `SupermaxSystem.StepDownAcquitted`). **No parole** (`ParoleSystem.MayBeParoled`:
  no hearing is due, the board does not roll, a hearing on the books is dropped when they are sent, a letter put to the warden earlier is refused): the way down is the
  step-down. Witness protection does not take a supermax inmate (it would skip the step-down and let them mix with the vulnerable).
- **A long stay** (`RP_SupermaxStay`, a hediff added after the first week, severity the weeks served, four stages): a lower mood
  (`ThoughtWorker_Hediff`, -3 to -13) and a higher mental break threshold (+0.02 to +0.09). Gone when they leave the level, and when they leave the prison (the active flag
  dropping on release, parole or a transfer takes it off). Not an illness: no medical rest, no tending.
- **Step-down.** After ten days with no misconduct the staff saw (and ten in supermax) the warden gets a decision letter (`RP_Decision_SupermaxStepDown`): accept moves them to
  maximum security when there is a cell, decline asks again in five days (`CompInmate.supermaxAskTick`). The automatic reclassification never touches them.
- Visits are not scheduled for them, and they do not ask for protective custody. Saved: `CompInmate.supermaxAskTick`, `yardSlot`, `supermaxWhy` and `levelBeforeSupermax` (in the saveload snapshot).
- **Known limits.** A fire opens the cell of the burning room and the cells that open onto a burning corridor, nothing more (no alarm opens the whole unit). The warden's own
  "to protective custody" button still works for a supermax inmate (the warden's choice; it skips the step-down). The state penitentiary's unit door across the corridor is
  staff-only (the unit's yard and shower room doors are ordinary ones inside it), so a supermax inmate is never on the corridor without a guard; in a layout the player builds,
  the same holds for whatever door they set to staff-only.

### Psychiatric care: the criminally insane and a psych wing (`Programs/PsychWing.cs`, `Defs/PsychWing.xml`; gap analysis 4, 1.6)
`RP_Psych` is a level for inmates whose minds are failing, never sent by the state (`SecurityLevelDef.referralOnly`: no intake row, none on the roster). It is `wingOnly` (`RP_WingPsych` painted over its cells) and `paddedOnly`
(a new flag: a bed counts for it only if it is a padded bed (`RP_PaddedBed`) and the only bed of a room with one place, `IntakeSystem.CollectFreeBeds`; `InmateUtility.BedFitsLevel` is the one test, so a plain bed in the painted wing is
no home for psychiatric care: `HasRoomIn`, `ChangeSecurity` and the hourly wrong-wing move all use it). Danger factor 1.5, daily payment 300, a regime of its own: 8 h asleep, 8 h locked up, three meals (07:00, 13:00, 21:00: no gap over 11 h), two showers, three free hours,
**no work hour** (`RP_Work` is nowhere in it; and the workshop and kitchen jobs allow only minimum and medium security anyway, `InmateJobDef.allowedSecurity`). The conduct ladder is the way out, after fourteen clean days **that have no break in them**
(`CompInmate.lastBreakTick`): to the level they were moved from (`CompInmate.psychFrom`, set by the referral and cleared when they change level), or `promoteTo` (medium) when that is not known or they cannot go back to it
(`PsychWing.StepOutLevel`, used by `ReclassificationSystem.MoveFor`). A Maximum inmate therefore comes back to Maximum, not two steps down to medium; protective custody (a witness among them) is never referred (`PsychWing.CannotMoveWho`).
**Unlock:** `RP_Bur_PsychWing` (1200, 16 h of paperwork) needs `RP_Bur_Psychology` and opens the level, the wing zone and the padded bed; the state penitentiary starts with it done.
- **A break is counted** (`CompInmate.breakTicks`, ten days kept; one break within three hours of the last is the same incident): a mood break (`Patch_MentalStateHandler_TryStartMentalState_Psych`, `causedByMood`), and a **vandalism or an assault on staff
  that the prison saw** (`MisconductUtility.Record` with `seen` -> `PsychWing.CountsAsTemper`). So a break nobody saw is no fact the chronicle or the psychologist's count may have (an unwatched vandalism, like for the sanction and the danger map, is not
  recorded as seen), and the mod's states are not counted by what they are: **riots are not breaks** (a riot is a protest of many about their conditions, and every rioter would be a case after two of them), nor an assault forced by a restraint resisted, an old
  score, or one that becomes a hostage-taking (the assault is the one break). Not the story's states either (a hostage held, a fight with a score behind it, an escape). Vanilla sends a letter for every mood break of a prisoner of the colony.
- **The assessment** (`PsychologyUtility.Complete` -> `PsychWing.Review`): two breaks in ten days (and since their last move: the ones before were acted on), or a trait the level names (`PsychLevelExtension.traits`: pyromaniac, bloodlust)
  **found at the first assessment** and then kept as a case (`CompInmate.psychTraitCase`) until they change level: a case that the first assessment could not act on (the project not done, no cell free) is acted on later, and a step-down is not sent back for
  what they are (the move clears it). `WantsSession` is true for an inmate whose case is due, a cell is free and the last look was two days ago (`CompInmate.psychCheckTick`), so the
  psychologist is sent for them like for a first assessment. The session moves them with `InmateUtility.ChangeSecurity`, the reason in the chronicle line ("moved to psychiatric care, on the psychologist's referral, after 2 mental breaks in 10 days"
  or "... after a pyromaniac streak the assessment found"). With no padded cell free nothing moves, the warden is told (once in two days, not at every session) and it is looked at again two days later; without the project, or for one who cannot be moved at
  all (death row, supermax, protective custody, on the way out), nothing moves and nothing is said. The line names the breaks or the trait and nothing the staff have not learned (no hidden reputation, no unknown gang: a check reads it).
- **Padded cells** (`PaddedCells`): a padded cell is a room indoors with a padded bed that **fits the psych level** (`InmateUtility.BedFitsLevel`: painted as the wing and the only bed of its room); a padded bed in a dormitory, off the wing's paint or outdoors
  (where the room is the whole outdoors) pads nothing and houses no one (`IntakeSystem.CollectFreeBeds`). An **inmate** (not staff, not a manhunter) **in the cell of their own bed** who is in a mental state does no harm: their blows on themself and on the
  things in the room and on its edge (its walls and its door: what vanilla's tantrum and the mod's vandalism go for) do nothing (`Patch_Thing_TakeDamage_Padded`; a person in the cell with them is still in danger: the padding is for the cell), and no fire they
  start there takes (`TryStartFireIn`, `TryAttachFire`, the same condition). Anywhere else a break harms as before. The question is cheap: the map's few padded beds are walked, not the room's things, and a map with none answers at once. (Vanilla's tantrum
  cannot smash the colony's furniture for a prisoner at all, only items of value and people: the break that smashes prison property is the mod's own `RP_Vandalizing`, and that is what the check runs in real ticks beside a plain cell.)
- **Medication rounds**: `RP_Antipsychotic` (a bottle of doses, in the supply catalog) and the hediff `RP_Medicated` (one dose = severity 1, falling a day's worth a day, so a dose lasts about a day; the game's break threshold -0.04 / -0.08).
  A doctor (the Doctor work type, and only the doctors: a guard has that work too, to tend and rescue, but is wanted at the posts and a long walk for a dose would take them off one, `ShiftSystem.IsMedic`; `WorkGiver_MedicationRound`, priority 60 below tending; candidates `InmateCandidates.Medication`: an active inmate of the level, not in a break, whose cover is at half a dose or less or gone) takes a dose off the
  map and gives it (`JobDriver_MedicationRound`: fetch, carry, walk, a short wait; `PsychWing.Give`: severity 1, `CompInmate.lastDoseTick`, Medicine experience). **A missed round**: the cover runs out; every hour without it is counted
  (`CompInmate.unmedicatedHours`, counted wherever they are), and after six hours there is an 8% chance an hour of a break (`PsychWing.StartBreak`: a `MentalBreakDef` that can happen to them and is **not aggressive**: berserk and the rages are vanilla's
  mood breaker's, and do not wait for a missed round), against 0.4% with cover, **rolled only while they are in their padded cell** (the regime has them in it sixteen hours of the day), so a break never comes among the others in the canteen, the showers or the yard.
  The break goes through the same count, so a patient who relapses twice is a case for the next level up. Midnight (`PsychWing.Daily`): with patients, the drug is kept at four plus four doses a patient (ordered from the supply company when it is not,
  paid as supplies); with none on the map, or with no doctor who can make the rounds (none hired, or all on leave: `PsychWing.AnyMedic`), the warden is told.
- **State penitentiary**: the medical ward's north end is two padded cells (`Padded cell A` 2 x 3 and `B` 3 x 3: padded bed, toilet, lamp, B a heater, a security door each onto the ward; the ward's beds moved south), painted as the psych wing,
  and 12 doses at the start. The inherited facility has none and no project is done in it.
- **Saved**: `CompInmate.breakTicks`, `psychCheckTick`, `unmedicatedHours`, `lastDoseTick`, `lastBreakTick`, `psychTraitCase`, `psychFrom` (in the saveload snapshot, `PsychDescribe`; `lastDoseTick` is kept but nothing reads it yet). **Tests**: `Testing/PsychChecks.cs`,
  `PsychRounds.cs`, `PsychReviewChecks.cs` (the review fixes: where a break comes, what a padded bed pads, the gang exit, the count, the way out, the trait case, the medics, the dose, the intake), `PsychReload.cs`; the plan `psych` runs them alone (about
  2 minutes), the events plan runs them after its events, the showcase plan checks the layout, the saveload plan the fields.
- **Known limits.** A patient in a break is dealt with by the guards as anyone is (the subduing and the punishment of the prison do not know that a missed round caused it). The cover is one number: a doctor on a long night shift gaps (rounds are
  offered below half a dose, twelve hours before it runs out). No tab for it: the inmate's health tab shows the hediff, the chronicle the referral. Nothing sends the warden's own choice of inmate to psychiatric care (the psychologist decides).
  The penitentiary's two padded cells open onto the medical ward, so a patient's walk to meals, showers and the yard crosses the ward's beds (a redesign of the layout, not done). A patient out of their cell with no cover is not confined to it:
  the break waits for the cell. A padded bed can still be built anywhere once the project is done; it only pads and houses nothing outside the wing.

### Inmates at risk: the watch list (`Programs/WatchList.cs`, `Defs/WatchList.xml`, `Patches/WatchList.xml`, `Keyed/WatchList.xml`; gap analysis 7, item 6)
- **At risk**: a trigger in the last 3 days and mood under 10% for more than 24 hours. The triggers: a Dear John letter and a death in the family (the post's `MailKindWorker.Deliver`), the death of kin inside
  (`Kin.Notify_Died`), a green light (`GangExit`: the threat and each beating), a beating over a debt (`DebtSystem.TryBeat`) - these four write `CompInmate.riskEvent`/`riskEventTick` - and, read from what is already
  saved, a parole refusal (`paroleDenialTick`), a life term begun (a lifer's `admittedTick`, not death row; one handed down mid-stay by a hostage charge is hooked, `HostageCrisis.Charge`) and a supermax stay of more
  than 3 days (while it lasts, dated from the day it passed 3 days). `WatchList.Trigger` names the latest.
- **The flag**: the hourly pass (`WatchList.Hourly`, one pass over the inmates, a few field reads each) keeps the clock of very low mood (`lowMoodTick`). With a psychologist on the staff (not away) the hourly pass
  waits and the session is due (`PsychologyUtility.WantsSession` -> `AssessmentDue`); the session flags them (`Notify_Session` from `Complete`). Not seen within 12 more hours, or with no psychologist, the unit
  officer of their wing (serving, on the map) flags them, else any guard on duty on site (`CareDetail.Why`). At most two letters a pass; not again within 2 days of a period's end.
- **The letter** (`RP_Decision_WatchList`, budgeted as any decision, a day to answer, unanswered is "nothing"; `amount` is the flag's tick so a letter of an earlier period decides nothing) names who flagged them, why,
  the mood, and states each answer's cost: a **watch** (the check due 1000 ticks after the last, so about every half hour with the walk, at most 1800 between two; `RP_OnWatch` -8 mood for the belongings taken;
  `Sanctions.Banned(LoseYard)` is true while it lasts, so free time is in the cell), a **padded cell** (only when `PsychWing.CannotMove` says yes: the project done and a padded cell free; the move is the psych wing's
  `ChangeSecurity`, `psychFrom` kept; given up when the period ends or the warden ends it - back to `psychFrom` (`LeavePadded`: only the move of `measureTick`, nobody has moved them since, no psych case found
  meanwhile; no room there, they stay, told) - unless they had a case of their own for the wing when it was chosen, then it lasts and conduct moves them out as for any patient; the letter says which), **counselling** (one hour-long session a day by a counsellor, else the psychologist: 3 hours of their time, stated; +8 mood for a
  day and -10 tension each), or **nothing**. The period (`riskTick` .. `watchUntilTick`) is 3 days, or less once the mood is above 30% (whatever the answer); one who leaves the prison is taken off and the letters closed
  (`StorySystem.Notify_Leaving`). A choice that cannot be done when it is made (the padded cell taken, the counsellor gone) is nothing, with a chronicle line and a message beside the answer the chronicle recorded.
- **The warden's End** (Intel, `EndByWarden`) stops the measure, not the period: a watch ends (the yard and the belongings come back), the sessions stop, a padded cell given for the period is given up; the answer
  becomes nothing and they stay on the list, at risk, with the crisis rules of one nothing was done for, until the time is up or the mood recovers. With nothing done there is nothing to end (no button).
- **The watch is care work**: `CareKind.Watch` in the care detail's queue (oldest-waiting reporting, the reserve takes it first through `RP_WatchCheckGiver`), and for every guard the emergency giver
  `WorkGiver_WatchCheck` (Warden, priority 185, under the rescue of the downed): walk to them, look in for 120 ticks. The check and the crisis call reserve the inmate on a layer of their own (`RP_WatchLayer`): one
  guard at a time, and a doctor's tend, a rescue or therapy (the default layer) is never blocked by them. **Leisure gives way to them**: emergency work beats recreation only when a guard next chooses a job, and a
  guard at leisure does not choose again until it is over (a meditation is 5000 ticks, a staff-room break 2000, and the guard kept on site off shift has all their sleeping hours for it), so at the map's 250-tick beat
  (`WatchList.CallFreeGuards`) each due check or crisis call nobody is on calls the guard at leisure who would be given it (`LeisureRank`: idle or patrolling first, then recreation, then a staff-room break; the nearest
  of each; never asleep, drafted, on strike or on an ordered job) to choose again (`CheckForJobOverride`), and the emergency node gives it to them.
- **The watch's timing rule** (stability round 24): a check falls due `CheckEveryTicks` (1000) after the last; the beat calls a guard to a due check within `CallEveryTicks` (250); the guard walks there and looks
  (`LookTicks`, 120); all of it must fall within `CheckMostTicks` (1800), the line between a watch kept and one let lapse (`Watched`). So a guard on duty within 430 ticks' walk of the inmate (about 30 cells)
  keeps the watch; one further off, busy on something the beat does not call them from, or off shift lets it lapse, and the crisis rules for the unwatched apply. The product does not call earlier than the check
  is due (a check before the half hour is a shorter half hour); the "watch ticks" check holds its scene to the rule (a walk of a few steps, the guard kept on shift) and fails a scene that does not fit it.
- **The crisis** (`RP_Crisis`, `MentalState_Crisis`, not a mood break: nothing else starts it): only for one flagged, answered and not watched whose mood has stayed under 10% for 24 more hours (since the flag, or since
  it last went low). Answered: no roll while the letter waits on the desk or is held back by the day's budget (the grace is the letter's own day, or the budget's hold; the lapse is "nothing" and the rolls start).
  Not watched: no watch, or a watch whose last check (or its start) is more than 1800 ticks ago (`WatchList.Watched`: a watch the guards do not keep up protects nobody). Awake, not downed, not held, not leaving, not in an evacuation; 10% a day (an hourly roll). They keep to their room. Any member of staff within 2.9 cells in that room (or in its doorway beside them) ends it
  ("reached them in time"); one the staff see (`TensionUtility.Watched`: a guard, a tower, a camera), or one on watch, has a guard sent (`WorkGiver_CrisisResponse`, emergency 195, as the rescue of the downed is).
  Nobody in 2000 ticks: two vanilla cuts on the arms (about 1.4 blood a day, a death in some sixteen hours if nobody tends them), a letter "harmed themself" and a chronicle line; the rescue of the downed, the doctors'
  tending, the death's neglect inquiry (blood loss) and the bitter family letter are the existing ones, unchanged. In the padded cell of their own bed nothing happens (the padding). Wording: "a crisis", "harmed
  themself", no more.
- **The setting** "Self-harm crises" (default on, `RimPrisonSettings.selfHarmCrises`): off, the flag, the letter and the watch happen and no crisis ever starts (`TryStartCrisis` refuses; the letter leaves out its line
  on the crisis); a crisis already running when it is turned off ends at once, with no harm.
- **Saved** on `CompInmate`: `riskTick`, `watchUntilTick`, `crisisCount` (defaults -1, -1, 0) and `riskEvent`, `riskEventTick`, `riskCause`, `lowMoodTick`, `watchAnswer`, `watchCheckTick`, `counselTick`, `riskEndTick`,
  `measureTick` (when the answer's measure began); a new stay clears all but the crisis count. Intel's "Watch list" (each inmate, the answer, the days left; the letter reopened, "letter tomorrow" for one the budget
  holds, or "End" for a measure); the file's status line.
- Not done: the belongings are a mood penalty only (nothing is moved out of the cell). Left as it is: a blood-loss death goes to the neglect inquiry unless an inmate hit the victim in the day before
  (`DeathAftermath.Notify_Died`'s `RecentAssailant`): an inmate in a fight within a day of a self-harm death gets the murder charge instead (the debt beating itself is at least two days earlier). A parolee brought back as a
  lifer reads as "began a life sentence" (their admission is reset: it is a life term begun again).

### Contraband
Items: shiv (the kitchen's knife is one: kitchen theft gives a shiv), tools (from workshop; the sharpened spoon is one of them, `RP_Tools`), drugs (beer, smokeleaf, yayo, flake), hooch, cellphone.
There is no lighter: it would have no job here (nothing in the prison lights a fire on purpose), so the kitchen knife and the spoon are the shiv and the tools, and the list stops at what has a use.
Sources: visitors, deliveries, workshop/kitchen theft, thrown over the fence, corrupt staff, and what the inmates make inside (below). Stored in inmate
inventory or a hidden cell stash. Found by shakedowns/frisks → misconduct. Contraband effects: weapons enable deadly
fights, drugs give mood + addiction/overdose, phones raise escape planning, tools/spoons enable tunnels.

**Made inside** (`Contraband/Brewing.cs`, `Contraband/Garden.cs`; all hidden until found):
- *Hooch.* A kitchen worker (`InmateJobDef.stealsFood`, the kitchen job) nobody is watching pockets a piece of raw fruit or vegetables from the Kitchen zone (8% an hour of work, tension 15 or more; nothing in the kitchen, nothing to take) and hides
  a lot of `RP_HoochMash` (kind `Brew`, a `CompHoochMash` holding the tick it was started; the stash's stack keeps the older start, and a piece split off a lot keeps the lot's) in their cell stash. Two days on (the stash's own 250-tick pass, `HoochUtility.Ferment`, wherever the inmate is) it
  is `RP_Hooch`, two to a lot: a beer-like drink (a `DrugBase` with the Alcohol chemical, a little stronger and likelier to addict) that is kind `Drug` like the others, so the inmate drinks it through `ContrabandUtility.TryUseDrugs`, a dealer keeps it as
