  stock, a dog smells it. Both have a smuggle weight of nothing (they are never brought in from outside); the mash, the stolen tools and the cellphone are `tradeability` None and kept out of the vanilla cargo pod (`preventSpawningInResourcePod`), which takes any item a trader could sell worth one to forty silver. A cell search finds the mash as the still and the hooch as a stash, a bust and an amnesty take them like any stash.
- *The garden's smokeleaf.* A gardener inmate (`InmateJobDef.growsContraband`, the garden job) who is inside for `RP_Crime_SmokeleafGrowing` sows one patch among the garden's crops when nobody is watching (15% an hour of work): a `GardenPatch`
  (cell, gardener, sown tick, last tended, harvests) in `PrisonMap.patches`, never drawn or listed. Ripe after five days, it is harvested hourly into joints in the gardener's pockets, but only by the gardener at their garden job (`growsContraband`), within nine cells of the patch and unwatched (a job that is work in the laundry is no harvest, and the watch that counts is the one over the patch), and it grows back. A gardener who has not worked the garden for eight days (moved to another job, or the job taken away) has the patch overgrown. A harvest is made inside, not smuggled in: it is not in the Statistics tab's smuggled count or the day's security grade (`ContrabandUtility.Give(.., smuggled: false)`; the mash never was). For a gardener who
  carries for a dealer's ring that is `RingSystem.Notify_BroughtIn` with the garden as the source (`RingSystem.GardenSource`): a supplier link of its own (`RingLink.garden`, a `Relative` link beside an outside supplier, one for each gardener whose harvests feed the ring: the second gardener's patch, found, reveals their own link, and a bust that takes out a garden clears that gardener's patch), unknown
  until found, and the joints are what the courier carries for the dealer. The dealer's ring takes on a gardener with a patch (a weight of 4 in `Recruit`). The staff gardener (12% an hour within nine cells of it) and guards about the garden (5%, less for a
  guard on the gardener's payroll) may come across it, and a cell search of the gardener's cell finds it (60% a search): pulled up, marked on the map of finds (`FindSource.Garden`, glyph G), the gardener written up, and, for a ring member, their link and the
  garden's found and the heat raised (`RingSystem.Notify_GardenFound`). A bust takes the patch out with the garden's link; a patch whose gardener has left is overgrown, and their garden link goes with them (it is not handed to the dealer like a known outside supplier).

### Count the tools back in (`Labor/ToolCount.cs`, `Defs/ToolCount.xml`, `Languages/English/Keyed/ToolCount.xml`)

The theft hook (`ContrabandUtility.Notify_WorkedWithTools`, hourly for an inmate whose job has `toolTheft`) used to make a tool or a knife out of nothing. It now takes one from a finite supply where the room has one, and the supply is counted.

- **The supply.** One `ToolPool` per job zone the labour code knows that has benches - the workshop (`RP_Workshop`: a `CompWorkstation` with an input, the license-plate presses) and the kitchen (`RP_Kitchen`: the stoves `InmateJobWorker_Kitchen.KitchenStoves` finds) - **a ledger per zone, not per painted room** (the labour code asks the zone, never a room, and a zone has no id that survives repainting; a "room split" is then a non-event). `owned` is `benches x PerBench` (4 a press, 3 a stove); `ToolCount.Hourly` makes a pool at full the first hour it sees benches (an old save's too: it is saved from then on, never made twice), moves `owned` by `PerBench` when a bench comes or goes, and **retires** the pool - with every trace to it - when the zone has had no benches for a day (`BenchlessGraceTicks`: a press that is uninstalled or rebuilt is the same room, and nobody can steal from a room with no bench meanwhile) or has no cells left (no tool is made, nobody is told, a later find of one of its tools says nothing). `Hourly` runs for the first home map only. The garden has no benches and keeps the old unlimited creation; so does an autotest run unless `ToolCount.TestOn` (as the funeral and the letter budget).
- **The trace.** A theft in a counted room does not run the old creation: `ToolCount.Steal` gives one thing (`ContrabandUtility.Give(..., tracedTo: pool)`, stack of one) and writes `ToolTrace{thingId, poolId, period, order, tick}` (`order` from `ToolLedgers.nextOrder`: what a count has counted is the traces below the pool's `countedOrder`; a tick would not tell a theft from a count in the same tick) *before* it is handed over; nothing is taken from a supply with nothing in it (`owned - missing`) or while the period is supervised. **Stacking is forbidden for a traced thing:** a Harmony postfix on `Thing.CanStackWith` (which `Thing.TryAbsorbStack`, `ThingOwner.TryAdd`/`TryTransferToContainer` and `GenPlace` all ask, through the `ThingWithComps` override's `base` call) answers false when either thing is in `ToolLedgers.byThing` (an index on the traces, rebuilt on load; the postfix does nothing while it is empty). A stack of one that is split off is the same thing with the same id, so the trace survives a pocket, a stash and the ground. Old contraband is never traced.
- **The period and the count.** An hourly pass reads, for each pool, whether any inmate of its job (an active one with the program, on a `RP_Work` slot of their level's regime today) is on shift: a rise is a period begun (`period++`, the crew rostered, `issued`), a fall one ended (`countDue`). A guard (on the Warden work type, `RP_CountTools`, just under the headcount) or a workman (on the Hauling type, `RP_CountToolsWorkman`) with nothing more pressing walks to a bench and counts for 300 ticks (`JobDriver_CountTools`, a job and not a pass); a count nobody came for is dropped when the next period begins, with a message that it was not made (nothing of what it would have found). The period's end makes a count due **whether or not the warden has been asked**: a count while a letter is open says its message, updates what the staff know and sends no second letter. `Counted` sets what the staff know (`knownMissing`, `countedPeriod/Tick/Issued/Returned`) from the true ledger (`Missing`: the traces not paid for): a rise is a message (room and period) and, unless the warden is already asked, a letter - never a pawn, a name, a `suspected` flag or a search. **The screens read only what was counted** (`IntelLine`); the output cut reads it too. **A find changes the staff's number only for a tool they had counted** (`Notify_Taken`: `trace.order < countedOrder`); a tool taken after the last count and found before the next says nothing and the next count finds the room whole of it. Replace pays for, and marks as replaced, counted traces only.
- **The answer** (`RP_Decision_ToolCount`, budgeted, not urgent; a day; unanswered or lapsed: *carry on short*): *supervised re-count* (the next period starts `RecountLateHours` = 1 late and is supervised, so no theft in it), *search the crew* (the workers of the period(s) that were counted - `countedWorkers`, snapshotted at the count and added to by each count while the letter waits, not whoever works the room when it is answered - are owed a frisk - `WorkGiver_Frisk.Owed` reads `ToolCount.SearchOwed` - and the next period starts `SearchLateHours` = 2 late; a day, then it lapses), *carry on short*. If the room is at work when it is answered the hold starts now. A room's work is held by `RegimeWorker_Work` (the crew wait in the zone). A known shortage costs `CutPerTool` = 15% of the output a tool up to `MaxCut` = 50%, as longer work (`WorkTicks` in the press's and the stove's toils), until it is put right; so the three answers differ in what they cost beside that: time (re-count, search) for less opportunity or a way to get the tool back, nothing (carry on) for the cut and the opportunity. **Replace** (Intel's button): `ReplaceCost` = $40 a known missing tool from the supplies; the traces are marked `replaced` (a tool that turns up later is only taken: the supply does not get it back).
- **Recovery.** `Notify_Taken` at each place contraband is taken - `ContrabandUtility.Search` (a frisk or a pat-down: the pockets and the hand), `StashUtility.Search`, `Amnesty.HandIn`, `RingSystem.Confiscate` and a found tunnel (`TunnelSystem.SearchCell` -> `Notify_TunnelFound`: the traced tools its owner holds are taken with it, and are finds like the others: the map's find marks, the game's and the day's counts, a possession misconduct) - removes the trace (so **once**), puts the pool right, and says the room, the period and where, never who. A tool destroyed some other way stays missing (no stock is made).
- **Saved:** `PrisonGame.tools` (`ToolLedgers`: pools with every field above, traces; the traces of replaced tools are let go of after `ReplacedKeepTicks` = 5 days); old saves load with none and get pools at full the first hour. The saveload plan seeds two pools and two traces off every default and a traced tool in a real inmate's stash (`tools=` in the snapshot; after the load the tool is taken from the stash once).
- Checks: `Testing/ToolCountChecks.cs` (`EvToolCount`, the last of the events list; `ToolCount.TestOn`). Windowed tour: `RIMPRISON_TOUR=tools`.
- Not done: the cap on a kitchen's output reads the stoves' cook toil only (the staff cook's meals are not slowed); the garden is not counted (no benches); a work period is per zone, not per bench or per level; no stat counter; a tool carried off the map is only a shortage (nobody is told it left).

### Cells by conduct
`Inmates/CellQuality.cs`, `PrisonGame.cellsByConduct` (Policy, default on). A cell's grade (0-5) is vanilla's stage of its room's impressiveness (20/30/40/50/65 are the floors of 1-5), cached per room on the bed refresh walk (`PrisonMap.GradeOf`). An inmate's entitlement
(`CompInmate.entitlement`, 0-5, a new arrival 1) rises a step per 5 clean days and per passed program, and falls 2 at once for a seen misconduct of severity 2 or more. The grade is kept apart from the social fit (`Cellmates.Fit`: wing, cellmates, gangs, feuds): `CellQuality.Term` (-1 a grade below the entitlement, -1.5 above) breaks ties for an arrival (`PickBed`: the social fit decides, the term within 1 point of the best of it)
and between equally good targets of the ordinary review, and decides the grade moves, which may never make the social fit worse and never go into a rival gang, a feud, an extortion pair or the other sex. The daily review moves bad fits first (on the social fit alone), then an inmate two or more grades off
their entitlement (`ReviewWouldForGrade`) in the same two-a-day quota, to a free cell whose term is better by a grade-step (`BestFreeForGrade`); each candidate's own fit is worked out just before the choice. A grade move needs the mismatch at two daily reviews in a row
(`CompInmate.gradeMismatchTick`) and not another grade move in the last 3 days (`gradeMovedTick`). Protective custody, psychiatric care,
supermax, death row, solitary and padded beds are outside it, as are the warden's placements within their hold. Off: no term, no move.

### Ex-inmates come back
`Story/ExInmates.cs`, `FormerInmate.pawn/past/applied/declined/hired/lastVisitTick`, `CompStaff.formerPast`. A released inmate (not one who reoffends: the rearrest keeps those) with a passed program, a trade or an `RP_Friend` still inside is kept as a forcefully kept world
pawn (`MaxKept` 20, the oldest released let go; `FormerInmates.Notify_Released`, `ExInmates.EnforceCap`); the record at the gate is `ExInmatePast` (crime, days served, serious incidents, programs, trade, gang and rank and whether the warden knew it, dealer, ring). The daily pass lets the keep go
when the story is done (application window of 14 days shut or answered, no friend inside, dead). **Application**: `ExInmates.ApplicationRoll` (daily, 12% per kept former inmate, a test seam for the odds), 3 to 14 days after release, only while an upkeep post (cook, workman, gardener: fewer on the staff than one per
`InmatesPerPost` = 40 inmates) is open and they can do the first work type of one; one letter at a time (`RP_Decision_ExInmate`, `ExInmateDecision`: cook / workman / gardener / turn down; unanswered is a no; a gang the warden never knew of is not in the text). Never a guard, warden or keys role.
**Hire**: `StaffUtility.Hire(map, role, free, candidate, record)` takes the world pawn (out of the world, the player's faction, the usual fee), `integrity = RollIntegrity + IntegrityShift(past)` (clean +8 or -4 per incident up to 3, program +6, trade +4, gang -12 and -6 per rank, dealer -6, ring -4); friends inside get
`RP_FriendHired`; the staff file says "former inmate, served N days". A hired gang member's old gang may lean on them (`ExInmates.Pressure`, daily 5%): `CorruptionSystem.Approach` with +0.25 on the odds (hidden, as corruption is). **Visit**: `ExInmates.HourlyVisits` after the family visits in `VisitationSystem.HourlyTick`
(2% an hour per kept former inmate with a friend inside who `CanBeVisited`, not within 3 days of release, 5 days between visits): `SpawnVisitor(..., former)` spawns the kept pawn as the visitor (spawning drops the keep, so `KeepWhileOut` puts it back; `ExitMap` returns them to the world, still kept), `CompInmate.visitorFormer`,
searched and counted like any visit, no family money and no family news, contraband chance x2.5 for a dealer or a ring member (`ExInmates.SmuggleChance`).

### Kin across the wall
`Story/Kin.cs`, `CompStaff.kinAsked/kinKeepOff`, `Defs/Kin.xml` (`RP_Decision_Kin`), `Keyed/Kin.xml`. About one generated hire in twelve (`StaffUtility.Hire`: not free, not a register candidate) is kin to an inmate inside, about one arrival in twelve (`IntakeSystem.AdmitArrival`, the transfer request) is kin to a member of staff, and one in thirty
to an inmate inside (`Kin.OnHired`, `Kin.OnArrived`; `HireChance`, `ArrivalChance`, `InmateKinChance`). Made by hand after generation (`Kin.TryMake`, `Kin.Link`; never the generator's relations), as vanilla relations: in 1.7 only `Parent` is direct (Child, Sibling, HalfSibling and Cousin are
implied by it), so a parent/child is one relation, siblings share a generated mother and father and half-siblings one (world pawns, `MakeParent`, ages 16 to 45 years older than the children, forcefully kept and listed in `StorySystem.kinParents` while a child of theirs is on the map, at home or kept as a former inmate: the world-pawn GC seeds only from world pawns, so it
would discard parents whose children are all on the map and `Pawn.Discard` clears the relations with them; `Kin.ReleaseParents`, daily, lets them go when no child is left), and cousins (four hidden pawns)
are not made. Fits by age (`Kin.Fits`: parent 16 to 45 years older, siblings within 15, half-siblings within 20) **with no chains**: a link is only made between two pawns who have no parents and no children yet, so vanilla never derives a grandparent, an uncle, a niece or a cousin from it, and inmate-to-inmate kin is siblings only (`TryMake`'s `only`); weights sibling 3, parent and child 1.5 each, half-sibling 1. Never the warden, the state's posted chief, a callout officer or a visitor
(`Kin.EligibleStaff`); an inmate only when active. Public: `Kin.Lines` ("brother of guard Hale") in the inmate file and `CompStaff.StatusLines`. **The request**: a guard with kin inside is asked once (`kinAsked`, `Kin.AskIfDue`, at the hire or arrival and by `Kin.Daily` for any guard never asked) to be kept off the
wing their kin is in or left on it (`KinDecision`; unanswered = left on it). Kept off (`kinKeepOff`): `Kin.PatrolAllowed` filters the zones offered in the three patrol-zone menus, `WorkGiver_Patrol` skips inmates standing in those wings and ignores a deployed zone that has become one,
`GuardPosts.KeptOff` keeps them off a post in them (`CanTake`, `FreeAndAble`, `NamedElsewhere`), `Kin.Daily` clears a patrol zone that became the kin's wing. Shifts are by time, not place, so there is nothing to do there. **Effects on hooks that were there**: `ComplaintSystem.Evidence` +0.15 for a true
complaint against an officer who is kin of the complainant's enemy (a motive), -0.15 for one against the complainant's own kin, a line in what was looked at either way (`Kin.ComplaintShift`); `CorruptionSystem.OddsFor` +0.15 on the odds when the fixer, or their gang or ring, holds the officer's kin (`Kin.Leverage`,
inside `Approach`); `GuardUtility.CanRespondTo` (the subdue work giver and the alarm) is false for a guard whose own kin it is in a share `HesitateChance` (0.75) of 250-tick windows, seeded, when another guard on duty who is no kin can go (`Kin.AnotherGuardCanGo`: reachable, and one who could take the job themselves, `Kin.CouldTakeResponse`: Warden work on, the work giver's required capacities (hands), not barred from violence), never for an order from the warden (`Kin.Hesitates`); deaths
(`DeathAftermath`, `StaffDeaths`, `DeathRowSystem.Execute`): `Kin.Notify_Died` writes the colleague line ("guard Hale's brother Jones died inside", "was executed", "of the staff, died on duty"); vanilla's family thoughts come with the relation (`everSeenByPlayer`), and a staff member's mental break that night is
not barred (only a run into the wild is, as for all staff). A child counts as still here for `Kin.ReleaseParents` also while carried on the map (`MapHeld`). **Scope**: the request letter goes to guards only (keeping off a wing only changes patrols, their target cells and posts: a cook, a doctor or a janitor with kin inside is not asked); a guard may still carry out the execution of their own kin (a possible later letter). **Tests**: in an autotest run the odds are zero (`Kin.Chance`): the plans' own hires and arrivals are made kin to nobody (determinism), unless a check forces the roll (`Kin.ChanceOverride`, `Kin.PoolOverride`); the real-hire check fixes the generated candidate's age (`StaffUtility.CandidateChronologicalAge`) so the pair is known, and a second hire of 330 years chronological (cryptosleep) is refused.

### The roster
`Staff/Roster.cs`, `Staff/Shifts.cs`, `CompStaff.shiftPin/nightsRun/lastNightDay`, `PrisonGame.handoverHours/overtimeOwed/overtimeHours`, `Defs/Roster.xml`, `Keyed/Roster.xml`, the Staff tab (`UI/StaffTab.cs`) and the Deployment tab. **Pins**: a guard on the roster (`Roster.OnRoster`: guard duties, not the warden, not riot police) is pinned to auto, days or nights (`ShiftPin`, a click on the Staff tab's "pin" column,
`Roster.SetPin` asks for a balance at the next hour like the policy checkbox). `ShiftSystem.Rebalance` puts a pinned guard where the pin says and counts a guard pinned to nights as one of the third; the rest of the third (`max(1, all / 3)`, none under two guards, as before) is filled from the unpinned by `thingIDNumber`, so with no pin it is the old balance and an old save behaves as before.
All pinned to days is no night guard (what the checkbox already allows). **The shift policy off beats a pin**: nobody works nights then, pinned or not (the tab says so). A doctor, cook or the warden has no pin (`ShiftSystem.PinOf`: only a guard's counts; doctors keep the one-third rule). Between balances the hourly pass applies a pin at once, also to a guard on leave (`Pinned`: a pin never flaps), so a pin set in the tab is in the timetable at
the next hour. Every flip goes through `ShiftSystem.SetShift`. **The handover** (`PrisonGame.handoverHours`, 0-3, a - / + on the Staff tab): a day guard (`ShiftSystem.StaysForHandover`: guard duties, day shift, permanent) works that many hours past 22:00 (`DayShiftEnd`): `Apply` writes Work in those hours (so a post is manned and `GuardPosts.OnDuty` holds) and `StaffCommute.ShouldBeHome` reads
`ShiftSystem.InHandover` as well (so the change shows before the hour's timetable pass), after the suspension and leave tests and before the timetable. The warden, a night guard, riot police and the non-guards are never kept. **Overtime** is paid for the hours worked, accrued as they are worked: at each hourly pass (`PrisonGame.Notify_HourChanged`, after the commute) in a handover hour (`ShiftSystem.AccrueOvertime`), each day guard who `ShiftSystem.WorksHandoverHour` (on site, in the handover, available, off the commute's `ShouldBeHome`, not a hostage) earns `StaffCareers.OvertimeHour` (`ceil(wage x 1.5 / 16)`),
added to one saved total (`PrisonGame.overtimeOwed`, with the guard-hours in `overtimeHours` for the ledger note; one total rather than one per guard, so a guard fired or sent home before midnight is still paid for their hours). `DailySettlement.PayWages` pays it as a line of its own on the wages account beside the wages and clears it, so the handover turned off at 23:59 still pays what was worked and turned on at 23:59 charges nothing. The hour is paid as it begins; the hour-0 pass of a three hour handover falls
either side of the settlement and is paid by it or the next one. `StaffCareers.Overtime/OvertimePay/Payroll` stay as the forecast (the alert, the land purchase test, the Staff header and the handover's tooltip read it: the cost of the handover as it is set now). **Thoughts**: `ShiftSystem.Daily` (the settlement, after the corruption pass) counts a night guard's nights in a row (`nightsRun`) for the guards who are available: a night counts only if it follows the last one worked (`CompStaff.lastNightDay`, a day number), so leave, a suspension, a day off by a pin or the policy breaks the run and it begins again at one; from the seventh (`Roster.NightsAgainAfter`) "nights again" (-5, two days, kept fresh each night); taken off nights
after a run of seven or more that was current (`ShiftSystem.NightRun`: the last night was last night or tonight's, so a stale run left over from a leave gives nothing; `SetShift`, by the balance or a pin) "a fair rota" (+5, five days) and the run begins again; a day guard's run is 0. **The strip**: `Roster.Strip(map)` is the guards SCHEDULED on duty in each of the 24 hours, the head count of `StaffCommute.ShouldBeHome` over the timetables (a guard on the roster who is not to be home in that hour: a hostage held is on site and counts, a suspended guard or one on leave is in no hour). It is a schedule, not presence (a forecast for the hours to come can be nothing else, and the posts' hours are read from it), and the tabs say so ("scheduled"); the commute keeps one member of staff on site
overnight whatever the timetables say, and `Roster.KeptOnSite` (`StaffCommute.OffShiftOnSite`: spawned, off shift by `ShouldBeHome`, not on the way home; worked out once in 250 ticks and when somebody goes home or comes back) is shown beside it in the Staff tab's heading and the strip's tooltip. The strip is worked out once an hour and again on `Roster.Invalidate()` (`Roster.Version`) or a change in the key (the staff version and headcount, the handover, the policy): called at `CorruptionSystem.SendHome` (suspension, discipline, stand-down) and when
one of them runs out, at a leave starting (`StaffLives`, `StaffTraining.Send`) and ending (`StaffCommute.Arrive`, `StaffTraining.CheckInHouse`), a hostage taken and freed (`HostageCrisis`), a pin (`Roster.SetPin`), the handover buttons and a write to a member of staff's timetable (`ShiftSystem.Apply` once for each pass that changed one, `ShiftSystem.Balance` at the end of the hourly pass, and a Harmony postfix on `Pawn_TimetableTracker.SetAssignment`, which vanilla calls only from the Schedule tab's cells, and on the tab's paste button (`PawnColumnWorker_CopyPasteTimetable.PasteTo`, which writes the hours directly), for the warden's hand: the prison's own writes hold `ShiftSystem.Writing` so that the postfix leaves them to the one bump). A suspension, stand-down, discipline or leave also ends at a tick with no hook (a guard at home runs no corruption tick, and a leave is cleared on arrival at the next hour): `Roster.Strip` keeps the earliest pending deadline of those among the guards on the roster (`nextExpiry`) and works the strip out again once the clock is there. Never per frame (`Roster.Computations` counts it for the check). The strip's text (the counts, the axis, the hour names and the 24 bar tooltips, built on hover through the `TipRegion(Rect, Func<string>, int)` overload) is kept with the counts and the language; `Roster.DrawStrip` draws it on the Staff tab (with the split of nights, days and pinned) and in the Deployment tab above the shuttle pad's row, and `Roster.Fewest` gives each guard post's row "fewest on duty" over its hours. **Where the brief and the code differ**: the day shift and the night shift
already overlap four hours at each end (nights start at 18:00 and end at 10:00, days run 06:00 to 22:00), so the handover is an extension past the day shift's end, not a gap closed; `CorruptionSystem.ShiftStartHour` reads `nightShift` and needed no change, and the complaint odds (`Story/Complaints.cs`) do not read the shift at all: they read who is in sight, so the night guard's lone rounds are where the dark and the missing colleague already count. Not done: the doctors' shift has no pin or handover, the order of the unpinned is still by `thingIDNumber` (no rotation: the pin is the lever, and "nights again" its reason).

### Staff agreements
`Staff/Agreements.cs` (`StaffAgreements`, `StaffAgreementBook` on `PrisonGame.agreements`, `StaffGrievance`), `Events/Unrest.cs` (`IncidentWorker_StaffStrike`), `PrisonDecisionWorker_StaffStrike` in `Events/Events.cs`, `StaffCommute.GuardsOnDuty`, `StaffCareers.WageAt`, `DailySettlement.PayWages`, `StaffTab`, `Defs/Agreements.xml`, `Keyed/Agreements.xml`. The staff's demand letter has a third answer, **agree to fix it** (the bonus, agree, refuse; no answer is the last, the walk-out, as before), with a term per grievance (`IncidentWorker_StaffStrike.Grievance`, the same four tests in the same order as the old `Cause`, saved in the letter's `note`):
**understaffing** - in each hour of the day, at least one more guard on duty than the roster had in that hour when the letter was written (a per-hour `int[24]` copy of `Roster.Strip`, taken once in `TryExecuteWorker`, carried in the letter's `note` as `Understaffed:2,2,...` and handed to `Agree`, so what the warden agrees to is what the letter said however long they take), in at least 80% of the 120 hours of the next 5 days; **nights** - the grievance itself: an hour counts when `IncidentWorker_StaffStrike.TooManyOnNights` (more than half of the employed staff on the night shift - the roster's `nightShift` of everyone on the payroll, at home or on the map, not who is spawned at that hour: the day staff are home at night - the test that sent the letter) no longer holds, 80% of the 120 hourly looks (adding a night guard does not meet it, moving guards to days or hiring day staff does; the letter says so); **safety** - no member of staff hurt by an inmate (`Patch_Pawn_PostApplyDamage_Wants` tells `Notify_StaffHurt`; broken at the next hourly pass, not at the deadline, and a blow after the deadline is not in the term) - **cheap but risky by design**: agreeing costs nothing, a kept promise buys 15 days of peace and a good memory, a broken one costs the bad memory and the bonus; **pay** - a 10% rise in `StaffCareers.WageAt` for the representative's role (one rise per role: `Agree` refuses while the role has one), so every settlement pays it, five of them keep the promise, and the rise then stays until the warden takes it back on the Staff tab. Taking it back whenever it is ends the peace over pay, and within `TakeBackDays` (the term + the peace, 5 + 15 days) of agreeing it breaks the promise (the record turns to broken, the bad memory, no bonus next time): the rise cannot be had for the term and dropped for the peace.
Cover is **measured, not forecast**: at each hourly pass (`PrisonGame.Notify_HourChanged`, right after `StaffCommute.Hourly`, so those who came in are on site and those sent home are not) `StaffCommute.GuardsOnDuty` counts the guards on the roster who are spawned, not on the way home, not held hostage, not striking and not off shift by `ShouldBeHome` (the off-shift one the commute keeps on site is not cover), and the hour counts if it meets the target; the pass counts an hour once (`lastHourStamp`) and a cover added in the last hour is one hour of 120; a guard who is down or in a mental break is not cover. Judged at the deadline (needed = ceil(80% of the term's hours)): kept - the "kept their word" memory (+6, 8 days) for the role's staff, no demand over that grievance for 15 days (`peaceUntil`, saved per grievance); broken - "a broken promise" (-10, 12 days), the peace cleared and no bonus in the next letter over that grievance (`BrokenBefore`: the last record of it is a broken one - until one is kept), the other grievances untouched.
**One at a time** (`Agree` is disabled in the letter while one runs, with the reason; a demand over a grievance whose promise runs or whose peace lasts is not sent at all). The **representative** is the most senior (rank, then service) of the grieving role - the guards for the first three, the role with the lowest mood for pay - never a state appointee or the warden (`CompStaff.Protected`), and is named in the letter, the promise and the panel; if they leave the promise stands under the name saved (`repName`). `StaffAgreementBook` also keeps the last 10 records (grievance, outcome, day), the rises that stand and the last hour measured; a save from before has none of it (all defaults). Existing strike timers (`strikeUntilTick`, `Strike`, the job filter) are not touched: a strike is not reset by a hire, a role change or a load.
The Staff tab shows a line for the running promise (grievance, representative, the term, the compliant hours so far / needed, days left) and one for each rise that stands with "take back the rise" (a confirmation when it would break the promise); the outcome is a message and a chronicle line. **Where the brief and the code differ**: the demand letter named no representative, so this feature picks one (most senior of the grieving role); "grievance Def" is an enum (`Unrest.cs` never had a def); there was no staff refusal memory to be stronger than (a refusal is the strike), so the broken-promise memory is compared with the kept-word one; the brief's "agreed care detail with strike job filtering" (minimum cover during a strike as a term) is not built - the decisions list the four terms only. **Known edge**: the understaffing term is per hour, so one more guard on each shift is what meets it (a day guard alone does nothing for the night hours); the baseline counts the roster's schedule (`Roster.Strip`) and the measure the commute's on-site count, so a guard who is on the roster but not on site is a hole against it, as it should be. A striking guard is not cover: a refused letter's 12-hour strike eats 12 of the 24 hours the promise can miss, if one runs at the time. While a grievance is in its peace or its promise runs, `Grievance` still returns the first matching one, so no demand of another kind comes while that one is the top of the list.

### The count
`Security/Count.cs`, `Defs/Count.xml`, `Keyed/Count.xml`, `PrisonMap.count` (`CountState`), `PrisonGame.countsPerDay`, `CompInmate.lastCountedTick`, `Fugitive.headStart`, `MisconductDef.mark`; hooks in `Security/Escape.cs`, `Security/Intel.cs`, `Shuttle/ShuttleSystem.Stories.cs`, `Security/Misconduct.cs`, `Security/Fugitives.cs`, `Security/GuardAI.cs`, `UI/PrisonHUD.cs`, `UI/Alerts.cs`, `UI/PolicyTab.cs`.
**Policy**: `PrisonGame.countsPerDay` 0-4 (default 2, saved with the default, the Policy tab's "Counts a day" row cycles it and shows the next count's hour). 0 is off: an escape is known the moment it happens, as before the count. The hours (`CountSystem.Hours`) are the lockup hour of the default security level's regime (`LockupHour`: the lockup that leads into the night's sleep: from noon round the first sleep slot, and back over the lockup slots before it, so the default regime's evening lockup at 22:00 and not its midday one; the sleep hour itself when nothing locks up before it; 22:00 if there is no sleep) and then 06:00, 14:00 and 02:00 as more are asked for; the hour is the regime's own wherever the warden puts it.
**The walk**: at a count hour `PrisonMap.OnHourChanged` calls `CountSystem.Hourly`: a count begins (`CountState.startedTick`, `wingsLeft`: every wing, `WardenSystem.Wings`, that has an active inmate in it or a hidden runaway of it); the inmates are given `GraceTicks` (600) to settle, then `WorkGiver_Count` (Warden work type, priority 100: over a post and the patrol, under the escorts and pad duty) hands a guard the first wing nobody is on by `CountSystem.JobFor`: a guard who is a guard (`GuardUtility.IsGuard`), on duty (`GuardPosts.OnDuty`), not on a post, a tower or pad duty (`CountSystem.HoldsAPost`), with no alarm to answer, not kept off the wing (`Kin`) nor deployed to another. `JobDriver_Count` walks the stops of the warden's `WalkPlan` (a pause at each) and counts the wing when the last is done or half an hour (`WingTicks`) is up; an interrupted walk leaves the wing for the next guard, and an alarm the guard would be sent to (`GuardUtility.AlarmFor`, looked at every 30 ticks) ends the walk (`InterruptOptional`: the guard answers it, the wing waits). A count called while the one before still runs (a regime with two count hours side by side) finishes it as it stands (the wings not walked are missed, with the line) and begins clean: it is never overwritten. A patrol breaks off for a wing that wants a guard (`CountSystem.WantsGuard`, as for a post). A wing no guard took within the hour (`WindowTicks`) is missed: `Finish` writes a line in the chronicle and a message and sets `lastMissedTick`; a count nobody walked at all is a miss and clears nothing, so the runaway stays unknown to the next one (the risk). No guard employed at all: no count is called and nothing is held back (the alert for no guards is the news).
**Who is accounted for** (`CountSystem.Accounted`): in their cell, the hole, a medical bed, holding, a hearing, a class, a job they are allowed, a visit, on an escort (a supermax leg, a parole escort, an arrival, on the way out) or carried by staff, hurt or in a mental state, or where the regime wants them now or wanted them the slot before (`RegimePlacement.InExpectedPlace`: an inmate still settling at the hour is not written up). Walking (a walk that is no wander), held by staff (the `Wait_MaintainPosture` of a session, a search or a frisk), at prayer or at the commissary is in transit or in hand (`InTransitOrInHand`), not out of place. Anyone else of the wing on the map is written up: `RP_OutOfPlace`, severity 1, no punishment hours (nothing is imposed unless the warden sets some), recorded as evidence (so seen). It is a *mark* (`MisconductDef.mark`): it never pushes an older record out of a full file (it replaces the oldest mark, or is not written), restarts no remission or supermax clean days, bars no mentor, is not the offence a restraint was for (`PolicyHoursForLatest`), is left out of the weekly report, and is not counted in the statistics or on the desk board. Every inmate on the wing is counted (`CompInmate.lastCountedTick`).
**Escapes nobody saw**: `CountSystem.StartEscaping` replaces the four places that start `RP_Escaping` (a breach walk-off, the tunnel, the escape-attempt misconduct, a stowaway): with counts on, a guard employed, the runner not in a mental state and nothing that watches in sight (`TensionUtility.SeenAt`, the one seen rule) the state is started silently (`transitionSilently`: no begin letter) and a `HiddenEscape` is kept on `PrisonMap.count.hidden` (way, start tick, where they were, the wing's level). A hidden runner is left out of the escape alert (`CountSystem.IsHidden`) and is not trouble to the guards (`GuardUtility.ActingOut`) nor a target of the armed response (`ArmedResponse.ShouldShoot`) until seen; every 250 ticks (`CountSystem.Tick`) one who is seen is told (the begin letter, or the tunnel's, or the stowaway's message that was held back) and one who has given up or been caught is forgotten. At the edge (`Patch_Pawn_ExitMap_Escape` → `CountSystem.Notify_Exiting`) a runner still unseen is gone with the books shut: no fine, no `stats.escaped`, no letter, no chronicle line, no fugitive record (all of that is `EscapeUtility.Register`, moved out of the patch unchanged and called at once for an escape that was seen); the world keeps the pawn (`ForcefullyKeptPawns`). The count of their wing (`WingCounted`, or `Finish` for a wing with no cell left) finds the cell empty (a runner still on the map then is a sighting, not unaccounted): `Reveal` writes the unaccounted entry (name, where, when), calls `Register` with the escape's own letter and chronicle line replaced by the count's ("Short at the count", one line), and the police are told with the time the wait cost them. Seen, or with the count off (or no guard), an escape is as before. A guard stopping a stowaway at the ramp (`CountSystem.Forget`) is a sighting. A hidden runner's own record of the escape attempt (`MisconductUtility.Record` with `CountSystem.IsHidden`, no guard's evidence) is written unseen and tells nothing: no message, no tick on the desk board. A stowaway for whom boarding closed (`StowawayGoal` cleared them) goes the ordinary way and is held as `Walked`, not as a stowaway.
**The head start**: `FugitiveSystem.Notify_Escaped(p, game, escapedTick)` reads the hours since the escape: the odds of the police getting the runaway back are `RecaptureChance` (0.6) with no head start and `CountSystem.RecaptureChanceAfter` less each hour (`HeadStartPenaltyPerHour` 0.02, never under a quarter of them); six hours is 0.528. The record carries `headStart` and the tick they got away (the time on the run still does not count as served), and the search (the return, one to four days) runs from when the police are told. **Where the description and the code differ**: the code had no head start (a flat 0.6, rolled at the escape; there is no `escapeKnown` on the record) and a record only for the 60% the police would find, so an unseen escape is held in `PrisonMap.count.hidden` (every unseen escape, found or not) and the fugitive record is made when the police are told, with the odds read from the head start then; the begin letters of the escape (`RP_Escaping`'s own) and the stowaway's message were also told at once and are held with it.
**Screens**: the HUD has a row for the count (`CountSystem.HudLine`: "count: 2 unaccounted", "count missed 06:00", "counting - 2 wing(s) to go", "count cleared 22:10", "next count 22:00", "counts off", in that order of rank: trouble comes first, the unaccounted outranking a missed count, and a count running is added after it ("count: 2 unaccounted, counting - 1 to go") rather than hiding it, since a warden needs the missing names before the progress of the next count; alone it ranks above cleared and next; its tooltip lists who is missing and where they were last seen), `Alert_RP_Unaccounted` (named apart from `Alert_RP_Missing`, which is about missing rooms) lists the same names (`CountSystem.UnaccountedLines`, which leaves out one who is back on the map), the Policy tab's row cycles counts a day. **Saved**: `PrisonMap.count` (`lastClearedTick`, `lastMissedTick`, `startedTick`, `dueTick`, `wingsLeft`, `walked`, `writeUps`, `unaccounted`, `hidden`), `PrisonGame.countsPerDay`, `CompInmate.lastCountedTick`, `Fugitive.headStart`; all with defaults, so an old save loads with the default of two a day and nothing behind it; in the saveload snapshot (`count=`, the inmate's `lc=`) and seeded off their defaults. **Tests**: under `AutoTest.Active` the count is off (`CountSystem.Enabled`) unless a check sets `CountSystem.TestOn`, so every older escape and stowaway check keeps its letter at once; `CountChecks.cs` (events) runs on the dark checks' scene.

### Men and women apart
`Inmates/Cellmates.cs`, `Intake/Intake.cs`, `PrisonGame.wingGenders`. Men and women are kept apart two ways. *In the cell*, always: a man and a woman under one roof is a term of the cellmate fit (`Cellmates.MixedCell`, -10, on the pair, as bad as rival gangs), so the cell block calls such a
cell tense, `ReviewWould` (below the review's -8) moves one of them when the daily review finds a clearly better bed (two a day, as ever), and a newcomer fits a cell of their own sex better. Where nothing else is free they still go in (kept apart where there is a choice). *By the
wing*, if the warden says (Intake tab, a button on each security level's row: all inmates, men only, women only; the default is all; `WingGender`, in `Intake/Intake.cs`):

- **The policy is a promise to the warden.** Whatever puts an inmate in a wing honours it or tells the warden plainly why it could not (a message naming the inmate and the wing, `RP_WingKeptForOther` and its kin), and never silently fails or silently breaks it. A wing kept for one sex offers none of its
  beds to the other (`IntakeSystem.WingAllows`/`BedAllows`, `FreePrisonerBeds(gender)`, `FreeBedCount(gender)`; `Cellmates.Fit` of such a bed is minus a thousand, so `PickBed`, `BestFree`, `Move` and the move menu skip it). A bed in no wing takes anyone. There is one wing for each level, so
  "men only" on a row does not split the wing: every inmate of the other sex at that level may live only in beds of no wing.
- **Who puts people in a wing, and what each does.** *The shuttle's roster* (`IntakeSystem.GenerateRoster`): with beds free in the level's wings for one sex only, whoever the state sends is of that sex (`SexWithABed`); with none free (overcrowding allowed) a single-sex level takes only that sex (`PolicySex`),
  so nobody arrives at a wing that will never take them. Passengers a shuttle could not unload (`carriedOver`) come first, but only those a bed of theirs, or overcrowding, can take now; one who cannot waits and uses up none of the day's quota. One of a sex no bed of their level will take at all
  (`IntakeSystem.LevelTakes`: the wing is kept for the other sex and there is no bed in no wing) waits a day (`CompInmate.carriedSkipDay`: the warden may set the wing back) and the next roster sends them to another prison, with a message (`RP_CarriedSentElsewhere`).
  The marshals' prisoners are queued only when there is a bed of their sex, and under overcrowding come only to a level that takes their sex. *An arrival* (`AdmitArrival`) takes a bed of their sex; with none the warden is told (`RP_ArrivalNoBedForSex`), and the hourly `TryReassignBed` asks for no list of beds when none is
  of their sex. *A reclassification* (protective custody, promotion and demotion, the trial's move to maximum): `InmateUtility.HasRoomIn` counts beds of their sex, the letters and buttons say why there is no room (`NoRoomText`), `ChangeSecurity` refuses and tells the warden when no bed of theirs is free in a wing kept for
  the other sex (it returns false: nobody is reclassified in their old cell with no way into the wing), and the paths that only skip when there is no room tell the warden by name when that is the reason (`TellIfWingKeepsThemOut`); a commuted death sentence cannot wait, so it is reclassified all the same (`onPaper`), the warden is told
  they stay where they are, and the hourly pass moves them when a bed of theirs is free. *The events* (the celebrity, the state's transfer request) generate the sex there are beds for and take the best bed of theirs; the guard who carries a downed inmate to a bed, and the debtor's move away from a creditor, ask for beds of their sex.
- **Changing a full mixed wing to one sex.** The other sex already in it is the daily review's to move out over the next days, wherever a bed of theirs is free (first free bed of theirs, unless the warden placed them): up to `Cellmates.WrongWingMovesPerDay` (4) a day, a quota of their own (the two moves a day for the cells with
  rival gangs or a man and a woman are not used up by a stranded backlog, which sorted first at minus a thousand). With no free bed of theirs they stay where they are; the Intake tab says how many live in a wing kept for the other sex and how many of them have nowhere to go yet (`Cellmates.InWrongWing`).

### Keep-apart orders
`Inmates/KeepApart.cs`, `PrisonGame.keepApart`, `UI/KeepApartUI.cs`, `Keyed/KeepApart.xml`. The warden's list of inmates who are not to be put together (gap analysis 6, 1). `KeepApartOrder` (its own `IExposable`: two thing ids and names, a reason key, the tick) in a `KeepApartBook` (its own save block `keepApart` on `PrisonGame`, not a field of `StorySystem`; `??=` on load for an old save), capped at 60 (`KeepApartBook.Cap`; a 61st is refused with a message). Two lookups by thing id are rebuilt after any change (the pair as a `long` key, a pawn's orders), so the pair test in the placement is a hash lookup and never a list walk (`Cellmates.DailyReview` is O(n squared) already). The pawns are not saved: `EnsureResolved` finds them once per change with one pass over the prisoners. Written by the warden (the file's "Keep apart..." window, and a button on Intel's feud, trial and debt rows), or by a letter (reason "trial", "debt", "gang") or a granted cell request (reason "request"). **Never read by compliance or the door verdict**: `RegimePlacement` is untouched (an inmate in a patch with the other counts as in place, or the "order" grade would move), and `CompPrisonDoor` (path threads) never sees it.

What the placement does with it, each where it chooses:
- **Cells** (`Cellmates`): `PairScore` gets `KeepApart.SameCellTerm` (-1000, `ExcludedFit`'s) for the other in a cell, so `Fit`, `PickBed`, `BestFree`, `ClashesIn` (and the grade move that reads them) skip it; `Fit` gets `KeepApart.WingTerm` (-30 for each partner living in the bed's wing, more than the wing's own +20 and the review's `BetterBy` together) so a bed in another wing, or in none, is the better one. `Move` refuses a bed in the other's cell (so does the warden's move menu, which shows that cell greyed). A newcomer for whom nothing else is free is still placed (the term is a score, as the other sex's wing is: no bedless inmate), and the line says so. `ReviewWould` is true for an inmate who shares a cell or a wing with a partner (`KeepApart.Violation`), unless the warden placed one of them after the order was written (the later word); `DailyReview` moves one of the pair to a bed `BestFree` finds, under the same two-a-day quota, with a chronicle line saying whom it was for and whether cell or wing. A wing is the bed's zone security (`IntakeSystem.WingOf`): a bed in no wing is no one's wing, so a prison with no wing painted cannot share one and only the cell is kept apart.
- **Yards and canteens** (`KeptRooms.PickCell`): the patches a partner stands in, or is walking to, are tried last of all (after the loose cells); one patch lookup for each of the pawn's orders, no scan. When it is the only patch, it is the pick.
- **Classes** (`ProgramSystem.TryFindVenue`): the cohort drops one of a pair (`KeepApart.Cohort`, the same list when no order matters): the one who `KeepApart.Precedes` the other that day keeps the seat (a hash of thing id and day, so it changes from day to day and from pair to pair) and the other waits for another class.
- **Work** (`LaborUtility.AssignJobs`): two of a pair never hold jobs of the same zone (workshop and tailoring share one, the kitchen, the laundry, ...); of an unlocked pair the one who `Precedes` the other that day keeps theirs, a job the warden locked is kept and the other gives theirs up, two locked jobs stay and the line says so. Jobs are held for good here, not by the hour, so "not the same hour" is "not the same room", and the room is shared out by rotation (`LaborUtility.RotatePairs`, after the separation, every hourly pass): when one holds a job in a zone and the other, who goes first today, has no job at all and could do it (eligible, not kept out by another order), the holder's job is handed to them at once and the holder is given another below if one is free - so a pair left with one room between them have it in turn. Two who each have a job are left as they are (they are in different rooms already; nobody loses a job to the rotation), an inmate kept apart from several takes one handover a pass (they hold a job after the first), and a locked job is never taken; at most one handover per order per day (`KeepApartOrder.handoverDay`, not saved).
- **Letters**: the trial letter's `RP_TrialCase_Apart` (a flag, `Trials.CaseApart`, so a letter from before it offers what it did; orders for each known inmate witness against the accused), the green-light letter's `RP_LeftGang_Apart` (against `GangExit.Enforcer`; "do nothing" is now the last option and the timeout's), the debt letter's `RP_Debt_Apart` (the debt and its schedule stay as they are; "let it run" is now last and the timeout's); a cell request granted for a conflict (not "in with a friend") writes an order.

What it says when it cannot be honoured: the file (`KeepApart.FileLines`, in `StatusLines`) lists each order with its reason and since when, and Intel has a "Kept apart" section with the same line, a Lift button and the trouble lines of `KeepApart.TroubleLines`: no bed on another wing or in another cell for the second party (worked out hourly and when the order is written, from the free beds as they are, unshuffled so no random draw is spent), both in one patch of a yard or canteen at an hour (the last hourly sample of today), both on a job in one room. **Daily** (`KeepApart.Daily`, before the review): an order whose party has left (released, transferred, dead) is dropped. In the saveload plan's snapshot (`keepapart=`). `KeepApart.TermsOff` (the placement ignores the orders) and `KeepApart.TestDay` (the day the daily priority and the rotation go by) are the checks' switches only.

### Tasking an informant (`Security/InformantTask.cs`, `Security/Intel.cs`, `Keyed/InformantTask.xml`; gap analysis 7, item 3)
A known informant's file (`InformantTask.CanBeTasked`: known as a snitch, not burned, not quit, not on the way out) has "Task...": a menu of the prison's inmates (`InformantTask.Targets`), every suspect first with what the desk knows of them (flagged, a search due, a tunnel known, a known gang, a link of a ring found, a known debt), then the rest nearest first, 40 at most; the present target is left out and "pull out" leads the menu of a tasked informant. One target per informant (`CompInmate.taskTargetId`, -1); choosing one replaces the old and starts the days in place again (`taskSinceTick`). Choosing it opens the second step, never forced: into the target's cell, onto the target's wing (the best fit of its free beds, as `BestFree` picks), or where they are. Each move on offer names the cell and the move menu's verdict on it (good, tense, rivals: `Cellmates.Verdict`, a hidden gang shows only as tension). Both moves go through `Cellmates.MoveByWarden` (so `Move`, and the warden's five-day hold on the placement) and are greyed with the reason when they cannot be done: a keep-apart order between the two (cell and wing), or with someone who lives in the target's cell (the cell move, naming them: `KeepApart.PartnerIn`, which `Move` would refuse), the same cell or wing already, no free bed there that the informant's level and sex may have, death row.

- **In place** (`InformantTask.InReach`, `InformantTask.Hourly`): the days in place count only while the informant is in reach of the target, as the tip's reach is: within 30 cells, or in the same cell or wing. Tasked in reach, they start at once; tasked out of reach, the task waits ("watching X - not near them yet", no risk) and the days start the hour they first are (or at once after the move). Each hour out of reach (or with the target out of sight) comes off the days (the start moves on by the hour, never past now). So "leave them where they are" for a target on another wing risks nothing and gains nothing.
- **The tip** (`InformantSystem.TryTip`): a tasked informant whose target is in reach is asked about the target first, before the staff gossip and the rest: a tunnel, contraband, a gang (half the time), a ring the target deals or carries for (`RingSystem.TryTip` with the target alone: it tells the ring's next link, who need not be the target; a ring with the target and another member nearby is rolled for twice, here and with the rest - accepted), the target's debt (`DebtSystem.TryTip(..., own: false)`: never one of the informant's own, not even a debt they owe the target); then as anyone is. The daily 45% roll and the walk's tip are as they were.
- **Exposure**: the term is 3 points a day in place (6 when someone else of the target's gang lives on the target's wing: the bed's zone, or the same level for a bed in no wing), at most 60 (`InformantTask.Term`). A tip acted on (`Notify_TipActedOn`) gives the informant away at 30% as before, plus the term when the tip was about their target (at most 95%). Each day in place a quarter of the term is rolled on its own (`InformantTask.Daily`, before the tips: day 6 4.5%, 9% with the gang), on a day the informant is in reach of the target. Either way it is the existing exposure (`InformantSystem.Expose`: exposed, afraid, the target and their gang hate the snitch and wind up, a threat letter, a chronicle line), with its own letter text when they were made in place; the task ends. The other ways an informant is burned (testifying at a trial, a ring working out its turned courier) end the task too.
- **Pulling out** (`InformantTask.End(..., remember: true)`): the target stays wary of the informant as long as the days in place were (it fades at the rate it grew). Pointed at the same inmate again within that, the informant picks up what is left (the days less the time since the pull-out), so two clicks do not wipe the risk. Kept in the same two fields (`taskTargetId` = -2 - the target, `taskSinceTick` = the tick the wariness is gone), forgotten by the daily pass once it is gone, when the target leaves, and with a new stay. Pointing the informant at someone else starts the days again, as the owner's rule says, and forgets the wariness of the first: going through another target and back is a way round it, accepted (there is no third field).
- **A wrong lead**: an informant whose trust in the warden in post is at or under the mark of hatred (`StaffMemories.HatedAt`, -40) feeds one 30% of the times they are asked about the target: "{target} is carrying contraband", the words of a true tip, for a target who carries none and is not flagged. It costs what a true contraband tip costs: it counts in the tips, the target is flagged (`suspected`, `informedBy`) and so is due a targeted drug test until searched, and the frisk is a frisk (a grudge-holder's false complaint after a search, the gang mark seen). The frisk finds nothing and clears the flag; with nothing found, nobody is given away. A target who picks up contraband before the frisk turns it into a hit, and the exposure roll with it.
- **Screens**: the file's status line and a row under the informant on Intel ("watching X - N days in place, exposure risk low / rising / high": under 15, under 35, from there), with "Pull out" on Intel. The band is the desk's view: it counts the target's gang on the wing only when the warden knows the gang of both the target and the other member (`GangOnWing(..., known: true)`, worked out every 250 ticks); the dice use every gang, and the tooltip says so. Otherwise the colour would tell the warden of a hidden gang.
- **The end**: when either leaves (released, transferred, escaped, dead: `Story.Notify_Leaving` -> `InformantTask.Notify_Leaving`), when the informant is burned, quits or is no longer known as one, when the target is on the way out, or when the warden pulls them out; a new stay starts clean (`ResetForNewStay`). The daily pass is the backstop: it looks the target up on the map including out of sight (carried, in a container), and ends the task only on a target dead, gone from the map, no longer a prisoner or on the way out ("no longer watching anyone" when the target cannot be found at all); a target carried or breaking out, or an informant away or not active that day, keeps the task and risks nothing that day. Both fields are in the saveload plan's snapshot (`it=`). Test seams: `InformantTask.ExposureRoll`, `InformantTask.WrongLeadRoll`, `InformantTask.GangPool` (who the gang on the wing may count).

### Unit officers (`Staff/UnitOfficers.cs`, `UI/DeploymentTab.cs`, `Keyed/UnitOfficers.xml`; gap analysis 7, item 2)
The Deployment tab has a "Unit officers" block under the wings: one slot per wing (security level) whose zone has cells, or that has an officer named. The default is none. A guard holds one slot at most.
- **Naming** (`UnitOfficers.Assign`, the slot's menu `SlotOptions`): sets the guard's patrol zone to the wing; the zone they had is kept (`CompStaff.unitPrevZone`) and put back when they leave the post (if their kin rule still allows it). Naming someone else to the slot takes the holder off. Moving an officer to another wing is a rotation: the days start again, and the patrol kept is still the one from before the first post. Giving an officer another patrol zone in any patrol menu takes them off the post first (`SetPatrol`). Refused, with the reason (the menu greys the guard and says why): not a guard, the warden or the state's chief, a callout, away (on leave or suspended), already there, or kept off the wing by kin (`Kin.KeepsOffZone`: the guard asked to be kept off it). A guard with kin on the wing who chose to stay on it can be named, and the menu says there is kin.
- **Familiarity** is the days in post (`unitSinceTick`), shown on the Deployment row and the staff record with the shift they work: an officer on days leaves the nights uncovered, and the row says so. Familiarity 0..1 = days / 10.
- **Effects** (all only while serving: a guard, not away, and not while the state's chief deploys the guards):
  - Trust: hourly, each officer on the map who is awake, in their right mind and not held by hostage-takers notes the wing's inmates near them (`StaffMemories.Near`: the same room, within 10 cells in a big or outdoor one); at midnight, after the daily fade, each of them gives the officer +2.5 (`TrustPerDay`), only while their score of the officer is 0 or more, and never lifting it past 50 from this alone (`DriftCeiling`). Being around makes acquaintances, not forgiveness: a grudge fades as any feeling does (5% a day, forgotten under 2) and presence adds nothing until it has. With the fade, daily contact levels off around 50 and reaches "trusted" (30) in about 18 days; one day's contact fades away within days.
    - The sum, for an inmate near the officer every day whom the officer restrains (-15) once every *k* days. Without the drift the score after each restraint settles at -15 / (1 - 0.95^k), about -300/k on average over the cycle: -57 at k = 5, -41 at 7, -36 at 8, -28 at 10, -14 at 20. A lone -15 takes about 40 days to fade under the forgetting line of 2, so from neutral (a new inmate) any *k* under about 40 never lets the score back to 0: the post adds nothing and the score is the one above - hated on average (-40 or less) while the restraints come about once a week or more often, disliked (-19 to -36) at once every 8 to 15 days. Only restraints rarer than about every 40 days let the drift back in (about 43 on average at k = 40). An inmate who already trusts the officer at 50 stays above 0 while the restraints are at least about 6 days apart (about 50 - 300/k: 8 at k = 6, 21 at k = 10); the first restraint that takes the score below 0 drops them into the no-drift sum. A beating in sight (-10) or an abuse (-12/-25) works the same way.
    - The memory list keeps an inmate's 8 strongest feelings (`StaffMemories.MaxPerInmate`): on a wing whose inmates already hold 8 memories of 2.5 or more, the officer's first +2.5 is the weakest and is forgotten at once, so the drift never starts for them. Accepted (it is the list's rule for any newcomer).
    - The presence noted today is not saved (a reload loses the hours before it), which is accepted.
  - The tip: a daily chance of 5% + 2% a day in post, at most 25% (`TipChance`), that the officer brings one hidden fact about the wing to the desk as a letter. It is the informant's ranking (`InformantSystem.StaffTip`, through `TipAbout`): a tunnel, contraband, a gang tie (half the time), then a ring's link (`RingSystem.TryTip`: the ring's next link, who need not be on the wing), then a debt one of the wing's inmates is party to. Nobody inside told them: a search acting on it gives nobody away (it sets no `informedBy`, and keeps one an informant's earlier tip set, so that search may still give that informant away). It is worked out on the daily pass's map, not the officer's: a day-shift officer is at home at midnight (they leave at 22:00), on no map, and the ring and debt steps take the map too (`RingSystem.TryTip`, `DebtSystem.TryTip`: the debt's want is unveiled and the chronicle line names the two). The letter points at the officer when they are on the map, else at nothing. An officer on a fixer's payroll (`corrupt`, not a double agent) leaves the patron out and does not give away the patron's gang (the gang step's own-gang rule); a ring the officer is a link of is skipped, as for any teller. A staff contraband tip flags the inmate (`suspected`, which brings the search) but does not make them due a targeted drug test: that reason (`DrugTesting.ReasonTip`) is "an informant named them", the `informedBy` a search clears, and a member of staff's word is a different source. Counted in the statistics (`unitTips`, not the informants' `tips`).
  - Complaints: the filing odds of a complaint against the officer from an inmate of the wing whose score of the officer is 0 or more are multiplied by 1 - 0.4 x familiarity (`ComplaintFactor`, in `ComplaintSystem.FileChance`): at most 40% fewer, true or false. An inmate who resents the officer (below 0, which is where a brutal officer's wing sits, see the sum above) files as often as anywhere, so familiarity does not shield the abuse.
  - Corruption: a fixer on the wing finds them 0.015 a day in post likelier to say yes, at most 0.15 (`Lean`, in `CorruptionSystem.OddsFor`, the size of kin's leverage). It weighs the answer; it does not make anyone a target (that still takes weak integrity). It adds to kin's leverage (`Kin.Leverage`, +0.15 when the fixer's gang holds the officer's relative): a long-serving officer with kin in the fixer's gang on the wing is +0.30, which is meant (both are true).
  - Restraints go better through the existing trust terms (a trusted officer +5 points), and the count follows the patrol zone (`CountSystem.Free`): the officer counts only their own wing.
- **Vacating**: on leave or suspended (or stood down, or on a course) the slot is vacant (no effects, "away" on the row), the days are kept and do not grow: each midnight they are away moves `unitSinceTick` a day on (never past now), so the days count the midnights served. Under the state's chief the days run on (the officer is on site and among the inmates; only the effects wait). Another guard may be named to the slot meanwhile, which ends the away officer's post: the row and the menu say so ("replaces X, who is away"). Firing, quitting (`StaffUtility.Fire`, `FireOffSite`), death (`StaffDeaths.Notify_Died`) and retraining out of guard work (`ApplyRole`) clear the post. The daily pass clears a post whose holder is no longer a guard here, and one whose holder is kept off the wing by kin (also at the kin letter's answer and in `Kin.Daily`). An officer who leaves the map without being let go (carried off, kidnapped, on a caravan) is not among the staff, so the slot shows vacant and someone else may be named; when they come back the daily pass finds two holders and takes the earlier named off (`ClearDoubledPosts`). No guard becomes Protected in place (the warden's role is no guard role, so `ApplyRole` clears the post; the state's chief arrives new), so a Protected holder is only a backstop of the daily pass. Under the state's chief (`SpecialMeasures.ChiefInPost`) the posts are kept and the effects wait.
- **Saved**: `CompStaff.unitWing`, `unitSinceTick` (null / -1) and `unitPrevZone` (null; the third field is what puts the old patrol back after a reload); a wing whose def is gone on load clears the other two (`PostLoadInit`). `PrisonStats.unitTips`. In the saveload snapshot (`uo=`, `unitTips=`). Tests: `Testing/UnitOfficerChecks.cs` (`EvUnitOfficer`), `SeedUnitOfficerForReload`, `RIMPRISON_TOUR=unitofficer`. Seams: `UnitOfficers.ChiefOverride`, `UnitOfficers.TipRoll`, `UnitOfficers.PresencePool`, `Hourly`/`Daily(map, only)`.
- **Performance**: one pass over the prisoners per serving officer per hour (one officer per wing at most), one over the wing at midnight; the slot lookup walks the staff list (cached per tick).
### The rumour mill (`Security/Rumours.cs`, `Keyed/Rumours.xml`; gap analysis 7, item 5)
A wing here is a level (`CompInmate.security`), as the regime orders and the mourning of a death read it.
- **Sources** (`Rumours.Notify_Source`), each with its chance to start a rumour on the wing it touches (also its rank): a cell search 15% (`TunnelSystem.SearchCell` -> `Rumours.Notify_CellSearch`, the owner's wing; during a shakedown, which turns over every cell, each wing rolls once for the whole of it - `shakedownRolled`, not saved, so a load mid-shakedown may give a wing a second roll), a transfer out 40% (`InmateUtility.TransferOut`), a death on the wing 75% (`DeathAftermath.Notify_Died`), the yard called off 50% and an early lockup 40% (`RegimeOrders.Give`; the second shower starts none), a restriction 60% (`RegimeOrders.Restrict`, the riot reckoning's answer: one roll for the restriction, `Rumours.Notify_Restriction`, and none for the yard order and the lockup it is made of, `Give(..., talk: false)`), a food scare 60% (each wing of the sick rolls once), a staff strike 50% (every wing with inmates rolls), a send to supermax 60% (the wing they were taken from). None on a level whose inmates never meet (`Rumours.CanTell`: not supermax's walks, the padded cells or a referral's ward). The one it is about never starts it; the origin is a random inmate of the wing, awake if there is one; a wing needs two inmates. A share is the distorted version ("the yard is being closed for good", "the guards killed Hale", "they're shipping the whole wing to supermax"): 25% on a wing at a mean tension of 30 or less, rising to 75% at 80 (`DistortChance`).
- **Words and truth** are two fields: `distorted` (which words: what is shown and what winds the wing up) and `isTrue` (whether they hold, shown only once known). Mostly one is the opposite of the other, so the words of most kinds give the truth away to a warden who knows what happened, and "true or false?" is a formality there. Two cases differ: a death at a staff member's hands that rolls the distorted version is told as "the guards killed X" and is true, in the same words as the false one - the one case the warden cannot tell from the words; and the plain words of an order stop holding when the order ends (below).
- **One live rumour per wing**: a new one replaces the live one only when its source ranks higher, or once the live one has faded under half the strength a new one starts at; the replaced one ends "overtaken by worse news".
- **The spread** rides the hourly patch count (`GangTurf.Sample`, which already walks the inmates once an hour for turf): the same pass counts, while any rumour is live, each wing's inmates and how many of them stand in each shared patch (yard, common room, canteen). The wing's reach (the share that has heard it, from 25%) grows by half of the share of the wing that stood in a patch with someone else of it (two wings in one patch do not meet each other), times those who had not heard it. No pass over pairs. Its **strength** is the reach times the life left: it fades to nothing over two days from the start (`LifeDays`), a day later after a firm address - once: firm addresses do not stack (`MaxLifeTicks`, three days from the start at most).
- **The effect**: every inmate of the wing has a tension cause "a rumour - ..." of strength x 1 an hour (x 1.5 for the distorted words, true or not, so the size of the cause gives nothing away) (`TensionUtility.HourlyUpdate`, `Rumours.TensionFor`); one rumour at full spread adds roughly 20 (30 distorted) to each inmate over its life. The cause names the words where it is shown (`TensionUtility.CauseText`: the inspector, the file's bar, Intel, the misconduct message and the riot letter). The file has "The wing is saying ... (band)" and "- they started it" once the warden knows.
- **An order over**: when the warden lifts the order a rumour is about (`RegimeOrders.Lift`), or it lapses at midnight (`Sweep`), or a restriction ends, its rumour is not ended: it goes on, false now and known to be (`Rumours.Notify_OrderOver`), so a calm word can end it. Given again the same day, the order rolls nothing new and its plain words hold again (`Rumours.Notify_Order`): toggling an order neither silences the wing nor re-rolls it.
- **Answers**: the warden's address, from the gizmo or Intel's buttons (which send the warden to the canteen where most of the wing is now, else the one with most cells the level may use, else the one nearest the middle of the wing's zone): it answers the live rumour of each wing of which at least two heard it (`AddressMinHeard`, or the whole wing when it is smaller; a stray inmate in another wing's canteen does not answer for theirs); **calm** ends a false one at once and halves a true one's reach (a true rumour is never ended by talk), and the warden then knows which it was; **firm** makes either stick a day longer, once. A live rumour the address did not reach is said in a message. **The walk** of the wing (gizmo or Intel) names who started it once someone of the wing is met. **A known informant**'s tip (the daily 45%, or at the end of a walk) is about the origin half the time when they live on the wing, did not start it and the origin is not known: who and whether it holds (after a tunnel, contraband, a gang and the rings, before debts). Or it **burns out**. Knowing who started it only names them (Intel's row, "- they started it" on their file, the row clicks to them): no sanction or lever is tied to it.
- **Intel's Rumours list** (left column, under the tense inmates): the wing, the words, "true or false?" until known, the band (faint under 25, spreading under 60, all over the wing), and under a live one who started it (once known) and Calm / Firm / Walk, greyed with the reason (no warden, the cooldown, no canteen, the wing not painted; worked out once a frame). Those ended in the last day stay listed, muted, with how they ended; ended ones are kept three days, eight at most (`PrisonMap.rumours`, saved: kind, level, origin id and name, true or false, the words, reach, start, end of life, end tick and how, the subject's name, what the warden knows; a rumour saved without the words gets the opposite of its truth).
- **The riot**: the riot's chronicle and letter add "Before it began the X wing was saying ..." (`Rumours.RiotLine`). The riot reckoning's record keeps a copy of the rumour live on the wing when the riot began (`RiotRecord.rumour`, saved, filled in `RiotReview.Open`), as it stood then, so the morning's incident report names it ("The talk before it began - ...", true or false as known then) even when the rumour itself has been calmed, overtaken or burnt out by then.
- Test seams: `Rumours.ForceStart`, `ForceDistort`, `ForceTip` (every check's `Rollback` sets the start and the tip to false and puts them back: only a rumour check rolls them on purpose); `GangTurf.Sample(..., turf: false)` (the count without the turf tally); `TensionUtility.LastContribution`. The check `Rollback` puts the map's rumours back.

### The shot-caller's deal (`Security/GangDeals.cs`, `Defs/GangDeal.xml`, `Keyed/GangDeal.xml`; gap analysis 6, item 5)
The warden's word with an inmate (`WardenWord.GangDeal`, `WardenSystem.WordOptions`) has one more line, "keep your people quiet", for a known gang's known leader and nobody else (`GangDeals.LineFor`: the leader's gang and rank are on the file, as Intel lists them; for anyone else there is no line at all, which would give them away). It is shown disabled, with the reason, when a deal with the gang runs, five days have not passed since the last, the leader is out of reach (the hole, the supermax, a hostage-taker, rioting, the gallows, the shuttle), is cast in another story, the gang is at war or the leader sleeps in no wing (`GangDeals.Blocker`). Saying it sends a decision letter (`RP_Decision_GangDeal`, `urgent`: the warden asked, so the day's budget does not hold it back) with four terms and "not now" (the answer when it times out). The pick is the leader's dice (`GangDeals.AcceptChance`: 0.35 and up to 0.4 more or less with their trust of the warden, the shape of `WardenSystem.InformantChance`, which is nothing for a leader; `WardenSystem.ForcedRoll` is the test seam, the same as the informant's). A refusal costs trust (-4) and three tension, as an informant's does, and no deal.

A deal is one arc (`RP_Arc_GangDeal`, `GangDealArc`, background: `occupiesCast` false, like a ring) cast with the leader, and `StoryArc.deal` (`GangDeal`, one `Scribe_Deep` with a default of null) carries the rest: the gang, the leader's id and name, the term, the leader's wing (the wing zone over their bed, else the one they stand in), the tick it runs to, the breaches, whether the deal made the claim on the kept patch, the member and job of a job term, and whether the press has told it. The kept patch's name (its first cell's index) is the arc's `stake`, which `GangTurf.Reconcile` already moves with the patch. Ten days (`DealDays`); one deal at a time per gang; five days after one ends, by any road, before the next: `StorySystem.gangDealEnded` (an arc is forgotten once it is over, so the clock is not on it). The hot reads are one dictionary lookup by gang (`GangDeals.Running`, rebuilt when the arc list changes or a deal starts or ends), and nothing at all while no deal runs.

While it runs:
- **Calm**: `TensionUtility.MisconductChancePerHour` (not `MisconductUtility`: that is where the chance is read, but where it is worked out is `Security/Tension.cs`) is multiplied by `GangDeals.MisconductFactor` = 0.6 for the gang's members on the leader's wing (`WardenSystem.InWing`: their bed is in it or they stand in it), 1 for everybody else.
- **No war, no recruiting**: `GangWarArc.CanStart` is false for either gang of a pair under a deal (a war is between two gangs, so the code cannot tell who "starts" it: neither may be under a deal); `GangUtility.TryRecruit`, the initiation incident (and its letter's "play it out") and the mentor's gang pull take nobody in.
- **Rivals**: `GangUtility.TensionFromGangs` adds `RivalTensionPerHour` (1) for a member of another gang on that wing, with the cause "the Ravens run the yard" (`RP_Cause_Deal_<gang defName>`, else a plain one; the gang's name only for a member whose own gang is on file, as `ShownCause` guards).
- **The inspector and the press**: `Inspections.Score` is lower by `InspectionPoints` (5), for the inspection and the overseer's tour alike; the press may hear of the deal once (`PressChancePerDay`, in the arc's hourly step, the incident's own request letter on a topic about the deal); an open interview while a deal runs costs `PressPenalty` (3) reputation more.
- **The term** (the leader's pick): *turf* - the yard or common room patch the leader's level may use that is nearest their bed and held by no other gang, made the gang's claim by hand (`TurfBook.Set`); `GangTurf.Daily` leaves it alone (`KeepsPatch`), so it is held for the ten days whoever stands in it, and it is let go when the deal ends if the deal made it (a patch the gang held anyway is left to the daily verdict). *Visits* - `VisitationSystem.HourlyTick` calls a member who has family and is owed a visit (`GangDeals.VisitOwed`: 3.5 days since the deal began or their last relative's visit, `CompInmate.lastVisitTick`, set when a visit of a relative is completed: a former inmate's is not one) whatever their need and the odds; bans and the visitation room still apply. *Commissary* - `Sanctions.Banned(LoseCommissary)` is false for the gang's members while it runs; the ban's own clock is untouched and holds again after. *Job* - the member of the gang (never the leader, never one whose job the warden locked) with the best fit for a job with a place free (their own place counted free; the highest skill in what the job asks) is given it and it is locked (`jobLocked`), as the talent letter does; when the deal ends it is unlocked and taken (unless the warden changed it).

A breach: a seen fight or assault by a member (`MisconductUtility.Record`: `seen && def.assault`). The first is the arc's decision letter (overlook it, or end the deal; unanswered it is overlooked; while it is open nothing else counts, `GangDeal.breachOpen`, so an ambush or a riot recording several members is one breach); the second ends the deal with no letter, and the first's letter turns into the closed notice. Ending it (the letter, Intel's "End it" button on the gang's row, a second breach) costs the leader `EndedTrust` (-12) of the warden: the gang ranks have no standing of their own to lower (a rank, no score), so the leader's trust is what falls. The deal also ends, with no cost, at its tenth day (`Lapsed`), and when the leader is gone, no longer leads, is no longer on the wing the deal was struck on (moved, reclassified: the deal is the leader's, not the wing's) or is on an escort-only level (`Valid` false, or the arc's `Notify_Leaving`: `Gone`). Intel shows the deal on the gang's row (term, days left, breaches) with the button.

Checks: `Testing/GangDealChecks.cs` (`EvGangDeal`, after `EvHireFit`; each says in its summary how it fails). The saveload plan seeds a deal off every default, the inmate's last visit and a cooldown clock, compares them in the snapshot (`gangdeal=`, `lv=`) and takes them out again after the reload. Not done: "a leader under a deal is harder to challenge" (`GangChallengeArc`) - Fable's risk note, not in the brief's terms.
### Drug testing
`Programs/DrugTesting.cs`, `PrisonGame.drugTests` / `drugTestsPerDay` / `drugTestLog`, `Defs/DrugTesting.xml`, `Keyed/DrugTesting.xml` (gap analysis 6, 4). Policy row (Policy tab): none (the default, so an old save is unchanged), targeted, or targeted and random (N a day, 1-6). Targeted reasons, each at most once in two days per inmate (`CompInmate.testedFor`, one tick per reason): a seen contraband record in the last two days (a pat-down, a cell search, a garden patch, the mail), a tip naming them (`informedBy`), an overdose survived, and a parole hearing or a trial. The hearing reason is the two days before the inmate's next hearing (`NextHearingTick`: the later of half the sentence served and four days after the last hearing) or trial (`trialTick`), and once for each: a test made in that lead (or, for a hearing overdue and not yet held, since it came due) is not repeated for the same hearing. (It was "the week before", which with a hearing every four days made every eligible inmate due every two days.)
