- **The list**: `DrugTesting.Hourly` (from `Notify_HourChanged`) builds the due list once an hour in one pass over the inmates, plus the random picks (the day's tests still owed - minus the random rounds a doctor has taken on - spread over the hours left of 6-20; nobody picked in the last day). It is the doctor's list, not `InmateCandidates` (that rebuilds from a scan of all prisoners every tick). Not saved: a load loses it and the next hour builds it again. Nothing is listed with the policy off, in a hostage crisis or while anybody riots.
- **The round**: `WorkGiver_DrugTest` / `JobDriver_DrugTest`, modelled on the medication round. A doctor must be on duty (`OnDuty`: the timetable's hour is a work hour of the day shift - not the commute hour before it, a break or sleep - and not due home; the night doctor never), a doctor (`WorkGiver_MedicationRound.IsRoundDoctor`), and one of at least two on site. The round is first *taken on* (`Claim`: a `DrugTestClaim` in `DrugTestLog.claims` - doctor, inmate, the reasons, begun - saved with the game), then the kit is picked up, then the inmate taken aside (`TryBegin`, a few hundred ticks, `TestTicks` 300), read, and the kit used up. The cap of two tests an hour in the prison counts the tests begun in the game hour (`DrugTestLog.hourKey`/`started`, saved: a load does not reset it) and the rounds taken on and not yet begun, so a third doctor is not sent. The reasons are the job's own (`JobDriver_DrugTest.reasons`, saved with it) and the claim's (for the random quota): a random test begun at 19:00 and read after 20:00, or across a load, is still a random test. The kit is the exact `Thing` picked up (`kit`, saved with the job): it is checked, with the inmate's wait, before a result is read and destroyed only after a completed test (a refusal keeps it; it is put down by the finish action). The round stops, reads nothing and lets the inmate go when the policy is turned off, a riot or hostage crisis begins (`CrisisOn`: the rioters' own ticks keep a flag, `RiotPolice.RiotActive`, plus the running hostage arc; no scan of the inmates), the doctor goes off duty or is the only one left, the inmate cannot be taken, the inmate's wait ends, or the kit is lost or swapped.
- **The inmate's wait** is a job of its own, `RP_DrugTestWait` (`JobDriver_DrugTestWait`, target the doctor): the round ends it (`EndWait`, only if it is still the inmate's current job, so a job they took up instead is not cut), and it ends itself when the doctor's job is no longer this round (`RoundOf`), so nobody is left standing until an expiry. The test toil fails if the inmate stops waiting.
- **The test** (`Conduct`): *positive* on a drug used in the last two days (`CompInmate.lastDrugUseTick`): a seen `RP_DrugUse` (severity 1, `defaultSanction` CellConfinement for 4 hours: the lightest sanction on the table that is more than a word on the record - the others are a warning, no yard, no visits, no commissary), a substance abuse referral (as the overdose letter's help), and for a customer of a dealer, who is not themselves in a ring, `RingSystem.Notify_Found`: the ring's own chance and line, heat only when the dealer is traced. *Positive (dependence)* on an addiction hediff and no use in the last two days (`DrugTestResult.Dependence`): the file line, the referral and the parole term, but no `RP_DrugUse` and no punishment (use is punished, dependence is treated), and no trace on a dealer; counted as a positive in the statistics. A *refusal* (`RefuseChance`: more with a recent use and tension, less when beaten down) is a seen `RP_RefusedTest` (solitary, 4 hours), keeps the kit, is neither a test nor a positive in the counts. The last use is set in one place, a postfix on `Thing.Ingested` for any drug that is not medicine: `TryUseDrugs` only starts the Ingest job, and hooch, the garden's joints and the dealer's sales only put an item in a pocket. `MisconductDef.defaultSanction` is new: the policy starts a misconduct with it instead of the hole (the warden can still change it on the Policy tab).
- **Read elsewhere**: `ParoleSystem.ParoleChance` (-0.1 for any positive, dependence or refusal in the last five days - `CompInmate.lastFailedTestTick`, the tick of the last of those, so a negative test after it does not clear it; the board reads severity 2 and up only, so this is its own term, and the hearing's reasons), `TensionUtility` (a random test is a small cause, "searched again", for twelve hours - on the inmate tested, not on the wing), the file's line (last test, result, addictions; its own line, apart from the detox/withdrawal status), Statistics (`drugTests`, `drugPositives`).
- **Kits**: `RP_DrugTestKit`, a supply catalog entry; `Daily` and the Policy button keep `StockSpare + 2 x expected` in stock, in packs of ten, like the antipsychotic.
Saved: `lastDrugUseTick`, `lastTestedTick`, `lastFailedTestTick`, `lastTestResult`, `lastTestRandom`, `testedFor[4]` on the inmate; the policy, N and `DrugTestLog` (today and yesterday, the hour's count, the rounds under way) on `PrisonGame`; two stats. All have defaults; all are in the saveload snapshot.

### The funeral (`Story/Funerals.cs`, `Defs/Funeral.xml`, `Keyed/Funeral.xml`; gap analysis 6, item 8)
When a guard (or anyone who hauls) lays a body of the prison's own in the morgue, the last toil of `JobDriver_HaulToMorgue` calls `Funerals.Notify_Laid`, which writes one budgeted, not urgent decision letter, `RP_Decision_Funeral` (`FuneralDecision`: "a quiet burial", "a funeral"; a day to answer, and **no answer is a quiet burial**, today's way, `TimeoutChoice` 0; a letter a day old, counted from when it was written and not from when it was delivered, is one too, and so is one whose body has begun to rot - the morgue is not cold - and an answer to a letter that lapsed is disabled, "already decided"). Nothing is asked where there is nowhere to hold one (no plot that can take the body, no chapel, no shuttle pad). A body that reaches the morgue by any other road (a check's `PutInMorgue`, a body that was already there in an old save) is not asked about and goes as before.
- **The record** is a `Funeral` in `StorySystem.funerals` (scribed, a default for every field; gone after the day): the dead one's id and name, staff or not, the state (asked, quiet, waiting, gathering, done), the place (grave, chapel, pad) and its cell, the tick asked, the hour chosen (`holdTick`), the start and the end, everybody sent (`attendees`), and - because the shuttle can take the body, and the pawn with it, before the service - what the file said when the body was laid: the wing (`level`), the gang, who was to mourn (`invited`, by id, the closest first) and which relative would come (`kinName`, `kinKind`). The letter carries the dead one's id in `amount`. **Who was kept apart**: the warden's order with an inmate ends with their death (`StorySystem.Notify_Leaving`), so the kill hook notes the partners first (`Funerals.Notify_Dying`, `StorySystem.funeralApart`, scribed pairs of ids), the letter's `Invite` leaves them out of `invited` and uses the note up, and the hourly pass drops one that is a week old (a body never laid in the morgue).
- **The burial waits**: `WorkGiver_BuryBody` is not offered a body whose letter is unanswered or whose funeral is waiting for its hour (`Funerals.MayBury`); a quiet burial, a funeral under way or a funeral held lets it through, and the workman digs at the site the funeral stands round (`Funerals.SiteFor`). The shuttle's lift-off, `Burial.Capacity` and `Burial.Wanted` are as they were (a body the plot can take is still not sent; one that is sent takes its funeral with it to the chapel).
- **The hour**: the next free-time or work hour of the dead's timetable (`ServiceHour`: the default level's for the staff; none at all is a quiet burial with a line), saved as the tick it starts at; `Funerals.Hourly` (from `Notify_HourChanged`) starts it then. Not at night; not in a lockdown, a riot (`RiotPolice.RiotOngoing`), a raid (`GenHostility.AnyHostileActiveThreatToPlayer`) or a hostage crisis, and not with inmates to bring and no guard to spare, nor with nobody to come at all (no mourner, chaplain, colleague or warden who can walk there) - then it waits, and a day past its hour it **lapses to a quiet burial** with a chronicle line. A service under way is broken off by any of those (`Funerals.Rare`, every 250 ticks) with no thought and no tension taken off.
- **The place**: the grave (a free site in the plot, `Burial.FindSite`) when the plot will take the body and the mourners can walk to it; otherwise the chapel (its middle), else the shuttle pad. **The plot is staff-only** (`inmateAccess` false; the doors into it are staff-only for inmates), so an inmate's mourners usually cannot reach it: the service is then in the chapel while the workmen bury as usual (the description's "at the grave" is for the staff and for a plot with no door in the way). Whoever cannot walk to the place (`CanReach`) is dropped from the cast.
- **Who comes**: the chaplain on duty (`RP_LeadFuneral`, the lead-a-service driver in its funeral mode, which also counts as the service for praying inmates in the chapel); the dead inmate's friends (`RP_Friend`) and anyone whose opinion of them is 20 or more, and their gang mates (`Funerals.Grief`; not an enemy, not one the warden kept apart from the dead, by the note above), at most 16, let out of the regime as a class is (`CanComeOut`: not in the hole or a punishment, not in a lockdown or in the regime's lockup, not supermax, protective custody, the gallows, a visit, a class, a hostage; `KeepApart.Cohort` drops the second of two the warden keeps apart); the guards who escort them, one for five mourners, of the guards on duty and not on a job they must finish at most half, never the last: **the cost** - they are on `RP_AttendFuneral` for the hour, and with none to spare nobody is sent; and the dead one's relative as a visitor (a pawn, `LordJob_AttendFuneral`: the visiting duty to the service's cell, then out of the gate) if the visitation system can bring them (a visitation room, a level that is visited at all, a relative who is in touch and not barred, a way in from the road). The regime's own giver sends the inmates (`Funerals.JobFor`, in `JobGiver_InmateRegime` after the class and before the visit); `RegimePlacement.Sanctioned` counts the job as in place and `Contraband.AtAppointment` as an appointment.
- **A colleague's funeral**: the colleagues on duty (spawned, not drafted, not striking, suspended, held, going home or down, on a job that may be put down; the warden is picked by the same test; at most ten and the guards' share above; not the workmen when it is at the grave: they dig it) and the chaplain attend; the warden speaks (`RP_WardenAddress` ordered with `JobDriver_WardenAddress.EulogyMode`: what it does when it is said is `Funerals.Notify_Spoke`, with no canteen and no cooldown). No inmate comes.
- **The end** (`GatherTicks` 2000, under an hour): each mourner who is near the middle has `RP_SaidGoodbye` (stage 1 when the chaplain was there) and 10 less tension (15 with the chaplain); the rest of the wing 4 less; for the staff `RP_StaffFarewell` for every member of staff (stage 0), for those who stood there (1) and, when the warden spoke, 2. Nobody within reach of the middle at the end (all called away, or none got there) means no relief for the wing and no thought for the staff. A chronicle line says where, how many, and who led it.
- **The turf**: `GangTurf.Sample` leaves out whoever `Funerals.Away` says is at a service or within 1000 ticks of its end (the pass at the turn of the hour), so a gang in one place for the hour does not tip a patch's tally, and the count of the others does not move.
- **The rival**: for an inmate killed by another inmate (the case against the killer in `story.trials`), when the killer's gang is a rival of the dead one's and one of its members who can come out is on the dead one's wing, a roll of `ScuffleChance` (25%) as the service begins sends that rival to it too (not the killer, and not one the warden keeps apart from a mourner; the rival is one of those a guard escorts, and does not come alone; the letter only speaks of the chance where a rival could come); once they and a mourner are within a few steps `Scuffles.TryStart` (the pastimes' roll, with a pair that is the rival and a mourner) is rolled once, and the chronicle says whether a fight broke out.
- Checks: `Testing/FuneralChecks.cs` (`EvFuneral`, the last of the events list; `Funerals.TestOn` turns the funeral on for them, as `StoryLetters.TestBudget` does the letter budget: an autotest run keeps the burial as it was otherwise). Each says in its summary how it fails. The saveload plan seeds two records off every default and a note of who was kept apart, and compares them in the snapshot (`funerals=`). Windowed tour: `RIMPRISON_TOUR=funeral` (the letter, the gathering).
- Not done: the relative's visit is not searched and takes no contraband; a family's relative who came is not in the funeral's thought; no stat counter; a funeral for the dead of a raid or a visitor (`Morgue.Ours` only).

### Smuggling rings
`Security/SmugglingRing.cs`. Every active dealer has a ring: a background story arc (`RP_Arc_SmugglingRing`, the dealer is its cast but it keeps nobody out of other stories: `StoryArcDef.occupiesCast`)
that carries a `SmugglingRing` (`StoryArc.ring`): links (dealer, up to two courier inmates, an outside supplier who visits or writes, a corrupt officer), a heat, and a schedule. The links are the network
the game already has, read from it rather than invented: corrupt staff whose patron is the dealer (or their gang) are the officers (read daily), couriers are gang mates and regular customers the dealer takes on
(a few days apart; never an addict the dealer sells to, nor anyone with a link in the ring, taken out or not, nor anyone under `SupplyCut`), the supplier is the visiting relative or letter-writer of the first delivery to a member (kept
by name, never a pawn). A courier with drugs in the dealer's room, unwatched, hands them over (hourly), but only what came in through the ring (a visit, the post, a guard on the payroll: a count on the courier's link,
`RingLink.carried`); what they bought from the dealer is theirs.
A smokeleaf patch in the garden is a supplier of the ring's own, next to an outside one (see Contraband, "made inside"); the Intel chain calls it "the garden".
Everything is hidden: each link has a `known` flag, and every line, letter, tooltip and look target names only known links (the gang war took three review rounds on exactly this). The warden learns one
link per step: a find on a member (frisk, cell stash, a visitor caught at reception, a letter opened in the mailroom) reveals that member (and the supplier at the door or on the envelope); a find on someone
the dealer sold to may trace the dealer; contraband traced to an officer reveals the officer; a snitch's tip near a member (after the tunnel, contraband and gang tips: `InformantSystem.TryTip` asks the ring just before the debts), a phone tap on a member's arranged delivery (officer first, then supplier), a session
with the psychologist (their own part first, then another's) and a turned member (one a day, also a guard turned by internal affairs) reveal one. A dealer whose file already shows the trade (a session, an inquiry into an overdose) is the ring's known dealer, and
finding the dealer in the ring shows the trade on their file. Heat rises with what a find or a tap gives away, never with the call or the find alone (a tap that overhears nothing, or a find on a customer that does not trace the dealer, leaves the heat where it was: heat is
visible, and it would say the caller is in a ring); an officer on the payroll of two dealers is traced into both rings. Heat decays 2 a day; 50 and over makes the members tense; at 35 with a link
known the warden is asked (`RP_Decision_Arc`, once in three days). The Intel tab draws the known chain (supplier, officer, couriers, dealer) with one marker, "more links suspected", while any link was still to find at the last daily pass (`SmugglingRing.moreShown`: read when the ring forms and at the daily pass, cleared by a find that leaves nothing unknown; never live, since a delivery
that got past the search adds a link out of the warden's sight and a marker that came on then would say so; never a mark per link, and no count of them anywhere in the UI or in a letter: how many are left
would show what happened out of the warden's sight: a guard bought overnight, a search that failed, an inmate released), the heat, and the moves:
*bust* takes out every known link now (inmates lose what they carry and hide, are put on record for what was taken (nothing taken, nothing written up) and get no visits or post for five days, the officer is let go and arrested, the outside supplier is barred by name (`RingSystem.SupplierBarred`: a relative with that label neither visits nor writes for eight days, `Relatives.RandomVisitor`; nothing is put on the file of the inmate the supplier's link stores, who may be a courier or the dealer nobody has found, and no letter, line or look target uses it);
if the dealer is among them the ring breaks and the dealer starts another once the cut is over, otherwise the ring stays and the dealer rebuilds), or *wait*, or *turn* a known officer (a double agent) or courier (an
informant): each gives up a link a day, a courier may be found out. A ring broken whole (the dealer, at least one link besides them, none unknown or left) is a chronicle milestone and adds 20 to that day's security grade (two rings at most); not for a dealer the warden took out less than ten
days ago (`CompInmate.ringBustedTick`), and a ring that forms again around a dealer taken out before starts with the dealer known. The letter lists only what a bust would take out, and once the ring has been busted its old letter offers only waiting.
`SupplyCut` zeroes the visit, post and shuttle-cargo smuggling chance of anyone taken out, for the cut (kept on the inmate, `CompInmate.supplyCutUntilTick`: it outlasts the ring), and stops a corrupt guard's handover to them. A turned courier works for the warden: they pass nothing
to the dealer and do not hold one of the two places for couriers. The letter's "turn" takes the courier the ring counts on most (`BestToTurn`). A courier moved to protective custody or death row drops out of the ring, and an officer away in a caravan or a pod stays in it. The map of finds (`PrisonMap.finds`: cell, kind, source, tick; 30 days) is drawn with the coverage overlay (marks by kind, a letter
by source); the danger map (see the morgue's section) is its counterpart for violence. Event-driven: nothing per tick.

### Mail
Letters in and out: inmates with family get news every few days (def-driven kinds: ordinary, good, photos, a birth, a
death, a Dear John, a former inmate, a lawyer) that moves mood, tension and the family need and goes in the file. Post
is sorted in the mailroom zone (a table) by a guard or a trusted inmate clerk (the clerk needs a mailroom behind an Auto door; with a
clerk on duty the guards leave a letter to them for 12 hours, then take it); the warden's policy is spot checks or
reading every letter (slower, finds more, a privacy cost to the family need). Letters can hide drugs: found = confiscated
+ misconduct + the family's next visit flagged; missed = the inmate gets them. No mailroom or sorter: mail piles up, the
need falls and an alert says why.

### Inmate wants
Each inmate may have one goal (`CompInmate.want`, an `InmateWant` of an `InmateWantDef` with a worker class): make
parole, get clean, earn a diploma, stay in touch with family, settle a score (a grudge: a staff memory of -60 or worse,
or an enemy inside; formed by events, not rolled). Rolled at intake from the crime, traits, addiction, family and sentence;
a birth letter gives a family goal about the child, a grudge replaces a weaker goal. Progress is read from the systems
(programs passed and sat, the addiction fading, the board's odds, letters and visits) on the inmate's own Hourly, and the
systems tell the goal when it matters (a hearing, a Dear John letter, an overdose, a failed exam, a release). On track:
a little tension relief and serious misconduct x0.8; a setback is a big negative beat, the goal is retried (a new
deadline) while it has setbacks to spare, else lost, and a goal lost to a setback can flip into another (family -> a
grudge). The grudge makes the misconduct picker go for the named person. Milestones, endings and flips go through
`Story.Record` (chronicle + life log); the inmate file shows the goal with a bar and what stands in the way; the storyteller
casts inmates whose goal is near a milestone more often. Leaving a gang is built (see "Gang structure and a way out"); innocence goals, and "keep my head down", are not:
each is a def, a worker and keys.

### Staff
Colonists with a role: Guard, Cook, Workman, Janitor, Doctor, Gardener, Psychologist, Counsellor, Chaplain, Administrator and the rest of `Defs/Staff.xml` and the other role defs; and the warden, who is not hired (below). Role sets
allowed work types, uniform, wage. Hire from a shortlist of applicants (see "Choosing the people you hire" below); fire. Staff morale matters: unpaid/overworked/unsafe staff go on strike, quit, or take bribes (smuggling).

### Choosing the people you hire (`Staff/Applicants.cs`, `UI/ApplicantsUI.cs`, `Staff/Staff.cs`, `Languages/English/Keyed/Hiring.xml`; gap analysis 6, item 3)
There is no catalog of candidates: a post's hire used to be blind (`StaffUtility.Hire` generated the person after the fee). The Staff tab's hire buttons now open `Dialog_Applicants` for the post.
- **The pool.** `PrisonGame.applicants` (`ApplicantPool`: `IExposable` + `IThingHolder`, its pawns in a deep-saved `ThingOwner<Pawn>` as `PrisonGame.home` holds the staff at home; not world pawns, not on a map). An
  `Applicant` is a pawn, its post, the tick it applied and its round; a `RecruitmentRound` per post says which round its pool was made in and how many were made. At most `PerRole` (3) applicants per post.
- **Rounds.** A round is `RoundDays` (3) days of ticks counted from tick 0 (`RoundAt`). The pool for a post is made on the warden's click (`Open`, from `HiringUI.Open`) when no pool was made for it this round: up to three
  `TryGenerateCandidate` pawns, stopping at the first time the generator gives up (the dialog says how many could be found); never while drawing, never for every post at once. Opening again in the round draws no random
  numbers and makes nothing, also when the pool is empty (hired out, or nobody fit): an empty pool is not a reroll. There is no reroll button. A post's past applicants are discarded when its next round's pool is made, and
  every past round's in the daily pass (`DailySettlement`), which also lets go any pawn the pool holds without an entry; a discarded applicant is taken out of the holder and passed to `WorldPawns` with
  `PawnDiscardDecideMode.Discard` (destroyed, discarded, relations cleared: not a world pawn, no dangling relation).
- **Generation.** No kin is rolled for an applicant (a relation to an unspawned, discardable pawn is a hazard). `StaffUtility.Hire(..., applicant: true)` is the applicant's path: an ordinary hire in everything,
  including `Kin.OnHired` at hire. A former inmate (`candidate != null`, `applicant: false`) keeps their identity and record and is not rolled.
- **The window.** Per applicant: portrait, name, age, the skills the post's work types use (with passion), the post's work they cannot do (the generator takes nobody who cannot do any of it, so the line mostly says they can do it
  all), their RimWorld traits (nothing of the mod's hidden integrity or corruption), a Hire button (greyed, with the reason, when the funds are short); the fee and wage, the time to the next round, and an agency button:
  `StaffUtility.HireThroughAgency`, the old blind hire at `AgencyFeeFactor` (2) times the fee, still no hire and no fee when no fit candidate is found. Row texts are built when the window opens and when the pool's
  `version` moves, not per frame. A window left open across a round closes itself.
- **Hiring one.** `ApplicantPool.TryHire` charges the post's fee, takes that pawn out of the holder and spawns it through the ordinary hire (same id, name, skills and traits as shown), once: a second try is refused.
  Hiring an applicant of a round that is over discards the round's pool and refuses.
- **Saved.** The pool's pawns, entries and rounds under `PrisonGame.applicants`; an old save loads with an empty pool. Tests: `Testing/HiringChecks.cs` (`EvHiring`), the saveload plan's `hiring=` snapshot and `SeedHiringForReload`.
  Seams: `ApplicantPool.TickOverride` (a later round without the days), `StaffUtility.CandidateUnfit`, `CandidateChronologicalAge`.

### The warden on the floor (`Staff/Warden.cs`, `Staff/WardenAbilities.cs`, `Defs/Warden.xml`, `Languages/English/Keyed/Warden.xml`; gap analysis 4, section 1.5)
PA's Warden's Edition made the warden a person with abilities. Here the warden is a pawn of the staff, `StaffRoleDef.warden` (`RP_Warden`: wage 0, not hireable, `Social` as the key skill, and a shut work tab: `Patch_Pawn_GetDisabledWorkTypes_Warden` leaves only the emergencies and self-care every member of staff keeps, so the Work tab greys the rest out and no order can send them to subdue or haul), who is the player on the floor.
- **The post.** Spawned at the start of all three prisons by `ScenPart_PrisonStart` (`WardenSystem.Appoint`, on top of the scenario's colonists, near the first one; no chronicle line at the start). A prison with no warden (a save
  from before this, or the warden died) gets one by the same path: the first midnight that finds the post empty starts the wait (`PrisonGame.wardenVacantTick`, scribed), and `SuccessionDays` (2) later the state names
  one (a letter and a line). `StaffDeaths` sets the wait when the warden dies, and sends a letter of its own (no compensation question: there is no family to look after, and no bitter letter after a death of neglect) instead of the staff-death decision.
  **A warden who is away has not left the post** (`WardenSystem.Away`: a caravan, a pod or shuttle, another map, the world): the midnight pass starts no wait and names nobody while one lives anywhere, and no caravan or pod takes the
  warden in the first place (`Patch_CaravanFormingUtility_AllSendablePawns_Warden`). If two wardens are ever on the floor (one came back after all), the one named last keeps the post (`InPost`) and the other is let go with a line
  (`StandDownOthers`).
- **Kept on the floor.** The three extreme breaks that would take them off it cannot occur (`Patch_MentalBreakWorker_BreakCanOccur_Warden`: give up and leave, run wild, jailbreaker: a warden who ran wild would be a factionless
  wild man with the role still on them, and a prisoner for the taking), and the midnight pass lifts their joy to 0.8 (`JoyFloor`: the evening a member of staff gets at home, which they never do). They cannot be drafted
  (`StaffUtility.IsProtected`, as the state's chief): a drafted warden fights, kills an inmate, and the inquiry has nobody to suspend; the inquiry's "suspend the officer" is off for the warden and the chief alike (and for a callout officer of the riot squad, who leaves within hours whatever a suspension says). No complaint is filed
  against them (`Protected` in every filer), they are not officer of the month, and the warden is left out of the staff mood average (strikes, burnout), the hazard-pay head count and the "three on site" for burnout.
- **Protected** (`CompStaff.Protected` = `statePosted || role.warden`, used where the state's chief was already kept out): `StaffUtility.Fire` refuses (a message, no record), `StaffCommute.ShouldBeHome` is false whatever
  the timetable, leave or a suspension says (and the warden is not counted as the one kept on site: a guard still is), `CorruptionSystem` never rolls integrity or approaches them and `Suspend`/`Discipline`/`StandDown`
  do nothing to them, `ShiftSystem` keeps them on days, `StaffLives.Storied` (burnout, debt, retirement), strikes, promotion and courses leave them out. No change-role or fire gizmo, no patrol zone.
- **A bed.** They never go home, so they sleep here, and the check "staff asleep on the floor" and the one that forbids a bed in the staff room stand: `EnsureCot` claims an unowned colonist bed, else builds a cot
  (free) in the smallest roofed staff-only room (painted `RP_StaffOnly`: the inherited facility's office) in a corner and off the doors, else near the warden in a room outside every zone; nothing on a bare plot. The hour before
  their timetable says sleep (`WardenSystem.Hourly`) they walk to it (`RP_WardenRetire`, not a casual job, waits there until sleep), so they lie down in it and not across the prison. Vanilla's "jealous of X's bedroom"
  (trait Jealous, -8) would fall on any staff member with no room of their own the moment the warden had one: `Patch_ThoughtWorker_BedroomJealous_Warden` is vanilla's rule with the warden left out.
- **The abilities** are three gizmos on the warden (`WardenGizmos`), each a job (`JobDriver_Warden*`) the warden walks to and does, with a cooldown scribed on `CompStaff` that starts when the thing is done
  (`addressReadyTick`, `walkReadyTick`, `wordReadyTick`). Not while held, down, in a mental state or busy with another; each says why in the gizmo.
  - *Address the canteen* (12 h): the warden stands in the canteen nearest them (a canteen is a block of painted cells that touch: a second one across the prison is another) and speaks for 420 ticks. Everyone awake in that canteen then: **calm** tension -10 and `suppressed` -15, a mood thought (+3, 1.5 days), a little trust
    (+2 in `StaffMemories`); **firm** `suppressed` +20 (fewer outbursts boil over), a sore thought (-3, a day), resentment (-2), and `CompInmate.wardenFirmUntil` (10 h): `TensionUtility` adds 1.5 tension an hour (cause
    "sore from the warden's word") until then. Nobody in the canteen: nothing done, no cooldown.
  - *Walk the wing* (6 h): `WalkPlan` takes up to five stops of a painted wing, at least seven cells apart, reachable, corridors before cells, in nearest-next order; the warden pauses 150 ticks at each and meets the inmates
    of the wing in earshot (9 cells, awake, in no mental state, once each on a walk): tension at most 45 gains +3 trust, at least 65 gives -4 (resentment), in between nothing. At the end a snitch among those met may tell something in person
    (50% each, the first that has something: the same tip and letter as the daily pass, `InformantSystem.GiveTip`), and anyone met whose complaint is filed and not yet on the desk says it to the warden's face
    (`ComplaintSystem.RaiseInPerson`: the same letter, now, within the two-letter limit). The cooldown starts at the first stop (and again at the end), so a walk broken off has still been walked.
  - *A word with an inmate* (3 h; targeter, then a menu of what can be said, one word a day with each inmate, `CompInmate.wardenWordTick`): the warden walks to them (or where they are) and talks for 450 ticks; an inmate
    who walks off is not talked to. **A few words**: +6 trust, tension -5, a thought (+4, 2 days). **Back their goal**, only for a goal the warden knows of (`Wants.Shown`: never a secret one or one that would name a
    gang nobody has found), once per goal (`InmateWant.backed`): `InmateWantWorker.Encourage` gives it three more days (so a slipping goal is lifted), a family goal counts a call home as a contact, a grudge is talked
    down, *if it takes* (`WantWorker_Score.TalkDownChance`: 0.3, +0.4 x trust/50, -0.25 x how deep the grudge is, -0.15 for a volatile one, between 0.1 and 0.85; and then the staff member it was against is vouched for, +10: the hate stays) and if it does not the grudge stands, -4 trust and +4 tension, and no more words on it; a line in the inmate's life. What the word is about is looked at again when it is said (the order was given hundreds of ticks before): an inmate spoken to or recruited in between is not spoken to again. **Sound them out as an informant** ($10 a day): they agree with a chance of `0.15 + 0.35 if afraid, vulnerable or broke and
    wound up + 0.4 x trust/50` (halved in a gang, never a gang's leader; a hidden snitch simply says yes: the asking finds them), through the same `InformantDecision.Recruit` as the letter; a refusal costs -4 trust and +3
    tension. **Where they stand before a hearing**, when one is within three days: tension -6, +4 trust, `CompInmate.headsUpTick`: for four days `ParoleSystem.ParoleChance` is 0.06 higher (not in what a want
    measures of their own conduct), and the warden is told the odds and what stands in the way.
- **The risk.** The warden walks among the inmates, and is what a riot would like to get: in `HostageCrisis.Candidate` the warden in reach is taken before whoever the ringleader went for (a hated one is still first), a
  seizure of them is twice as likely (`SeizureChance`), and a crisis over the warden is the worst there is: a 4-hour stand-off (not 6), 3 hours to the last deadline (not 4), the talks -0.1, the last deadline kills 0.1 more
  often, a storm 0.05 more, the prison's name suffers twice as much when it goes wrong (a crisis ended well earns no more than for any member of staff), and the letter says whose it is. A blow to them from an inmate
  is a letter and a line (once for a fight; not while they are held: the crisis tells it) and those who trust them in that wing take it hard (+6 tension; outside a wing, those within 25 cells). Their death is a letter, and the post is empty until the state names another.
- **Saved:** `CompStaff` cooldown ticks, `PrisonGame.wardenVacantTick`, the inmate's three ticks, `InmateWant.backed`, four counters in `PrisonStats` (the statistics tab). All of it is in the saveload snapshot
  (`WardenSystem.Describe`, `Wants.Describe`).
- **Checks** (`Testing/WardenChecks.cs` and `Testing/WardenFixChecks.cs` for what the review of it found, from the events plan; the start's also from the showcase plan; the saveload plan seeds and compares): the start, the protection (each gate, with a guard as the control), the cot and
  the walk to bed, the gizmos (offered, held, cooling down), the address (calm, firm, the creep, nobody there, through ticks), the walk (the plan, the meetings, the tip, the complaint, through ticks), the words (what is
  on offer and what is hidden, each effect, the dice forced, through ticks), the blow, the prize, the crisis over the warden, and the successor.
- **Not built:** the warden is not on the payroll and has no career; the gizmos are the only way to use them (no automatic rounds: but inmates can now ask to see the warden, below); the cot is not a room the player designs. The gizmo icons are
  vanilla's (Attack, SquadAttack, Draft) until there is art for them; earshot does not stop at a wall (a locked cell would hear nothing, and the walk would meet nobody).

### Asks to see the warden (`Staff/WardenAsks.cs`, `Keyed/WardenAsks.xml`; gap analysis 7, W2)
The warden's day had buttons on a cooldown and nothing that said where to go. Now the systems that give an inmate a reason to want the warden in person put them in a queue, and answering in person is worth more than a generic word or walk.
- **Who asks** (`WardenAsks.Raise`, each hook a line in its system): a trial witness who was got at and comes forward (`Trials.TryPressure`: the report's own 35% roll, no hated officer) and is one the warden knows of who trusts the warden in post (any trust above 0) - they ask to see the warden *instead of* telling the guards, so the request is taken out of the report's odds and the rate at which pressure comes out is what it was (failed dice: nothing shows, as before); a debtor after a beating (`DebtSystem.TryBeat`); a member of a gang the warden knows whose doubts became the goal to leave (`GangExit.RollDoubt`; a member of a gang the warden does not know does not ask, since a request would say the gang and the goal exist: they wait for the chaplain or a counsellor as before); each hunger striker when the strike begins (`HungerStrike.Join`, from `IncidentWorker_HungerStrike`); a supermax inmate put to the warden for the step-down (`SupermaxSystem.AskStepDown`); an inmate who asked for another cell (`CellRequests.TryRaise`); and, when a false rumour starts on a wing (`Rumours.Start`), someone of that wing who did not start it (awake if anyone is). A true rumour has nobody ask (the ask itself says the talk is false: someone who knows better comes forward). **Owner to confirm:** the request settles the rumour's truth before a tip or a calm address would (Intel's rumour row may say "truth unknown" beside a request about it, and a rumour with nobody asking is probably true); the cheaper alternative, asking on most false rumours and some true ones, is not built.
- **The queue** is per map (`PrisonMap.asks`: inmate id, reason, tick; saved, empty by default). One request per inmate: a reason as serious as theirs or less is not added (the same one starts its day again), a more serious one takes its place (the rank is the order above: witness, debtor, doubter, striker, step-down, cell, rumour). A request lapses after a day (`LapseTicks`); the hour (`WardenAsks.Hourly`, a pass over the short list) drops lapsed ones, those of an inmate no longer here or active, and those whose reason is over: the witness's case gone or recanted, the known debt gone (a hidden debt neither names the request nor keeps it), the doubter heard out, the goal gone or the gang not known, the strike over, out of supermax or the step-down letter answered or timed out, the cell letter answered or timed out, the rumour over. A rumour request is tied to its rumour by the tick: it is made the tick the rumour starts (`WardenAsks.RumourOf`), so a rumour live on the asker's wing that started later (a worse one that replaced theirs, perhaps a true one) or earlier (another wing's, after a transfer) is not theirs, and the request is over: it never describes, nor answers, another rumour. No new saved field.
- **Answered in person**, by the warden's word with them (any word: `WardenSystem.Word` calls `WardenAsks.Answer` after the word has done its own) or by a walk that meets them (`WardenSystem.Meet`, in place of the walk's meeting). A request whose reason is over when the warden arrives is dropped and gives nothing more. Otherwise:
  - *By a word*: a chat's worth again on top of the word (+6 trust, -5 tension: a chat with an asker is +12 and -10).
  - *On a walk*: twice the walk's trust (+6), never resented however wound up they are (the generic walk gives a wound-up inmate -4), and the warden's word as a memory.
  - *The reason's own*, each an existing effect stepped up: **a witness** tells the warden they were got at (`Trials.Report` with the warden: the report the guards would otherwise have had, said as told to the warden; the threat is known, Intel marks it, protective custody and keep-apart orders follow from there); **a doubter** is heard out as the chaplain or a counsellor would (`GangExit.Notify_Session`: the renouncement six hours later; a prison without a chaplain gets gang exits through the warden's word, which costs only its 3-hour cooldown - it weakens the case for a chaplain for gang work, **owner to confirm**; with a chaplain the session usually comes first and the request goes); **the rumour's asker** names who started it and whether it holds (the walk alone names the origin; this is the informant's tip, origin and truth, so a calm address can then end it; the chronicle's origin line is written only if the origin was not known). The debtor, the striker, the step-down candidate and the cell request get the trust and calm alone (the striker's mood gets the word's thought; the letters stay the warden's to answer). The deal and the hearing heads-up are not reasons of their own: a word chosen from "Go" can be either, and the request's extra comes on top.
  - One asked to see the warden since their last word may be spoken to again the same day (`WordOptions`: the day's limit counts from the word, and a request made after it lifts it) - for a few words, their goal or where they stand, not to be sounded out as an informant again nor offered the leader's terms (a pitch turned down is not redone the same day because they asked about something else).
- **The HUD** has a line under the count: "3 ask to see you" (amber; "Nobody asks to see you" muted), its tip naming them with their reason and hours left; a click opens Intel (`PrisonHUD.AsksLine`). The count leaves out lapsed requests and inmates gone, not a request whose reason ended within the hour (the hour drops it; "Go" then answers nothing): applying `Moot` there would also hide the tour's made-up requests.
- **Intel's list** (top of the right column, "Asking to see you"): the name (click to find them), the reason in words the warden may know (a witness: the case, not the threat; a debtor: the known debt's creditor; the rumour they asked about, not another), the hours left, and **Go**: the warden's word (the menu of what can be said, as the gizmo's) or, for a rumour, the walk of the asker's wing; greyed with the reason (no warden, the cooldown, cannot be talked to, the wing not painted).
- **Earshot through doors (not built).** The analysis's "earshot stops at a closed door (the walk then stops at cell doors)" is left out: `WalkPlan` is also the count's walk (`CountSystem.JobFor`), prison doors are closed but for the moment someone passes, so the rule alone would have a walk in lockup or at night meet nobody, and putting a stop at every cell door makes a 20-cell wing a 20-stop walk (150 ticks each) and changes the count's walks with it. Not a small, safe change to `WalkPlan`.
- **Saved and checked:** `PrisonMap.asks` (a save from before has none); the saveload snapshot's `asks=` and a seed of two requests about the seeded rumour's wing (`SeedWardenAsksForReload`). `Testing/WardenAskChecks.cs` from the events plan (`EvWardenAsks`): the queue's rules, the HUD's row (count and tip), the lapse (the clock forward a day and back), the hour's drops; a chat with an asker against a control, the day's word lifted by a later request (not the informant's pitch), a request whose reason is over; a wound-up asker met on the walk against a control; the witness (the report's dice forced, `Trials.ForcedReport`: failed dice raise nothing; coming forward with trust asks the warden, without trust or unknown to the warden tells the guards; one who hates a guard neither) and the report by the word; the debtor's hook (`TryBeat`, the known debt over a hidden one); the doubter (a hidden gang's never asks, a known gang's asks and is heard out); the strike's and the cell request's hooks; the false rumour's asker (never the origin; none for a true one) named on the walk, the origin line once; a rumour request tied to its rumour (not a true one that replaced it, nor another wing's after a transfer); the step-down letter's hook and its answer; "Go" and the walk it orders for a rumour. Every check's `Rollback` puts the queue back. `RIMPRISON_TOUR=asks`: the HUD's line and Intel's list, made-up requests and rumour, paused.

### Programs (reform)
ProgramDef: zone, required furniture, teacher role, sessions, cost/session, pass chance (skills/traits), effects
(lower reoffend chance, unlock kitchen/workshop labor, mood, parole bonus, grant money). Enrollment per inmate.

### Prison labor
Workshop: license plates from steel (exported for profit, tool theft risk). Kitchen duty, laundry, cleaning,
gardening for qualified inmates. Labor pays a little and fills the day (lower tension).

### Vocational training and prison industries (`Programs/Vocation.cs`, `Labor/Tailoring.cs`, `Defs/Vocational.xml`, `Patches/Tailoring.xml`)
Gap analysis 4, section 1.7. Three things, all built on the program system (a program's `forJobs` offers it to the inmates who could hold the job; an
`InmateJobDef.requiredProgram` shuts the job to everyone who has not passed it).
- **Tailoring**: a course of two programs. `RP_TailoringTheory` is taught in the classroom (3 sessions, by the counsellor, or a workman when there is none) and is a
  *stage* (`ProgramDef.stage`): passing it only opens the next part and adds nothing to the tally of programs passed that parole and the reoffend roll read.
  `RP_Tailoring` needs it and is taught in the workshop, at a vanilla tailor bench (the bench is the seat: `ProgramDef.seats`, one inmate to a bench), by a workman
  (3 sessions, a $250 grant). Passing it certifies a trade (`ProgramDef.trade`) and opens `RP_JobTailoring` (minimum and medium security, 3 a work hour,
  Crafting). One workshop room holds one session at a time, so the bench class and workshop induction take the room by turns. The bench class and the tailors share the
  benches: a bench someone has reserved (not a tailor at work, not a leftover attendee of a class that is over) is no seat (`ProgramSystem.TryFindVenue` counts the free seats of
  any program, the chapel's and the classroom's too); a tailor sewing at a bench is no obstacle, because a class that starts takes the room and the bench from them
  (`TakeSeatsFromWork`: the cycle is dropped, and unpaid, as it is paid at its end), and nobody is given a cycle at a bench in a room a running class holds
  (`ProgramSystem.HeldByClass`). So a prison with a tailor and one bench still gets its next class: the class goes first.
- **The tailors' work.** The vanilla hand and electric tailor benches carry a `CompProperties_Workstation` with `uniforms` (a patch: hand 3 per 2400 ticks, electric 3 per
  1500). A tailor sews a cycle at a bench in the workshop zone (`JobDriver_Sew`, `RP_Sew`) and the prison pays for the cloth, $4 a uniform. Tailoring is export work: the
  uniforms are all *surplus* (`LaundrySystem.surplus`, at most 24, scribed with `sewn`), which tailors keep sewing for while there is an Exports zone to load them from
  (`Tailoring.Room`: none without one) and which the next export run (`LaborUtility.SellExports`, which the shuttle's lift-off calls) sells at $14 a uniform and empties.
  The laundry keeps the clean stock (it washes for nothing, and tailors replacing it at $4 a uniform would be a net cost and would take the laundry's work), but the surplus
  refills the stock when the stock runs out: an inmate's swap of a filthy uniform takes one from it, and the midnight top-up takes from it before paying the outside service
  $9 a uniform (`LaundrySystem.Take`). A cycle pays for what has somewhere to go when it ends, not when it began (`Tailoring.Sew`: the room left under the cap, none and no
  charge without an Exports zone). No money for the cloth, or no room, means no work (the regime gives them the workshop to wait in). Uniforms are counted, not things: nothing lies about to be hauled.
- **Kitchen safety**: `RP_KitchenSafety`, 2 sessions in the canteen (zone `RP_Canteen`, seated at its dining chairs and stools: a room every prison has from the first day,
  idle in the work slot; the classroom is behind the Reform bureaucracy, and the kitchen is staff-only), taught by the counsellor, or by the cook when there is none (a work giver in the cook's
  own Cooking work type, so a class starts; the counsellor comes first in `teacherRoles` so a prison that has one keeps its cook at the stoves), no grant (like workshop induction: it
  is a gate to a job, and a grant per graduate for every minimum-security inmate would be a way to farm the state). It counts as a program passed, like induction. A class does
  not start in a zone the regime has inmates in right now (`ProgramSystem.RegimeHoldsZone`: the canteen at a meal, with the wings' meal times apart). `RP_JobKitchen.requiredProgram` is
  this program: the kitchen crew is the inmates who passed it (the hourly assignment takes the job off one who has it and has not, locked by the warden or not: an old save's
  locked kitchen job on an untrained inmate would be a dead job that holds a slot). Existing kitchen hands lose the job until they pass (no migration; pre-release).
  The kitchen's stand-down after a food scare does not close the course (`LaborUtility.Eligible(..., forProgram: true)`). Inmates are eligible for several programs at once, and the
  hourly pass takes each inmate for the first class in its order (a referred program first, then the fullest), so a small course would never start while bigger ones exist: a course
  a job with a free slot asks for (`ProgramSystem.JobWaits`: the job's `requiredProgram`, or the first part of it; free slots = capacity above the holders, and no stand-down) goes
  before the fuller classes (after a referral). It applies to the tailoring course and workshop induction as well; once every slot is held the course is an ordinary class again. A food scare is less likely with a trained crew: `FoodScare.SafetyFactor` multiplies the incident's chance (`ChanceFactorNow`) by 1 - 0.15
  for each trained inmate on the kitchen job, up to four (down to 0.4). The second wave's odds are not touched.
- **After release.** A certified trade (`Vocation.Trade`: the first passed program with `trade`) takes 0.10 off `ParoleSystem.ReoffendChance`, on top of what the program counts
  for, and gives a parolee with no job waiting a 50% chance (`ParoleLife.JobInPlaceChance`; with a job waiting it is certain, without a trade and no job waiting none) that the
  "job" term of their parole is in place. The register keeps the trade that found the job (`FormerInmate.tradeJob`, the `ProgramDef`, scribed as a def: the label is read in the language of the day): the term, the parolee's letter and the news from outside
  (`RP_FormerNews_Trade`) name it. The file shows "Certified in tailoring" (`RP_FileStatusTrade`); the reoffend band on the file and in the hearing already reads the lower chance.
- **Hidden information.** Nothing here names what the staff have not learned: the courses, the passes, the sewing and the trade are all things the staff ran or saw; the parole news
  is the register's, which only holds the ones who stay out.
- **Layouts and research.** Both prisons' workshops have a hand tailor bench (the inherited one 1, the penitentiary 2: `PrisonBuilder.Inherited.cs`, `PrisonBuilder.Penitentiary.cs`).
  The bench is vanilla's, and needs vanilla's ComplexClothing research, which every scenario already has done at the start (`ScenPart_PrisonStart.PostGameStart` finishes the vanilla projects).
  The bench is not behind a bureaucracy project.
- **Not built:** other trades (carpentry), a trade that matters to the hearing's speakers, tailors working to a buyer's order, sewn uniforms as things in the world.

### Release, parole, reoffending
Sentence served → release (exit map). Parole hearings at 1/2 sentence: chance from behavior, programs, tension.
Each release is reviewed for reoffending after the departure (see *Release preparation and supervision*; it used to be one roll at the gate); the reoffend rate is a headline number.

**Parole as a scene.** A *hearing room* zone (an office desk and at least one chair in it) turns the midnight roll into a process
(`Story/ParoleScene.cs`): those due are scheduled (a case in `StorySystem.paroleCases`, one hearing per chair per hour between 9:00 and
17:00; the rest are still rolled by the board at midnight, and without a room everything is as it always was). At the board's hours the
inmate is called, a guard walks them in (`RP_EscortToHearing`; a non-maximum-security inmate walks in alone after two hours with no
free guard; the doors on the way open to a maximum-security inmate only on that walk, see Doors; no hearing is due for a supermax inmate), and they sit in a chair before the board (an administrator at the desk, who comes for it, else the warden's chair). The
board's odds are the file's (record, programs, work, tension) plus who speaks (for: the officer who trusts them most, the chaplain for a
believer, their mentor, a goal of proving the board wrong reached; against: the victim's family for a violent crime) plus the reoffend band
(low +5%, high -10%), each stated on the warden's decision letter, which goes to the closest call of the day (the rest are decided at
once). A refusal puts its reasons on the file and in the chronicle (only what the warden can see: a hidden gang or an innocence claim
never enters), and the inmate may set out to *prove the board wrong* (a goal; the make-parole goal turns into it when its last chance is
gone). **Life on parole** (`Story/ParoleLife.cs`): a parolee walks out on terms (a job and an address, in place or still to find) for the
rest of the sentence (5-30 days); the register and the chronicle keep them, news and letters from parolees name them, and a parolee who
is rearrested is arrested for a cause (a new offence, or the term they broke) that every message, the chronicle and the return letter name.

### A restorative meeting (`Story/Amends.cs`, `Defs/Amends.xml`, `Keyed/Amends.xml`; gap analysis 7, item 8)
The victim's family was a dice roll at scheduling (`victimLetter`, 60% for a violent crime, -15% at the board). Now the warden can do something about it, at a risk.
- **Who.** An active inmate inside for a violent crime who has passed a program or holds the new goal *make amends* (`RP_Want_Amends`, rolled for violent inmates, likelier
  for the kind, rare for a psychopath; it forms only where a meeting could be held - a level visited across a table, not supermax, and a visitation room, `Amends.CouldMeet`),
  of a level visited across a table, in a prison with a visitation room, not banned from visits, not on remand for a killing inside, with no hearing in the hearing room and
  none due within a day (`Amends.HearingWithinADay`), and no meeting yet this stay.
- **The proposal.** At 10:00 a counsellor on site who can work (`ProgramSystem.CanTeach`) proposes one meeting a day (the goal's holders first, then the most programs): a
  decision letter (`RP_Decision_Amends`, a day to answer; it goes against the day's budget like any letter; one open per inmate; no standing order). It states the fee ($120),
  the counsellor's hours (about two), the odds and their parts with the proposing counsellor (the best Social on site) at the table, that whichever counsellor sits in counts
  +2% a level of their own Social, the odds should no counsellor make it, the search's -10% when visitors are searched thoroughly at the time, and what each outcome does (the
  tension a good meeting takes off counts the goal reached with it: 30 for a holder of the goal, 20 otherwise). Approve pays the fee (Programs); "Not now" (or no answer) is a
  no, and the counsellor may ask again after 5 days; a goal to make amends takes it as a setback.
- **The meeting.** In visiting hours, with a counsellor on site and the inmate free to be visited at a table, the visitation pass calls the family before the families' own
  visits: a visitor who is no relative of the inmate's (no family need, money or news; their death is no relative's, during the visit or walking out after it - the visit's
  lord is marked, `LordJob_VisitInmate.ForAmends`), who smuggles nothing, searched at the gate as policy says (a thorough search puts them on edge, -10%). The counsellor's
  work (`RP_AttendAmends`, an emergency giver of counselling, after a class) takes them to the table, and back to the family's side if they walked on; the inmate waits for the
  counsellor an hour at the most, then talks without them. The counsellor gets up when the meeting is over, the inmate is gone or a lockdown holds them (`Amends.TableOver`),
  and a class that starts may call them away while the inmate has not sat down yet. A family that leaves without the talk is called again; an approval that waits 3 days
  lapses and is refunded, and so does one whose inmate leaves for good (released, paroled, transferred, dead, escaped: `Amends.Notify_Leaving`, the family sent home).
- **The odds** (`Amends.Odds`, stated): 40%, tension +30% at 0 to -30% at 100, kind +15%, psychopath -25%, bloodlust -10%, abrasive -10%, the counsellor at the table +2% a
  level of Social (none at the table -15%), +5% a program passed (+15% at most), the goal +10%; 5-90%. The decision said "the counsellor's skill and their program record":
  there is no counsellor's record of programs in the code, so the record read is the inmate's (programs passed).
- **Well** (a good roll; a third of those, the family speaks for them): the family's letter is withdrawn (`ParoleCase.victimWithdrawn`, saved; a case not yet assessed is
  marked at once, a later one when scheduled or assessed; a case already assessed - the odds stated, the warden's letter perhaps out - is left as the board saw it, and the
  change counts from the next hearing; the letter says "not the victim's family - they withdrew their letter"), or the family speaks for them at +5%
  (`ParoleScene.VictimFor`); tension -20 (and the goal's -10 with it); the reoffend band a step lower (`Amends.StepDown`: high to the top of medium, medium to the top of low,
  in `ParoleSystem.ReoffendChance`, so the board's band and the supervision case opened at release - its `baseRisk`, read before the stay is cleared - both read it); a good
  thought; the goal reached. The step is a snap to the band below's top, not a cut: right for the board's band (-10% / 0 / +5%), but for the supervision hazard, which
  reads the chance itself, it is worth 1 point to an inmate at the bottom of high (0.45 to 0.44), 20 to one at the top of medium (0.44 to 0.24), at most 11 to a high one
  (0.55 to 0.44) and nothing to a low one. A proportional cut for the hazard (say `baseRisk * 0.7`) would be the alternative - a decision for the owner.
- **Badly**: the family writes against them at every hearing in the room (the letter certain, not 60%); tension +25 (once: a meeting concluded is not concluded again);
  the press hears of it 40% of the time (-2 reputation); a bad thought; the goal lost.
- **Saved**: `CompInmate.amendsState` (`AmendsState`, a byte: none, proposed, approved, meeting, declined, lapsed, withdrawn, supports, hostile) and `amendsTick` (-1), cleared
  with the stay; `ParoleCase.victimWithdrawn`; `LordJob_VisitInmate.amends` (false on old saves: a meeting under way at the load is still known by the inmate's state). The
  hourly pass mends a proposal whose letter is gone and a meeting whose family is gone.
- **Not built**: the victim's family outside a hearing room's hearing (the board's own midnight roll has no letter, as before); a meeting through glass; anything for a
  victim inside (a fellow inmate); a family that comes back.

### A murder trial, and a complaint against an officer
Two stories with a verdict. Everything is kept by id and name (a pawn who dies or leaves just drops out), saved in `StorySystem.trials` and `StorySystem.complaints`, and counted in the statistics.

**A murder trial** (`Story/Trials.cs`). A killing inside no longer adds a sentence on the spot. The killer is charged (`DeathAftermath.ChargeWithMurder`: the murder on their file, a level up if there is a bed, the victim's friends
and gang their enemies as before) and a case opens, a trial four to five days out (always a court morning, ten o'clock: a killing after the court rises is not tried a day early). What the prosecution has is read off the scene at the
moment of the killing: the staff and the inmates in sight (the killer's gang and friends saw it and say nothing; three of each at most), a camera over the spot, a guard within six cells (caught at the scene), the weapon (only a real
one: a punch or a bite is not), the serious offences already on the killer's file. Hidden until the case is assembled a day after the charge: the camera, the weapon, and the inmates who came forward; what the warden knows before is the
officers who saw it. The assembled case goes to the warden as a letter with what it has (witnesses who have since left are not listed) and how strong it looks on that (`Trials.KnownBand`: only what is known, a witness who reported a
threat at half unless they are in protective custody), followed by decision letters: move the inmate witnesses to protective custody (not offered where nobody can be moved: it is locked, full, or they are there already), and ask a known
informant to testify (not offered once they have left). From the day the case is assembled (that is when the killer's gang learns who will testify) until the trial the killer's gang (or the killer, if free), anyone of it in the witness's
own wing and out of the hole and protective custody, gets at inmate witnesses hourly: a threat (pressure 0.5, fear, tension) or one time in four a beating (pressure 1; not of someone already badly hurt, and only when both are out of their
cells: the regime has neither of them in lockup or asleep, a threat can be passed on through a door and a beating cannot; it leaves no trace the warden can
see: no misconduct or assault numbers, no relief of the beater's tension, a generic cause on the witness's file, and no vanilla "harmed me" memory naming the beater: the blow is struck with no instigator); a witness who has not been got at twice (or beaten) holds, and one who trusts the guards may report it, which makes them
and the threat known. Pressure on a witness in protective custody halves every day they are there, so moving them is a real answer even to one who was beaten first. They go back to their own wing after the trial, unless they are vulnerable.
On the day (ten in the morning to five: by video link from the hearing room if there is one; otherwise the accused is kept in their cell as away at court from ten and the verdict comes from four) the court weighs the case.

*Who is on the case.* Staff within twenty cells with a line of sight who are on the payroll and not suspended, but not the temporary staff (the riot police called in for a few hours, who will have gone when the case is assembled).
A second killing by an accused whose case is open is part of that case (its witnesses, camera, weapon and caught-at-the-scene are added; no second case opens), and an earlier murder on the file is a serious offence on the record of a later one.
*A death from the wounds.* The damage patch remembers, on the victim's `CompInmate`, the last inmate who hurt them (`assailantId`, `assaultTick`, `assaultCell`, saved). An inmate who dies of blood loss or of the infection of a wound within a day of that blow is
that inmate's murder (`DeathAftermath.RecentAssailant`: a case opens with the scene read at the cell where the blow was struck), not the inquiry into neglect (which is what a death with no such blow, or a day and more later, still is).
Witnesses the warden may move to protective custody are not themselves accused of a killing.

*Remand.* An open case holds the accused: no sentence is served out (a 12-day sentence, a charge on day 10, and they are still held and the case open on day 12), no parole hearing is due or held, no appeal is heard. An accused who escapes
keeps the case open while the police are looking for them; after the recapture it is tried (on a day at least a day on, if the old one passed). Shipping them out closes the case (they are gone) but costs the warden the reputation an
acquittal lost to witnesses who recanted costs (3), on top of the transfer's own. Vanilla's Release order on a prisoner of an open case is refused with a message (it would have let the case lapse as moot, at no cost), and an order given before the charge
is called off when a guard comes for it. A hostage crisis puts the court's day off (nobody is taken to court or sits in the hearing room while one runs; the day is kept when it is over). Off-site, the court's own lock-up is only what the court put on: a
sanction imposed meanwhile extends the confinement, and the end of the court day leaves that alone (`MurderTrial.courtLockUntil`).

*The odds* (`Trials.Strength`, `Trials.ConvictChance`: convicts from 0.6, never up to 0.4, in between by a roll of (strength - 0.4) / 0.2). Parts: an officer who saw it 0.53 (0.58 when the victim was staff and the witness a colleague), an
inmate who testifies 0.28, the footage 0.4, the weapon 0.15, caught at the scene 0.2 (an officer within six cells), an informant 0.25, the record 0.05 each up to 0.15. A witness who recants (or is gone) counts for nothing; a corrupt
officer on the killer's payroll forgets and counts for nothing too.

| the case | strength | convicts |
|---|---|---|
| one officer who saw it, nothing else | 0.53 | 65% |
| that officer, and the killer caught at the scene (within six cells) | 0.73 | surely |
| that officer, and a camera over the spot | 0.93 | surely |
| that officer and one earlier serious offence on the file | 0.58 | 90% |
| a colleague of a dead officer, nothing else | 0.58 | 90% |
| three inmates who testify (nobody on the staff saw it) | 0.84 | surely |
| two inmates who testify | 0.56 | 80% |
| one inmate who testifies | 0.28 | never |
| three inmates, one of them got at (recants) | 0.56 | 80% |
| three inmates, two or three got at | 0.28 or 0 | never |
| the camera alone | 0.40 | never |
| the camera and the weapon | 0.55 | 75% |
| two inmates and an informant | 0.81 | surely |

Convicted: life or thirty days more, maximum security if there is room. An acquittal takes this case's murder off the file, makes the victim's friends and gang tense (revenge) and costs reputation if the case would have been strong but
witnesses recanted; a staff victim's colleagues feel the verdict either way. A recanting witness, a corrupt officer on the killer's payroll who cannot remember (a trace for internal affairs) and the informant (exposed on the day: known to all,
afraid, wanted by the killer's gang) all come out in the verdict's letter and nowhere before. The open case shows on the killer's file and the Intel tab (what the warden knows), a known witness's file, and the chronicle. With more than one
home map, each map's hourly pass looks after the cases of the accused who are on it.

**A complaint against an officer** (`Story/Complaints.cs`). An inmate hurt by an officer may file one naming them: a restraint that went beyond need (`PunishmentUtility.ResolveSubdue`), a beating, harassment or a lock-up nobody ordered by a
guard with a brutal streak (Bloodlust, Psychopath, Brawler, Abrasive, a demoralised guard; Kind softens it; less often when another officer or a camera sees; `ComplaintSystem.HourlyGuards`), or now and then by any guard on a bad day (low or
no morale, or burnt out), whatever their traits. Not during a riot or a lockdown, and not of someone in a mental state, in protective custody or under lockdown. A lock-up like that is written up through `MisconductUtility.Record` as the offence the warden's policy gives a sanction for (`ComplaintSystem.AbuseOffence`: verbal abuse where the policy has one for it, else the mildest ordinary offence that is not an assault whose
policy is a lock-up: vandalism by default, with its own record, chronicle line, statistics and tension as the real offence has), so the inmate's file and the chronicle show nothing odd, and the abuse is no longer the one sanction nobody would have given.
A beating by a guard is struck with no instigator (vanilla would give the victim a "harmed me" memory naming the officer, which a lie's old injury never has). The state's own chief is never rough with an inmate in a restraint (a complaint against them is refused),
and guards do not abuse while a hostage crisis runs. A manipulative inmate (instigator, jailhouse lawyer, extortionist, psychopath, abrasive) or a vindictive one (volatile, hates the officer) may lie about an officer who only
restrained or searched them, more often when contraband was found. A lie can be about any kind of thing: after a restraint force, a hard word, a beating or a lock-up; after a search or a frisk only a hard word or a beating (never
"when restraining them"); some lies come with an old injury, so neither the officer's traits, nor an injury, nor the file says which complaints are true. A frisk is lied about rarely (about as many lies a day as true complaints at 300
inmates), at most once per inmate per shakedown, and a cell search is complained of only by an owner who was in the cell or near it, the evidence read at the cell. Whether a complaint is true is hidden: the letter, the inmate's file and the
Intel tab say the same either way. The warden decides: *uphold* (a warning, then a day's pay docked (the prison keeps the wages: a saving, not new money, and about once a month at most), then three days at home as a disciplinary suspension of
its own: it is no business of internal affairs, which looks for corruption, so it adds no suspicion and ends with the officer back from suspension; the officer and their colleagues take it to heart; the inmate and the wing see the warden
listen; an uphold with the officer already gone is written up as such), *dismiss* (the inmate's tension rises, and their friends'; a true complaint against an officer who has had a true one before, dismissed, becomes a lawsuit a day or two
later) or *investigate* (one or two days, with the officer stood down on full pay meanwhile, at most two investigations at once, and not the only guard on any hour they work (`CorruptionSystem.CanSpare`: another guard on duty must work every hour they do; the third
upheld complaint's suspension is a day's pay docked instead when they cannot be spared): the camera over the spot, who saw it and the officer's record weigh up, and then the truth comes out as far as
it shows: a true complaint is upheld with the evidence named, a false one is found out (the inmate on report, the officer cleared), and with too little evidence nothing is found, which is a dismissal with a smaller rise in tension, and never a lawsuit: the warden did the responsible thing). An
upheld complaint puts the officer's next promotion off by a week and fades after a month; the count of true complaints that reached the desk (a pattern, which makes a dismissed one a lawsuit and backs a true one in an investigation) is
permanent. Unanswered, a complaint is a soft dismissal: half the rise in tension, and nobody feels ignored.

### Events (storyteller: "The Board")
Prison-only incidents: riot, fight, mass escape plan, smuggling attempt, contraband drop over fence, overdose,
hunger strike, gang war, snitch exposed, celebrity inmate, family tragedy (mood), inspection, press visit, staff
strike, corrupt guard exposed, kitchen fire, outbreak, power failure, storm, lawyer visit, appeal granted,
new legislation (payment changes), riot spreading, hostage crisis (below). No raids, caravans, traders, quests.

**Hostage crisis** (arc `RP_Arc_Hostage`, `Story/Hostage.cs`). A riot's ringleader with a member of staff in reach (at the
riot's start, or each time the ringleader gets at a guard), or an assault in the workshop or the kitchen, seizes that
member of staff at a low chance. The hostage stays a real colonist: a mental state (`RP_BeingHeld`, a held job, no commute, no
guard duty) plus the `RP_Hostage` hediff. The rioters hold the room (the whole wing with five or more of them): the door pass marks the doors on its
border and they refuse staff coming in (those inside may leave), and the map shows the zone as held. The warden is asked
(stand-off): talk them down (a counsellor, a psychologist, an officer the ringleader trusts raise the odds), give the
grievance (a promise, `DemandSystem.Accept`), cut power and water, call the riot police to storm it (the HUD button does the
same during a crisis), or wait. The first deadline hurts the hostage and asks again; the last kills them (the staff death
path) or the hostage-takers lose their nerve. After it: the hostage's long memory and wariness (and resigning "over the
danger"), the colleagues' `RP_ColleagueHurt`, the ringleader charged (kidnapping, or capital murder) and moved up a level,
a chronicle milestone naming the officer. Chances and timings are constants in `HostageCrisis` and `HostageArc`.

### Grading & reputation
Daily report card A–F per area; inspections compare. Reputation changes payment multipliers and intake mix.

### Pay by the grade (`Economy/PerDiem.cs`, `Keyed/PerDiem.xml`; gap analysis 7, W1)
- **The factor.** At midnight the state pays each inmate's `dailyPayment` (`SupermaxSystem.DailyPayment`) times `PrisonGame.PaymentMultiplier`, which is now `BasePaymentMultiplier` (reputation's 0.85-1.15, an event's modifier, special measures' cut, as before) times `PerDiem.GradeFactor`: the letter of the mean of the last 7 grades of `gradeHistory` - A x1.15, B x1.05, C x1, D x0.9, F x0.75 (`PrisonGrading.Letter`'s edges: 85, 70, 55, 40). Fewer than 7 grades: the mean of those there are; none: 1. An empty prison's day is not graded (`NoteGrade`), so it is not in the mean.
- **When.** From day 8 of the game as every screen counts it (`PerDiem.Applies`: `TicksGame >= PerDiem.FirstTick`, 7 x 60,000 ticks - the day `TicksGame / TicksPerDay + 1` of the Overview's title, the ledger's When and the chronicle). The settlement runs at local midnight and the screens' day turns at the hour the game began (06:00), so in a new game the first midnight that pays by the grades is the 8th (tick 465,000, which the title calls day 8) and the seven before it pay the plain rate; the desk says "from day 8" until the title does. Nothing is saved: the clock and the grade history hold all it reads. An old save pays by the grades it has from its next midnight; one still in its first week waits for day 8, like a new game (the user's call, round 2: telling an old save from a new one would take a saved field, and it costs at most six days of the factor once).
- **Which grades.** The payment is the first thing the settlement does, before the day that just ended is graded, so a midnight pays by up to seven cards before it (the first graded midnight has seven, days 1-7, the first of them the partial day from 06:00; fewer when a day was not graded); the desk shows the factor of the history as it stands, which is exactly what the next midnight pays. The mean is shown rounded down (84.6 reads 84, a B), so it never shows a letter's edge it is short of.
- **Where it shows.** The desk's (Overview) grade tooltip: "+$120 a day from your B - the state's per-diem follows the mean of the last 7 days' grades (77, so +5% on every inmate's rate)", the plain rate for a C (the weekly report too), "from day 8" in the first week, and the scale (`PerDiem.DeskLine`, built with the overview's card, not per frame). The ledger's per-diem line (Finance) keeps its category and gets a note: "24 inmates, +$120 for the week's B (+5%)" (the big plan's prison below) (`PerDiem.LedgerNote`; the share is the payment with the factor less the payment without, `PerDiem.GradeShare`). The weekly report has a line under the money. The HUD's multiplier ("Rep 55 · x1.05") is the whole one, grades included.
- **How big it is** (census logs in `rim-prison-wt/logs`; full days only - a run's first book holds the setup's grants and its last ends when the run stops). A mid-size prison (`census-w-big.log`, the big plan, 22-24 inmates, grades 83, 82, 75, 75, 73: a B), its four full days: the per-diem is $2,380-2,440 a day of $2,400-4,060 income, against $2,930-3,150 going out (wages $890, supplies $1,070-1,200, utilities $160, programs, laundry), so a day nets -$540 to +$1,110, $330 on average. The B is worth about +$120 a day; an A would be +$360, a D -$240, an F -$605. A whole week of A against F is about $6,800, some twenty average days' net - but under a tenth of the $86-90k the prison sits on. The penitentiary (`census-c81-sc.log`, 42-43 inmates, grades 79, 77, 73: a B), its two full days: the per-diem is $5,500-5,900 a day ($6,100 at the next midnight), the B +$300, an A +$900, an F -$1,500, against a day's net of $420-750. So an F costs more than an ordinary day nets in both prisons: it turns three of the big prison's four full days into a loss and every one of the penitentiary's, and an A about doubles an average day's net. The grade moves the daily margin; it does not empty the bank. The sinks that give the money a use (the riot reckoning's bill and restitution, the unit officers, the items still to come) are separate features.

### Balance (the numbers the test plans tune, and why)
A prison that is staffed and fed holds for a week; a neglected one does not. What the 2026-10-20 pass set, with the reason (measured in the showcase, huge, normal, stress and events plans; WORKLOG "Balance"):
- **Meals.** Food falls 1.6 a day (0.067 an hour), so a full stomach is below 15% ("urgently hungry") after 12.75 hours and empty after 15. Every level that walks to the canteen has a meal slot at least every 11 hours
  (a regression check reads the defs): supper is at 21 (it was 19, a 12-hour night: every well inmate was urgently hungry between two and seven in the morning), so Minimum, Medium and Maximum eat at 07, 12 and 21,
  protective custody at 07, 13 and 21 (it had 08 and 17, a 15-hour night, and its six inmates were urgently hungry every morning). A saved game keeps the schedule it has.
  An inmate who is shut in a cell (or has no cell to be in), below 25% and with no way to walk to a canteen counter (the doors are shut for the night, or the hole moved them to a wing with no way to a counter) is fed in the cell like
  the cell-meal levels, or where they stand when they have no cell to be in (a bed wrecked in a riot: one stood in the supermax wing's corridor and starved for days) (`WorkGiver_DeliverCellMeal.StrandedBelow`). "Stranded" is the walk
  (`CanWalkToCanteen`: a cell of a canteen zone they can reach, kept per district for a tenth of an hour), not whether a meal happens to lie on a counter: it first read "no meal on any counter", which is true between cooking batches and
  whenever every stack is reserved, so every inmate under 25% anywhere (the yard, work, the canteen queue) counted, and half the cooks left the stoves to carry one meal each while the counters stayed empty. The food search for such an inmate
  is the cell for an inmate in a cell, a few cells (`FoodNear`) for one outside: not the whole outdoors (the cheap gates run first). The cell-meal levels, the punished and a lockdown are brought a meal below 50% (it was 40%: the condemned were urgently hungry at 19:00
  before it came). A meal is 0.9 of an adult's stomach (MealSimple and MealFine give 0.9 of 1.0), not half of it, so a meal that lies in the inmate's own cell is eaten once it fits (`MealFits`: at most about 20%, a fifth of a meal spilled over at the most;
  `JobGiver_InmateUrgentNeeds`), not at 30%, which lost a fifth of every cell meal and needed a fifth more meals from the kitchen; the urgent errand starts at 15%, so every cell meal used to leave the condemned and the punished urgently hungry for the hour it took.
- **Food stock.** The penitentiary's first fill holds a day of raw food as well as the meals (600 potatoes and 600 rice, all of it: the builder asked for 300 and 150 and placed one stack of 75 each, 15 meals;
  40 meals in the dry store, not 10). The restock keeps 60 potatoes an inmate (it was 25: under a day of cooking, so a shuttle called off for a day or two while hostiles stand off the prison starved the kitchen) and at least 300.
- **Tunnels.** A dug hour adds 0.004-0.009 of a tunnel (0.02-0.04 before), twice that for an escape artist (`TunnelSystem.DigPerHourMin/Max`): at ten dug hours a night about two weeks, a week for an artist, and
