  not inside three days even in a cell locked around the clock (death row's) for an artist (before: two to four nights, finished in two of six runs; an interim 0.005-0.011 let an artist dig through in 72 hours). That last case is about three days for the condemned artist
  (72 hours x 0.013 is about 0.94, finishing near hour 77): "days, not hours", not a week. A tunnel past 25% can be noticed by the guards without a tip: 1% + 2% x progress per dug hour,
  which sets the cell-search flag a tip, a phone tap and the dog already set (`TunnelSystem.NoticeChance`), but only with someone there to notice it: a guard within earshot of the cell, or a camera or tower over it (`TunnelSystem.SomeoneToNotice`; an event nobody saw is not recorded,
  and the flag shows in the Intel tab). That removes some of the notices of a wing with no cover and the night guards elsewhere: such tunnels finish more often than the earlier 11% / 33% estimates. A search finds it 85% of the time as before, informants tip it from 15%, the dog from 15%.
- **Compliance.** `RegimePlacement.InExpectedPlace` expects the cell for a slot that lists no zones, and Work lists none: every inmate with a job looked out of place at work, and so did anyone in a class,
  at a service, at a hearing or in a medical bed (the chapel and the classroom share one job). Work now counts in the job's zone (or doing the job), the regime's own places count
  (`RegimePlacement.Sanctioned`), and a downed inmate is left out of the reading (`RegimePlacement.InTheSample`; first counted as in place, which paid a violent prison's "order" grade for the fights that down its inmates: with 200-700 downed inmate-hours a week that was up to ten
  points; the health grade already holds the injured against the prison). The measured compliance (the grade's "order" is 110 x this) rose from 58-70% to 78-86% (with the downed in place); the grade's weight on it was
  tuned against the old reading, not changed (110 stays: re-tuning it is a decision for after a few weeks of readings on the corrected sample, see WORKLOG "Review fixes"). The longer walks of the redesigned inherited facility are a small part of the difference, and the Eat/Shower slots, where walks matter, read 70-100% in place.
- **The harsh levels' day.** At the end of a week the unrest sits in Maximum, protective custody, supermax and death row: mood 0.03-0.15, tension 80-100, hygiene 0.0-0.2 and joy 0.0-0.1 (the level's one shower slot is
  often missed: punished, in lockup, in the hole), and the two top mood penalties of the whole prison are "filthy" and "recreation-starved". Maximum has a second shower slot (18:00, in a lockup hour) and protective
  custody a second (19:00) and a third free hour (16:00; it had two free hours and one wash in the day). Death row and supermax are left as they were (no shower in the wing; a guard-led walk for supermax).
- **Cleanliness.** Free time starts with a wash below 50% hygiene (it was 30%: the thoughts are -2 grimy at 55%, -5 dirty at 35%, -10 filthy at 15%, and a one-hour shower slot a day left most inmates at 20-45% by day three).
- **The injured and the downed.** Downed inmates lay on the floor for hours (200-700 inmate-hours a week) and the injured in bed went hungry: most of the urgent hunger of a week's run was inmates on bed rest, with
  food in the cell and nobody to feed them. The doctors now carry the downed as well (`RP_RescueInmateDoctor`; the guards alone are on patrol, in fights and in searches), not as an emergency and under vanilla's rescue of a downed colonist (priority 55 against 60: a downed cook or guard
  is carried first), and a doctor who is not a guard does not walk into a riot or to a downed inmate with a fight within six cells (`WorkGiver_RescueInmate.FightNear`; unless the order is forced). A third of the doctors work nights, from three doctors up (with two, half would, and the day would have one;
  `ShiftSystem.IsMedic`, the same night-shift policy as the guards, and a doctor who is away keeps their shift: the day staff go home at 22 and four night guards were all that fed, tended and carried), and the penitentiary has a sickbay for the staff
  in the command suite: hospital beds set for prisoners take no colonist, and the infirmary's and the ward's all are, so a downed member of staff had no bed to be rescued to and lay where they fell (a workman
  bled out after a day, two cooks were down for two days and the kitchen stopped). The beds stand in the suite itself, which a medical bed makes a Hospital room (the dining table, armchairs, couch and bookcase lose their room roles for the staff, and a patient's infection risk is that room's cleanliness): a room of both kinds is not a prison cell,
  and a walled-off corner of the suite is the way to give the beds a room of their own (not done: the task kept the prison layouts; the README screenshots of the suite need a mention).
- **Stability round 4 (2026-10-21): who is carried where, and what may cut a carry short.** (No number changed; the reasons are the point.) Vanilla's wardens carry a downed inmate (or escort one who has no bed they can reach) to the closest bed a *guard* can reach and
  the inmate claims it (`WorkGiver_Warden_TakeToBed`, `RestUtility.FindBedFor`): it knows no wings, and a guard walks through the supermax unit's staff-only door. A minimum security inmate (the one who died of hunger in the showcase), a death row inmate and a protective custody inmate woke up in a
  supermax cell (the same cell's bed every time in the logs), behind a door they cannot open, further than the walk limit from any counter, and starved. `BedWings` (a postfix on `RestUtility.IsValidBedFor`) now offers an inmate only what `IntakeSystem.FreePrisonerBeds` always did: their own
  bed (and the one they go back to), a medical bed, a bed of their level's wing or of no wing (a wing-only level only its wing), in a wing for their sex, a solitary bed only when a punishment is due. And `DeliverFood`, `TakeWoundedPrisonerToBed` and `EscortPrisonerToBed` are not casually interruptible
  (`Patches/CareJobs.xml`; vanilla reads the flag only to keep a pawn on such a job from chatting): a class calling its teacher (`ProgramSystem.CallTeacher`, 2-11 times in a showcase run), the shift change (`StaffCommute.TrySendHome` dropped the meal in the cook's hands) and a staff break used to cut a meal or a
  carry short, and the inmate lay where they fell with the claim on their bed undone, or went unfed. What is left of the showcase's hunger is the documented limit (a night of riot, with every guard on the one who is down or berserk, the injured in bed with nobody free). The care reserve (*A protected care detail*) is the lever for it: a chosen number of guards kept for care, at the cost of fewer for the trouble.
  The escort or carry to a bed (`JobDriver_TakeToBed`) clears every claim the inmate holds as it starts; an inmate on their feet whose job held one has that job ended with it (stability round 24,
  `Patch_JobDriver_TakeToBed_TakeeLosesTheJobWithItsClaims`: a bedless inmate walked on to a meal holding nothing, a doctor's feeding took it, and the pick-up failed to reserve it). What they do next claims again.
  The checks that follow: a bedless inmate counts at the showcase's end only after three hourly looks without a bed (a riot breaks beds in the last hour or two, the hourly pass gives a bed); a stranded supermax inmate's looks count only when a guard could have come.
- **Tried and dropped: the cooks feeding the injured in bed.** In huge, where every guard is in a riot, the injured waited unfed; with the cooks allowed to spoon-feed them (within the half-of-the-cooks limit) two or three
  of six were walking trays to the wards all day, and the counters never held more than 10-30 meals in a week where they had held 170 (hunger 603 inmate-hours against 229-516). The kitchen is the scarce hand.
- **Not changed, and why** (reported in WORKLOG): hygiene for death row (the layout has no shower for the wing), the kitchen's walking (a cook spends a third of the shift walking to ingredients and hauling
  meals, so production only just keeps up with a week's eating), the mood penalties of a staff death, and the injured in bed who are not fed for hours when no guard or doctor is free.
- **After the reviews** (WORKLOG "Review fixes"): the stranded-meal rule, the cell meal's fit, the tunnel notice, the compliance sample, the doctors' rescue and the night doctors are as described above. Three things came with the stability review that
  the Balance section does not cover: a staff break does not take the parole board out of a hearing that is sitting (`StaffBreaks.Busy`, `ParoleScene.SittingNow`: on the sitting job, or at the hearing room's desk while a case is seated, an administrator is left in the chair),
  the escorts of an arrival and of a transfer are not cut short by a casual interruption (a break at the hour's change took the guard off the walk, as the supermax and parole escorts never were), and `DebtSystem.Huddles` takes a pair off the list only when it is
  a table (three or more): a pair that was thrown away took with it the neighbour who stood with two others, so a card game started less often than it should.

### The state shuttle (replaces PA's bus and trucks; `Shuttle/*.cs`, `Defs/Shuttle.xml`, `Intake/Intake.cs`, `Economy/Supply.cs`)
- **Pieces, all Core**: `RP_StateShuttle` (a child of Core's `ShuttleBase`, the state's blue over Core's hull `Things/Building/Misc/Shuttle`; a plain `Building_StateShuttle`
  that holds the arrivals still aboard, no transporter, no pilot) and the skyfallers `RP_ShuttleIncoming` / `RP_ShuttleLeaving` (children of Core's `ShuttleSkyfallerBase`; Royalty's flight curves
  copied, Core's sounds), driven by two `Skyfaller` subclasses. Royalty's `ShuttleIncoming`/`ShuttleLeaving` defs, its `TransportShipDef`, its landing beacon and vanilla's `TransportShip`/`CompShuttle` jobs are not used: they need
  Royalty's world object (`TravelingShuttle`) and assume a shuttle the colony owns. So the mod works with no DLC (verified with a mod list of Core + Harmony + this mod).
- **The pad**: `RP_ShuttlePad`, a flat 9x7 building (not an edifice, walkable, 45 steel, concrete with painted lines: `tools/gen-art.py`) in the Prison tab of the Architect menu, not under a roof or over
  walls. The shuttle lands centred on the nearest free pad to holding. A pad is *blocked* by a pawn, an item or a building (not the pad) on the hull's 5x3 or the ramp's cell: the visit waits in orbit for it
  (`Holding`; a message and the "pad blocked" alert name what blocks it once it has lasted ten seconds, 600 ticks, so a passer-by is not announced), and after an hour lands nearby. A pawn who walks under the hull as it comes down makes it hover (up to 600 ticks);
  at the touchdown pawns are stepped out (only to a cell they could walk to from where they stood, never through a wall: `ShuttleSystem.ClearSite`) and items moved aside: nobody is crushed. A pad that is *unusable* (a roof over the hull or the ring round it, which the
  skyfaller would bring down, or no way to it from holding) is no landing site and does not wait: the shuttle sets down on open ground and `Alert_RP_PadUnusable` says why.
  Without a usable pad (or when it stays blocked an hour) it sets down on the nearest clear open ground near holding (`FallbackSite`, on the prison's grounds first and, with none clear there, just beside them: see the grounds; 5x3 plus a ring, unroofed, no water or buildings, reachable, outside the painted zones if it can; plants and
  filth do not rule a spot out, the landing clears them as a vanilla skyfaller's does). The spot stays the site while the ground stays open (pawns crossing it do not move reception), and with none found the sweep of the map is made once an hour, never every minute: the visit holds
  with its own reason (`ShuttleHold.NoSite`: "no pad and no clear ground", a letter, its own status line) and is called off after six hours like a weather hold. An alert says to build a pad (`Alert_RP_NoPad`; the welcome letter, the Intake tab, Deployment and
  the zone texts say it too). The starter facility comes with a pad outside the gate.
- **The daily shuttle** is called at the intake hour (the old once-a-day rule, `IntakeSystem.ShuttleDue`): the roster is made when it leaves orbit (`IntakeSystem.GenerateRoster`: policy and free beds as before, special
  measures cap it, and so does what can step off in the stay: `ShuttleSystem.MaxRoster`, 49), it falls (200-250 ticks), stays on the ground two hours and flies away whether or not everyone boarded. One passenger steps off every 75 ticks; the intake grant,
  the chronicle lines and the search in holding are as before. *It does not lift off mid-unload*: with passengers or crates still to set down when the stay ends it stays (boarding stays open meanwhile, one message) up to a hard cap of two hours more
  (`HardDepartTick`). Boarding is open for as long as the shuttle is staying on (`ShuttleSystem.BoardingOpenFor`/`Staying`): the lift-off keeps being put back, and a rule that closed boarding 200 ticks before it would shut it for one tick each time (pawns tick before the
  game components), failing every boarding walk in progress. Pad duty is possible while it stays, and the status line and the HUD say it is unloading, not "lifting off in 0.1 h". A crate that cannot be set down anywhere stays aboard, paid for, and goes back with the lift-off; the unloading gives up on
  the rest (`ShuttleVisit.cargoBlocked`) rather than hold the shuttle up for it. What was set down before the cap is delivered all the same (the message, the contraband roll). The cargo crew's package goes only to someone who is not under `RingSystem.SupplyCut`. What is left at the cap goes back, nothing is lost (`ShuttleSystem.EndVisit`, also for a visit lost to a vanished skyfaller or map): undelivered orders return to `SupplySystem.pending` (ready at once, delivered by the next shuttle; they were paid for),
  passengers who never stepped off wait in the world for the next day's roster, which takes them before generating anyone new (`ShuttleSystem.carriedOver`), and the grant of those who did step off is booked, with the admissions (counted as each steps off), once
  the unloading is over or the visit ends first. `IntakeSystem.RunIntake` is for the developer button and the tests: the day's inmates admitted at once with no shuttle.
- **Arrivals**: spawned at the ramp (`IntakeSystem.AdmitArrival`), a bed picked as they step off. With a holding zone each is an `ArrivalEntry`: they wait by the ramp for a guard (`WorkGiver_EscortArrival`, one guard
  to an inmate, Warden work, above frisking; not for one in a mental break), who walks them to holding (`JobDriver_EscortArrival`); the inmate walks to the cell the guard is taking them to, a `Goto` at a walk, at the guard's pace (as in a parole hearing; a follow job
  that is done when they stand beside the guard was handed out again in the same tick); with no guard free in 1,800 ticks they walk on their own (a clock of its own, `ArrivalEntry.walkOnTick`; the ten hours to be inside run on `sinceTick`). No guard is sent for an arrival who cannot reach holding (a locked gate). Without a
  holding zone they walk to their cells as always. `CompInmate.inTransit` (not saved, rebuilt on load) opens the doors to an arrival: any that is not locked, held by rioters or into a staff room, and a staff-only one only if it is the way out to the
  open (so a gate set to staff only does not strand them outside the fence, and a starving arrival does not walk into the kitchen). An arrival still outside the perimeter ten hours on (no holding zone and no free bed, a locked gate, a break at the pad)
  stays on the way in with the doors open to them and is reported (`Alert_RP_StuckArrivals`); once inside (`ShuttleSystem.IsInside`) the walk ends.
- **Cargo**: orders (manual, restock, blueprint materials) are paid on ordering and ready two hours later (the truck's delay, `SupplyOrder.arriveTick`). The daily shuttle takes every order that is ready when it leaves orbit.
  *A cargo run between the daily shuttles*: decided yes, because blueprint materials and food that wait a whole day stall construction and the kitchen; but batched, so the pad is not busy all day: a run is called when 80 items
  are ready (or the oldest ready order has waited four hours), the last run was six hours ago (at most four a day) and the next daily shuttle is more than three hours off; it stays one hour. Orders aboard count as ordered (`SupplySystem.PendingCount`, so restocking does not
  order twice) and a cancelled blueprint refunds them even aboard, the crates not yet set down only (an order's price is spread over its stacks as they are unloaded). Unloaded stack by stack (30 ticks) in the Deliveries zone if one is painted, else at the pad's edge beside
  the margin kept clear round the ramp; never on the ramp or in that margin (`KeepClear`), so the next shuttle finds the pad free.
- **Exports** (decided: *counted as loaded when in the Exports zone at lift-off*, not hauled to the pad: inmate workers already stock that zone and the crew loads it while the shuttle waits): `LaborUtility.SellExports` now runs when
  any shuttle lifts off (it was the morning run at seven); export contracts count their runs as before (a second run the same day adds to it). With no shuttle that day, the goods wait.
- **Mail** (decided: *not on the shuttle*): letters are records in `MailSystem.waiting`, composed at midnight and sorted in the mailroom, not things; sacks as items would mean rewriting the sorting for no gain.
- **Out**: a release (`ReleaseSystem.Release`, also vanilla's own Release order, via a postfix on `GenGuest.PrisonerRelease`) and a transfer (`InmateUtility.TransferOut`) register an `OutboundEntry` (released / paroled /
  transferred). *On the way out* is one predicate, `ShuttleSystem.OnTheWayOut` (released flag or an entry), and such a pawn is not one of the prison's inmates: not released a second time (`SentenceServed`, `Release`), not paid for, not counted in the intake's room, the report
  card or a gang's leadership, no gizmos or Inmates-tab commands (the file only; no second transfer), no rearrest while they are still on the map (it waits; for one who died waiting it is dropped). While one is outstanding the pawn's think tree is `RP_Outbound`
  (`ThinkNode_ConditionalOutbound` before vanilla's "exit the map if released", which would walk them off): to the ramp and aboard (`JobDriver_BoardShuttle`) if the shuttle is down and boarding is open (it closes 200 ticks before lift-off), else, as any prisoner,
  food when hungry, rest when exhausted, shelter in a bad temperature (vanilla's own givers called from `JobGiver_Outbound`), else waiting in holding if the prison has one (indoors, under a roof; they go out to the pad when the shuttle lands), else in reception beside the pad
  (or beside the place it sets down, off the hull's spot; nowhere to walk to: they wait where they are; the site is swept for by the landing and by the hourly pass when there is no pad, and read from the cache by everyone else: reception, a transfer's way out). One of the colony's own who
  was held here is a free colonist again on release and is not sent anywhere. A transfer that walks out for want of a shuttle is discarded like one that boards, not freed. Passengers carried over for a level that wants no new inmates are taken back by the state (not piled up). A release counts when they leave the map (a prefix on `Pawn.ExitMap`: `stats.released` and the reoffence that was rolled with it, `stats.reoffended`, at the same moment; a transfer was
  counted when ordered); vanilla's own Release order is registered *uncounted* (no release for the reform grade, no reoffence roll: decided not to block it for lifers and death row, the warden's order stands, it simply does not count); the rest of the release bookkeeping
  (former inmates, parole, rearrest rolls) happens when it is ordered, as before. A transfer leaves the world as a transfer (despawned and discarded), not as a freed prisoner. One who misses the shuttle waits for the next.
  A death while waiting is *not a death in custody* (decided: the prison has let them go): no `deaths`, no safety grade, no mourning, no state inquiry or fine; a chronicle line says it. A reoffender coming back (`Reoffenders.Return`) leaves the list.
  *A transfer is escorted*: when the shuttle is down and boarding, a transferred inmate waits (in holding or reception, where they were) for a guard: `WorkGiver_EscortTransfer` (Warden work, one guard to an inmate, not for one in a mental break or who cannot reach the ramp) gives the
  guard `RP_EscortTransfer`, which meets them, sets `OutboundEntry.escortId` and walks to the ramp (a walk, at the pace of the inmate: the guard walks the inmate's own way, 6 steps ahead of them on their path (`JobDriver_EscortTransfer.GoalFor`, read every 10 ticks by `LeadAtTheirPace`), to the ramp itself once the inmate has 7 steps or fewer to go, and stays with an inmate who stands still away from the ramp for up to 1500 ticks; it was "stop while the inmate is more than 6 cells behind in a straight line to the ramp, go on within 3", which sent the guard round one side of a block and the slow inmate round the other, 16 steps apart, in a third of the runs; 6 stays the number, its unit is steps of the inmate's path now, and the 3 is not read; the cell is the goal, and how the guard walks to it is judged once per goal (`Judge`): one it cannot reach sends it its own way to the ramp, and one its own way reaches only by going round - more than 3 steps longer than walking to the inmate and 7 on along their way (`WayGoesRound`, `GoesRoundBy`), because the pathfinder weighs a cell's cost against the walker's pace and a quick guard's cheapest way can go round the doors or rough ground a slow inmate walks through - has the guard follow the inmate to touch instead, until its way to the goal is along theirs again (stability round 24: handed the cell, a guard walked round the block away from a slow transfer, 22 steps apart)); a lame inmate was walked to the ramp by a guard 20 cells ahead); the inmate's own job is a `Goto` at a walk to the spot the guard is making for (`ShuttleSystem.TransferWalk`, read by `JobGiver_Outbound`), never a follow
  job (the arrivals' lesson), and within three cells of the ramp they board with the guard beside them; the guard's job ends when they are aboard. A walk cut short (the guard sent elsewhere) leaves them waiting at the ramp, where their own walk ended (it goes on to the spot the guard was making for), for the next guard; the two walk at the same pace by different routes (the guard paths to Touch), so one can come aboard before the other is beside them. On a cargo run (`CargoStayTicks`, with boarding closing 200 ticks before lift-off) a transfer that waited the half hour for a guard can miss the run, and catches the next shuttle. With no guard for half an hour of the stay
  (`EscortWaitTicks` from landing) they go on alone, so a prison with no free guard still sends them. The released are not escorted: they are no one's to lead.
  *The marshals' prisoners come on the shuttle*: a returning ex-inmate (`Reoffenders.Hourly`) and a recaptured escapee (`FugitiveSystem.Hourly`) who are due, with a free bed of their sex in their level, are not put on the map at the hour: they join `ShuttleSystem.marshals`
  (`MarshalEntry`: the pawn, returning or recaptured, why, when the escapee got away; kept in the world meanwhile). `IntakeSystem.GenerateRoster` takes them first, before anyone new (nobody is generated for them, the special measures' cap does not hold them back or count them), when there is
  a bed for them (or overcrowding is allowed); they step down the ramp like everyone and `IntakeSystem.AdmitArrival` runs their return at the ramp (`Reoffenders.Return`, `FugitiveSystem.Return`: the longer sentence, the second stay, the letter and the chronicle line), with an arrival line
  of their own (`RP_MarshalsArrival_*`), no intake grant (the state pays nothing for the return) and a wait for a guard as an arrival. A shuttle that leaves before they have stepped off leaves them on the list (not in `carriedOver`: they would come back as new inmates, through the intake
  and not through the return); one who has waited six days is sent to another prison (a message and a chronicle line name them: `RP_MarshalsGaveUp`; the hourly pass runs it, and the Intake tab's count leaves such a one out). The hourly queueing checks for a free bed of their sex but reserves none: with several due and one bed free, all are queued, and without overcrowding the rest wait their turn or the six days. The escapee's open murder case still waits for the recapture (`FugitiveSystem.Tracks` reads the list too). `IntakeSystem.RunIntake` (developer button, tests) lands them at once.
- **Stories**: a shuttle can be *delayed* (a thunderstorm, hard snow or toxic fallout: held in orbit, a letter, it comes down as soon as the weather allows, called off after six hours: a letter, no arrivals that day, the supplies stay on order) or by a *threat*
  (hostile armed people near the prison) or by having *no pad and no ground* (see the pad); special measures that send nobody (limit 0) write a letter (every five days at most). A *stowaway*: a few hundred ticks after the daily shuttle lands
  (`StowawayDelayTicks`, once the guards on pad duty have had time to take their posts), one time in eight an inmate within 30 cells who can get there (bashing doors; not the condemned, nor anyone a guard is walking to a hearing) runs for the ramp (`RP_Escaping`); whether a guard
  on the pad stops them is decided when they get there (a seen misconduct, a line); with nobody there they board: an escape with a line and a letter, and no passenger.
  *Contraband in the cargo*: one time in eight, when cargo is unloaded, the cargo crew is the outside supplier of a smuggling ring or a dealer (`RingSystem.Notify_BroughtIn` with the crew as the supplier, unknown to the warden):
  a guard on pad duty finds it three times in four (a find on the map, `FindSource.Cargo`, the supplier learned), otherwise it reaches the inmate unfound (no statistics row counts what got past the guards: it would tell the warden something did). *Pad duty* (Deployment, 1 guard by default, up to 3): guards with
  nothing more urgent stand watch beside the ramp while the shuttle is down (`WorkGiver_PadDuty`, below arrival escorts), at the cost of their rounds.
- **UI**: the HUD corner counts the hours to the shuttle, or says where it is (landing, down for N hours, held, leaving); the shuttle's inspector shows the same; Intake tab and Deployment show the pad; alerts: no pad, a pad that cannot be used (roofed, cut off), a blocked pad, no beds for the next shuttle (the hours before it comes), new inmates stuck outside;
  statistics: landings, cargo runs, landings off the pad, shuttles called off, stowaways, cargo contraband; the summary line of the tests: `shuttles=landings/cargo/offPad/cancelled`.
- **Tests** (`Testing/ShuttleChecks.cs` and `Testing/ShuttleRegressions.cs`, last in the events plan; real ticks on a clear stage, each rolls back; a pawn handed a job again and again in one tick fails the check it happened in, `JobLoopErrors`): the pad is in the prison tab, goes on open ground only and its blueprint orders and refunds its steel; lands on the pad and unloads inmates who reach holding (a guard walking one of them, as a goto at a walk); with no holding zone they walk to their cells; without a pad it lands near holding and the alert shows;
  cargo is delivered (deliveries zone, pad); released and paroled inmates wait in holding, board, are counted once; a missed boarding waits for the next shuttle; a blocked pad holds it, then it lands, or lands nearby after an hour,
  and nobody under it is crushed; weather holds it and then calls it off; stowaways (stopped at the ramp by a guard, or aboard); cargo contraband; pad duty. The review's findings each have a check that fails without the fix: released once, nothing left of an inmate on the way out,
  needs and death while waiting, the stay stretched for the unloading and what is sent back at the cap, the stowaway rolled after the guards are posted, plants and the stable open-ground site, no pad and no ground, a roofed pad, a stuck arrival, the release counters, crates clear of the ramp,
  the landing's moves, the doors for arrivals, and the small ones (refund, mental break, transfer, sealed in, pad-blocked message). The saveload plan seeds a shuttle on the pad mid-unload (one passenger down, more aboard, cargo to unload, an arrival on the way, a
  transfer waiting, a carried-over passenger) and compares it after the reload. Normal, stress, big and huge fail if no shuttle landed in two days or someone is stuck on the way in or waiting days to leave.
  `tools/uitour.sh` has four shuttle steps (in the air, unloading, cargo, lift-off; `RIMPRISON_TOUR=shuttle` runs just those) that tick the game by hand and stop at the moment shown.
### The state steps in (special measures, its security chief, flagship)
The softer failure state, and its mirror. No hard game over (the user's call); always on, no flag, and said so in the welcome letter.
Everything is `Story/SpecialMeasures.cs`; its state is the scribed `StateOversight` in `StorySystem.oversight`, counted in `Ledger.CurrentDay`s.
- **Trigger** (midnight, after the day's grade and the loan's night): three daily grades in a row below C, or deaths plus escapes in the last seven
  days at the limit (a tenth of the inmates, at least five), or a state loan in default (five nights in the red while it is owed). Not while measures
  run, or in the grace (5 days) after they were lifted or the chief came. With the state's chief in post the same record is not shielded but tightened (below).
  An "The state is watching" alert comes first: two bad days, the week within two of the limit, a loan two nights short of default.
- **The term**: seven days, an overseer (a visiting `RP_Overseer` staff pawn, the `leaveAtTick` mechanism of the riot police; it tours the yard and
  canteen, comes at the start, half way (with the inspector's score, `Inspections.Score`) and at the review; an extended term counts its half way from the
  extension). Three targets: safety C or better on two daily reports running, no escapes since the term began, and what brought the state in put right
  (the overall grade back at C on two reports, the week's deaths and escapes under the limit, the loan out of default). Restrictions: the shuttle's new inmates capped at two a day
  (none once extended; it still lands for the supplies and the released), the state pays 10% less per inmate (`PrisonGame.PaymentMultiplier`), armed response held at escapes only
  (`ArmedResponse.Effective`), solitary capped at 24 hours (`PunishmentPolicy.SolitaryHoursFor`, and `PunishmentUtility.HoursToServe` for a sentence already due:
  only solitary is capped, not the lighter sanctions), and no land is sold (`PrisonGrounds.ParcelFor`); the policy screen shows what the warden set and what is in force, and the warden's own settings are not overwritten.
- **Outcomes**: targets met, lifted with praise, a $3,000 grant and +5 reputation; missed once, extended seven days with no new inmates sent (a
  decision letter lets the warden ask for the chief at once instead, which gains nothing: the term ends, the restrictions stay, the chief and the lost project come);
  missed again, the state installs a security chief and the bureaucracy loses its latest finished project (`BureaucracySystem.LoseLatest`: the one no other
  finished project, and not the one in progress, builds on). Failing is worse than being under measures: the term ends but the restrictions do not (`StateOversight.underChief`,
  `SpecialMeasures.Restricted`: no new inmates sent, payments cut, armed response and solitary limited, no land sold) for as long as the chief is the state's. A chief who is lost (killed,
  vanished) is replaced at the next midnight with the week of B grades counted afresh; a failing record with the chief in post costs another project (then 5 days grace).
  The chief is a real staff member (`RP_SecurityChief`, $120 a day, guard duties) flagged `statePosted`: cannot be let go (`StaffUtility.Fire` refuses, the gizmos are
  locked; taken hostage they get the hostage-crisis button like anyone), cannot be drafted (`Pawn_DraftController` patches), is not promoted, trained or sent on strike, is left
  out of the staff stories and of corruption, and sets the patrols (the warden's patrol zones are not used, `SpecialMeasures.DeployedZone`). The state recalls the chief after
  seven days in a row graded B or better, and the restrictions end with them; never when they are the last colonist (`SpecialMeasures.OthersRemain`: the recall would start
  vanilla's game-over countdown). Recoverable at every step, and the visible end is a chronicle milestone, a letter, a staff member and a lost project, not the game's end.
- **Flagship**: five days graded A earns it (A days are not counted under measures or with the chief, so not on the night of the lift): grants pay 25% more, and about every eight days the state offers a
  legendary prisoner (a decision letter; a celebrity arrival with six times the premium, `Celebrities.Admit`) whose arc (`RP_Arc_Legend`) brings a
  film crew two days later (a fee and reputation against a prisoner who feeds on the attention). Lost after two days graded below B, or when measures begin.

### The inspector who itemises (`Story/Inspector.cs`, `Story/Calendar.cs`, `Defs/Inspector.xml`, `Keyed/Inspector.xml`; gap analysis 6, W3)
- The state inspection is still announced 20 hours ahead with the deep clean, the envelope or nothing as the choices, and still grades the prison on the report card and pays or fines on the grade. What changed is what the visit is: an
  **inspector** (a visiting `RP_Inspector`, on the overseer's mechanism: `SpecialMeasures.Visit` spawns them at the intake spot, queues a walk, and the staff comp sends them off after `VisitHours`, no wage, no chronicle line) walks a bed in an ordinary wing (not supermax, protective custody, death row, the psychiatric wing or solitary, and none while a riot, a hostage crisis or a lockdown is on), the canteen, the kitchen and the yard and
  **writes down what only the warden can put right**, read from the systems that already know: bodies outside the morgue for an hour (it is full, out of reach or not painted), the danger map's patches no lamp lights (judged with the sky left out, so the same by day and by night, and kept by a cell of the patch, not the zone grid's anchor), the night (the fewest guards on duty in 22-06 by the roster against one for every 20 inmates, at least one),
  the structural gaps of the to-do list (a room not painted, no stove, no showers or toilets: the `PrisonDiagnostics.Item`s that carry a key) and cells graded 0 with an inmate in them who is meant for better (with cells by conduct on, an entitlement above 0: the ladder houses entitlement 0 in grade 0 on purpose; counted as cells, not beds). **Not written**: what the prison's routine clears (hunger, filth)
  and a complaint (the letter dismisses itself within the day, and one held back for the desk's budget has not been seen: the warden cannot be said to have left it).
- **One cause is one finding**: no showers is not written beside the shower room that is not painted (`InspectionReport.OneCause`; the hourly look still reads the raw list, so painting the room away does not clear it). At most five, the most pressing first (a body, a dark patch, the night, a gap in the rooms, a bare cell), and only what the prison already had when the visit was **announced** (`InspectionReport.Notice` keeps the kinds the prison
  gave then; what is made in the 20 hours - a zone unpainted, a guard taken off the roster - and put back after the visit is not read; no notice taken, as in a dev incident, reads everything). Each is due at the re-inspection and is a line of the **to-do band** ("Inspector - ..., 2 days left") that opens the screen it is about.
- The hourly look marks a finding **gone** from the hour it is no longer so (`goneSince`), and open again the hour it returns. The **re-inspection** is five days after the visit: the inspector walks again, each finding that is gone and has been gone for the whole last day (`HoldDays`) is a grant (`GrantPerFixed`, $400) and each other a fine (`FinePerOpen`, $300), two ledger entries and a letter that says each item. A fix at the last minute, or a routine that clears it on the day, is not paid. No new inspection is announced while
  one is to come back; a visit made anyway (a dev incident) settles the pending one first. A visit with nothing to write down has no re-inspection.
- **The bribe has something to hide**: a bribed inspector walks and writes nothing down (no findings, no re-inspection, the letter says so); what they left out is kept (`InspectionState.hidden`) and the exposure letter, when the roll comes up, names it (or says the envelope bought nothing). The leftovers go at the next visit when no exposure is pending.
- Saved on `StorySystem.inspection` (`InspectionState`: the findings (key, cell, id, due tick, goneSince), what a bribe left out, the re-inspection tick, the notice's kinds, the inspector), all with defaults; an old save has an empty book. In the saveload snapshot (`inspector=`).

## The first hour (what a new warden meets)
The first hour decides whether a new player gets far enough to see the deep systems. What is done for it (gap analysis 4, sections 3 and 2):
- **The welcome letter is told by start** (`PrisonGame.IntroText`): a lead, the next steps of the start chosen (one text for the inherited facility, one for the state penitentiary, one for an empty plot), a tail. A prison handed over built is told what it has and where to look; an empty plot is told what
  to build, in the order the first shuttle needs it (pad, holding, cells, canteen and kitchen with its bill, showers and yard, an office and an administrator). The dialog also names the two mod settings that change building (pay on placement, no power grid) with a button to them.
- **A to-do band on the overview** (`PrisonDiagnostics.Items`): the same list the alerts and the autotest read, each line opening the screen where it is fixed (four lines, the rest counted; the pad first, then holding, cells, canteen, kitchen, showers and the yard, as the welcome letter of an empty plot tells; hiring more cooks opens Staff, the rest Rooms; the card and the list are kept per map). The "no beds" alarm needs a cell to run out of
  (`PrisonAlerts.ShuttleWithoutBeds`: not on an empty plot's first morning), and the report card shows a dash, not a failing grade, with no inmates, with a tooltip on each tile saying what drives it.
- **The inherited facility reads as a prison that works** (`Testing/PrisonBuilder.Inherited.cs`): the administrator's office with its desk in reception (seven starting staff: the administrator is added to the six, `ScenPart_PrisonStart.seventhRole`, and the janitor stays, since nobody else cleans and the kitchen is a staff-only room; the new facility has six), a morgue
  beside it, two solitary cells off the holding room (the default sanction is solitary, so the first punishment finds a bed; they are not among the cell beds), a small chapel in the yard east of the gate, armchairs where the staff room had beds
  (staff go home to sleep). The library and chaplaincy projects are unlocked in the scenario (their rooms are painted). Start on a bureaucracy project is off, with the reason, while there is no administrator on the staff or no desk (`BureaucracySystem.StalledReason`).
- **The emergency buttons ask first** (`PrisonHUD.RequestLockdown`, `RequestShakedown`): a shakedown always; a whole-prison lockdown unless a riot, an escape, a hostage crisis, an assault or a gang war under way is running (`PrisonHUD.FightingOn`).
- **The clock and the speed buttons are in the prison panel** (`PrisonHUD.DoClock`, top row; `Patch_GlobalControlsUtility_DoDate_InPanel` / `_DoTimespeedControls_InPanel`): the time of day large (`Prefs.TwelveHourClockMode`), "Day N" and "quadrum, season" small beside it (the full vanilla date and its tip on hover), and vanilla's `TimeControls.DoTimeControlsGUI` in the rect to its right, so the buttons, their tips, states and the pause and speed keys (that call reads them too) are vanilla's. `PrisonHUD.ShowsClock(map)` is the one test (prison game, the home map on screen, not the world map, not a screenshot frame) for drawing it and for the prefixes that skip vanilla's date and speed buttons without touching `curBaseY` (the weather and the rest move down); elsewhere vanilla's stay. The time is a table of strings by the minute, filled as they come, the date rebuilt when the day turns. The panel's height (`PrisonHUD.Height`, followed by the messages) includes the row only where it is shown.
- **Deployment greys out** armed response (and says what it needs) until the armed security project is done. Programs are on the Policy screen (the alert says where).
- **The prison scenarios and the Board are not in vanilla's lists** (`<showInUI>false</showInUI>` on each scenario; "New prison" starts them by defName), so a new colony cannot pick them. The Board is listed (the in-game storyteller page draws its difficulty buttons only for a listed storyteller, and a prison switched to another storyteller must be able to come back), and
  hidden from a new colony's list and preselection only while the main menu is the state (`UI/StorytellerList.cs`).
- **Difficulty** (`Core/PrisonDifficulty.cs`): no prison system read the game's difficulty (vanilla's factors are about raids, crops and trade), so each of vanilla's six levels is mapped onto five prison levers, "strive to survive" (the dialog's default, and every test's) being one of
  everything: how often incidents and story beats come (x0.6 peaceful ... x1.3 losing is fun; `StorytellerComp_PrisonEvents` for the Board's incidents and `StorytellerComp_PrisonLife` for the beats), the grade below which the state counts a day as bad (40 ... 65, a C is 55;
  `SpecialMeasures.BadGrade`; each day of the history keeps the line it was graded under, `PrisonGame.gradeRules`, a save from before has the neutral one; the letters and the pane say it in points, `SpecialMeasures.BadGradeText`), the week's limit of deaths and escapes (x1.5 ... x0.7; `SpecialMeasures.WeeklyLimit`) and the starting funds (x1.6 ... x0.7, set by the scenario's start). The new prison dialog's difficulty pane prints this table and a line of what the level feels like
  (`RP_Difficulty_<defName>`) instead of vanilla's raid text, and vanilla's own mood setting for the level (-10 ... +10 on everybody's mood, staff and inmates: `Need_Mood` applies it to prisoners too). The penitentiary starts from 60000 times the level's funds
  factor and the dialog shows that. The state's price for land is a fifth lever (x0.6 ... x1.5, see the grounds). A custom or modded difficulty is the neutral level. An empty prison has no grade: nothing goes into the history, the reputation, the grants' streaks or the state's counters (`DailySettlement.NoteGrade`).
- **The learning helper teaches the deep systems** (`UI/Concepts.cs`, `Defs/Concepts.xml`): fourteen `ConceptDef` notes (the pad, holding, the kitchen and its bill, bureaucracy, the hearing room, a trial, a complaint, a ring, a debt, turf, a want, special measures,
  the flagship, the morgue), each shown in vanilla's learning readout the first time its system matters (a room painted, a case opened, the first ring link found...) through `LessonAutoActivator.TeachOpportunity(.., Important)` and marked learned when the player does what it is about or
  clicks "Mark learned"; the main buttons they are about are highlighted. Vanilla's own notes (drafting, caravans...) are held back in a prison game and the helper does not pick a note by itself; it follows the player's own setting (adaptive training), and learned notes stay learned across games as vanilla's do.

### Fewer letters, one for each decision moment
- A killing: the case's letter is the decision (`RP_Decision_TrialCase`): moving the inmates who came forward to protective custody and asking a known informant to testify are choices in it (it leaves things as they are when it times out). A staff victim's charge is in the staff death's letter.
  The evening before the court sits (18:00) a message says so, and off-site the day it sits a letter names who will speak, as far as the warden knows. The case's "uncertain" band is split into leaning to an acquittal and to a conviction.
- A cargo run says it once (on landing: no message coming down, none lifting off with nobody aboard); a stowaway's escape has one chronicle line, the stowaway's.
- **A budget of decisions** (`Story/StoryLetters.cs`): `3 + inmates / 20` decision letters a day (more at a difficulty where events come more often). Past it a decision letter waits until midnight and is then delivered (its answer time starting then), oldest first, within that day's budget, which
  starts again before the day's own systems write theirs and delivers the backlog after the loan's and special measures' letters. A letter that warns of something with a deadline of its own is never held back, whatever its answer time: urgency is the decision's `urgent` flag (`PrisonDecisionDef`: inspection, staff death, the
  case, the debt crisis, special measures extended, an overdose, protective custody, an inmates' demand, hazard pay, a riot's negotiation, a fire, a staff strike) or its arc's (`StoryArcDef.urgent`: a feud, a gang war, a leadership challenge, whose event runs on its own clock), or an answer time under twelve hours; urgent letters do not use up the day's budget.
  A held letter whose arc has ended is dropped, one held more than three days is answered as an unanswered one is; one that waits still counts as open for the systems that look for an open letter (`StoryLetters.Open`; the Intel screen says "held back, reaches your desk at midnight"). In an autotest run the budget is off unless a check turns it on (`StoryLetters.TestBudget`).
- The Intel screen scrolls, has a tooltip on every row, a "next" column (the court's day, the answer time, the report due), a hint on every empty list of what starts such an entry, and a "Letter" button that reopens a complaint's or a ring's letter while it waits. The inmate list has a Want column
  (a secret goal, or one that would name a gang nobody has found through a grudge every member got at once, never shows, sorts or filters: `Wants.Shown`) and a "slipping" filter, and the weekly report names the two goals nearest their deadline.

### Standing orders and the desk (`Story/StandingOrders.cs`, `UI/DeskBoard.cs`, `UI/OverviewTab.cs`, `Keyed/StandingOrders.xml`; gap analysis 7, W3)
The letter was the only interface, and the budget of decisions made the routine ones push the ones that matter to the next day.
- **Which decisions.** `PrisonDecisionDef.standingOrderAllowed` (default false) marks a routine decision; `standingOrderOptions` lists the answers that may be given for good (empty: any). Marked: the funeral (quiet burial or funeral), the adjudication sheet (the policy's sanction for all only, not "open the sheet"), the custody offer (sign or decline), the mentorship, the talent show, the family day. Not the export contract (round 2): its two answers are always the same, but each offer's terms are its own (a target about 1.3 times the current rate, a penalty, a reputation cost, a buyer), so a standing "sign" would take any size of contract. Never: an `urgent` decision (`StandingOrders.Allowed` refuses one whatever the XML says), an arc's letter, or one whose answers depend on the case (the trial, the debts, the green light, kin, an arc's reckoning, parole, complaints...). The holiday meal and the big match are routine too but come with 10 and 6 hours to answer, under the 12 that make a letter urgent, so they are not marked. What an order gives up, by design: a standing mentorship answer does not see each pair's gang risk, and a standing "hold a funeral" does not see the letter's warning of a rival gang's scuffle (`RP_FuneralRivalNote`): the warden who wants to weigh those lifts the order.
- **Giving one.** A marked letter shows "Do this from now on" (`DiaOption_StandingOrder`, a checkbox drawn by a prefix on `DiaOption.OptOnGUI`; a click does not close the letter) between its answers and "postpone". Answering by hand with it ticked records the answer's **key** (`PrisonGame.standingOrders`: def name to option key; `AcceptButton`/`RejectLetter` for a plain accept/decline letter), so a list of answers that changes keeps the order's meaning. Only an answer by hand records one (a letter that times out or lapses never does), so a decision is always answered by hand at least once first; an answer the def does not allow for good gives a message and no order.
- **Answering.** The letter stack's prefix (`Patch_LetterStack_ReceiveLetter_Budget`) asks `StandingOrders.TryAnswer` before the budget (`StoryLetters.Defer`), and the midnight delivery first gives every held letter of a decision with an order to it, whatever room the new day has (a held letter had twelve hours or more to answer, so it is not urgent): a letter of a decision with an order is answered at once (`PrisonDecisions.Choose`/`Accept`/`Decline`, the chronicle line saying "by standing order", a line in the letter's text), never reaches the stack, uses none of the day's budget, is put in the archive (History) and told as a message whose "Review" link (fading with the message) (`StandingOrders.DrawReview`, drawn beside the live message by the prison panel's message patch) opens it read-only (off the stack only "close" is offered). The desk keeps the last six answered for review (`PrisonGame.standingAnswered`, not saved). A letter sent with a delay is weighed, by the orders and the budget alike, only when the queue delivers it (it was counted against the budget when queued as well before round 2). A worker that throws while an order answers is caught (logged; the letter archived as answered): the answer runs inside the sender's `ReceiveLetter`, which an exception would have stopped half-way. At midnight the orders are weighed before the letters that waited too long lapse (an order given on a letter's last day answers it), and not as a delivery: a decision letter a worker writes then asks for its place in the budget.
- **Falling back.** The order cannot answer a letter that is urgent (`StoryLetters.Urgent`: the 12-hour rule), whose answer is not among its options, or is disabled for its case (`Worker.Disabled`: no money for the show, no place for the funeral, the custody offer gone): the letter goes on as usual (the budget, the desk) with a line saying the order could not apply and why. Weighed once (`standingTried`, saved since round 2): a held letter does not get a second line at midnight, nor after a reload. The line is added raw (resolved when the letter is shown): resolved first, its money came out in two colour tags.
- **Policy.** The Policy tab's right column has two buttons at its head, Punishments and Standing orders (N): the latter lists the orders, decision and answer, each with "Lift" (`StandingOrders.Lift`). (The column was full with the punishments, hence a switch rather than a section under them.) The punishments scroll (round 2): seventeen rows and the help did not fit at 1280 x 720 once the switch took its 32 px, and the last misconducts (drug use, a refused test, a false complaint) could not be seen or set.
- **The desk** (`DeskBoard.Desk`, the overview's lower band: "Show the desk (N)" beside "Show wings"/"Show needs and complaints"; the panel's letters count opens it and its tooltip carries the head line): the day's budget used and left (a bar), the open letters soonest deadline first (hours left, a red mark when urgent, a click opens one), the held letters (the answer time they will get, the hours before they lapse unanswered - to the midnight that lapses them, `StoryLetters.LapseTick`: the first of the prison's midnights after the letter has waited `MaxHeldDays`, not the hour it was held three days on, which read up to 24 hours early; a click reads one read-only), and under them the letters standing orders answered lately with their review links. Worked out with the overview's card (every 120 frames), never per tick.
- **Saved:** `PrisonGame.standingOrders` (`Scribe_Collections`, values; empty when absent; an order for a decision that is gone or no longer allows one, or for an answer the decision no longer offers or takes for good, is dropped at load: `StandingOrders.Keeps`). The letter's `standingTried` (default false). The saveload plan seeds two orders (`standing=` in the snapshot) and a held letter weighed once (the held letters' part of the snapshot shows `standingTried`), and puts the game's back after the comparison.
- **Checks:** `Testing/StandingOrderChecks.cs` (`EvStandingOrders`, last in the events list): which defs allow one, the box and an order only from an answer by hand (not unticked, not a lapse, not the sheet's "open"), a later letter answered at once on an empty and a full day (no budget, archived, the review link, read-only, the chronicle), the key looked up by name, a missing key, lifting, a decision with no orders ignoring the book, a disabled answer and an urgent letter falling back with one line (also after waiting for midnight), an order given while a letter waited answering it at a full midnight (the letter with no order waits on), and the desk's rows (hours left, order, answer time, lapse, the budget; the clock moved two days on and back). Round 2: the lapse is the third midnight for a letter held a day ago, and for a letter held at any hour a midnight that the pass finds past its days (the one before not); a marked decision made urgent allows none; the book's filter; an order given on a held letter's last day answers it before it lapses; a worker that throws (the check's own worker swapped in) leaves the sender going and the letter archived; a letter a worker writes at a full midnight is held; a delayed letter is weighed once, on delivery; no check leaves an order in the book. Windowed tour: the Policy tab's punishments at the top and the foot of their scroll view. Windowed tour: `RIMPRISON_TOUR=standing` (the letter's box, the Policy list, an answered letter's message and review, the desk).
- **Where the description and the code disagreed:** the analysis's saved state is option *index*; the decision (and this code) keeps the option *key*. The open-letter count shows on the prison panel, not the Overview: the desk is on the Overview, reached from the panel's count. The budget's line numbers had moved (`Budget` at 35, `Defer` 56-75, `MaxHeldDays` 24) and the budget is `3 + inmates/20` times the difficulty's event factor (never less than 1).

### The handover - the prison you were given has a past (`Story/Handover.cs`, `Testing/PrisonBuilder.cs`, `Keyed/Handover.xml`, `Testing/HandoverChecks.cs`)
Today the penitentiary opened clean (forty inmates with no history and every hidden system at zero), so the rings, debts and tunnels took days to form. A prison that is **handed over built** now starts with secrets in the systems that already hide them, and a note from the old warden about some of them. Nothing is a new system: every seed is a field that exists.
- **The choice.** The New Prison dialog has "Inherit the old warden's problems" (`NewPrisonOptions.inheritProblems`, on by default, shown for the penitentiary and the inherited facility, not for an empty plot). `PrisonGameStarter.Setup` puts it in `Handover.Pending`; `ScenPart_PrisonStart.PostGameStart` takes it once (`Handover.Take`, and a `Game` constructor patch clears a stale one) and hands it to `PrisonBuilder.InheritProblems`, and `BuildStarter` sows once the inmates are admitted. **A start that does not ask is clean:**
  `NewPrisonOptions.inheritProblems` is false, so every test plan starts as it always did unless it asks (the `handover` plan, or the `uitour`/`shots` plans with `RIMPRISON_TOUR=handover`: `AutoTest.HandoverWanted`), and every other plan checks it (`CheckHandoverStartsClean`).
- **What is sown** (`Handover.Sow`, once, the penitentiary's all of it): a **gang** that holds the yard (the one most of the minimum and medium inmates already belong to, made up to about 60% of them (a gang is kept at 40% of those seen on its patch, and its dealer and courier are in the laundry for most of a day) with inmates who had no gang, a leader among them if the gang has none anywhere (it may already have one in another wing), its mark on every other recruit so that a frisk gives it away, a claim on the yard patch as old as the quadrum),
  its **dealer** (the reputation, a little cash, two doses in the pockets) and the dealer's **courier**, both on the laundry crew, and a **ring** round the dealer (`RingSystem.Form`: the dealer, the courier, and the **officer on the payroll** - a guard who is `corrupt` with the dealer as `patron`, which `RingSystem.Sync` reads as the ring's officer link); **two overdue debts** (drugs to the dealer, a card debt between two others, in one wing,
  the debtors out of cash: `DebtSystem.Open` with a negative term, so the first daily pass escalates them and does not drop them); a **feud** (two of one wing, in cells of their own, as enemies: the medium wing's in the penitentiary, whose minimum wing is one dormitory, and not gang members where there is a choice); a **tunnel** a third dug (`CompInmate.tunnelProgress`) under a maximum cell (a lifer; with none, an escape artist - which also multiplies their escape misconduct, and is logged - with the tool in the cell's stash); a **guard with a suspicion file** (the officer, at 28: under the 50 that opens a case);
  a **complaint** filed the day before against another guard (`ComplaintStage.Filed`: on the desk in six hours); and the **chronicle** holding the last quadrum's milestones in the old warden's words (7 lines, 6 to 15 days before the game). The **inherited facility** gets a debt, a feud, the suspicion file, a complaint and four old lines - and the six inmates (three minimum, three medium) the old warden left in its cells (it opens with none, and a debt and a feud need people; their intake grant is not paid).
- **Hidden as the systems hide them.** A debt is not `known`, a gang's members are not `gangKnown` (and so its turf is not on the overlay, the rooms line or the Intel gangs rows), the dealer's reputation is hidden, no link of the ring is known (so it is not on the Intel screen), the tunnel is not `tunnelKnown` and no search is pending, the complaint is still `Filed` (no letter, no line on a file). Nothing is counted as smuggled (`ContrabandUtility.Give(smuggled: false)`). Two things the code shows by design are shown: a guard's suspicion over 0 is a row on the Intel staff list, and
  an enmity (a vanilla relation) is an "enemies" line on the two inmates' files. The feud is therefore not an arc (an arc is an Intel row with a letter, and starting one needs a fight between two who are not yet enemies); the enmity simmers as opinion and tension until they meet.
- **The note** (`Handover.NoteText`, `PrisonGame.IntroText(.., note)`): the welcome letter's body is the old warden's note, then a paragraph with the lead's practical part (what the shuttle brings and pays), then the facility's steps and the tail as before. Three hints, **two true and one wrong**, naming only what the old warden could have known (a suspicion, a wing, a crew; never a ring, a tunnel, a debt or a name that is part of one):
  a file open on the guard (the officer on the payroll), a wing that is too quiet at night (the maximum wing, where the tunnel is; the inherited facility's own is two men in the minimum wing who do not belong in a room together), and "watch the kitchen crew" - where the dealer and the courier work the laundry. Where a seed was not sown (no maximum inmate in a cell) its hint is left out.
  Two product facts the hints carry: in the inherited facility there is no dealer, so "the file" is on a clean guard (a 28-point file that leads nowhere: the feud's hint is then the one true lead of three), and the "wrong" kitchen hint is partly true by design (the kitchen has tool theft and food stealing of its own).
- **Seeds go into quiet people only.** A seed is not put in an inmate the warden already knows something about (flagged, a cell search due, a debt or a complaint on their file): a prison with a history has such people, and a start has none.
- **The chronicle before the game.** The old entries have **negative ticks** (`-days * TicksPerDay + hours`, 6 to 15 days back: past the five days `PressDecision.Topic` and `RecentMilestones` read), no pawns, and sit first in the list. `ChronicleEntry.Day` now counts down (floor, not toward zero) and `Hour` reads right; the Chronicle tab has one heading for everything before the game ("Before you", the day label of each as "-9d") and `Story.When` says "9 days before day 1, 14h". The overview's latest list shows them after anything newer.
- **Saved:** `PrisonGame.handover` (a bool: the prison was handed over with its past; the note is sent only by the start that sowed it). Everything else is an existing field. Old saves load with it false. The saveload plan seeds the flag, two old entries, a tunnel, an enmity and a file off every default (`handover=` in the snapshot: the flag, the old entries, the tunnels, the enmities and the staff's files).
- **Where the code and the description disagreed:** the inherited facility has no inmates at the start (hence the six); a feud arc would be on the Intel screen and a suspicion file is by design (so the check's "none shows" is for the other seeds, and these two are judged as what the code shows).
- **Checks:** `Testing/HandoverChecks.cs`. The `handover` plan (penitentiary, 2 days) judges the start (every seed there; nothing on an Intel row, file or overlay bar the two; the letter is the note; the chronicle's order and clock; the press does not read the old lines) and the end (at the start the first daily pass is run once on the seeded debts and put back: each is still in the book, threatened; at the end a cell search of the seeded cell finds the tunnel, and the debts' state and whether the yard claim held are logged, not judged). The seeds' effects on the counters are judged against the counts just before the sowing (the arrivals' own contraband is the game's), and the leak scan reads the note, not the welcome letter's steps and tail (they name the Intel screen's feuds).
  `EvHandover` (events plan, self-contained) covers the options and the dialog's default, the letter with and without a note, the clock of a day before the game, the note on made-up seeds and a sowing on the live prison (the small one: given two clean-sheet guards, judged on what it adds to the screens, put back). Windowed tour: `RIMPRISON_TOUR=handover` (`tools/uitour.sh`, or `tools/showcase-shots.sh penitentiary` for the note as the first picture).

## Stripped vanilla
World map tab, caravans, raids, traders, quests, ideology start, research (replaced by Bureaucracy), wild animal
spawns. World is generated tiny and the tile is picked automatically from a "New prison" flow. The learning helper stays, but teaches only the prison's own notes.

## Architecture & performance principles
- **Def-driven, not hard-coded**: zones, regime activities, security levels, crimes, reputations, misconduct acts,
  programs, staff roles, fixtures and events are Defs with worker classes, so adding content is XML + one small class.
  C# switch statements over def names are not allowed; behavior lives on the def's worker.
- **One owner per state**: game-wide state in `PrisonGame` (GameComponent), map state in `PrisonMap`
  (MapComponent: zone grid, fixtures index, door index), per-pawn state in `CompInmate`/`CompStaff`. UI only reads.
- **Ticking**: nothing per-tick per pawn. Inmate logic runs on `CompTickInterval` hash intervals (250/2500 ticks);
  map-wide passes (regime changes, intake, daily settlement) run on hour/day boundaries only. Think-tree job givers
  are cheap: they read cached indexes (fixtures by zone, zone cell lists, per-room zone) instead of scanning things.
- **Caches with explicit invalidation**: zone grid changes / door mode changes / regime hour changes bump a version
  and clear reachability once, not per query. Fixture lists are kept by spawn/despawn hooks, never rebuilt by scans.
- **Allocation-free hot paths**: reuse lists, no LINQ in think nodes, job givers or tick code.
- **Scanner work givers offer short lists, not every inmate** (perf pass): vanilla's work scan checks that the pawn can *reach* everything a giver offers before it asks `HasJobOnThing`, so a giver whose
  `PotentialWorkThingsGlobal` is all the prisoners costs a reachability query per inmate per scan, to be told no. Ours offer `InmateCandidates.Shared` lists (the giver's own guard-independent tests, built once a
  tick when asked; an empty list makes `ShouldSkip` true), and a giver's `HasJobOnThing` starts with the same `Candidate` test, so list and giver cannot drift apart. Do the cheap tests before `ShouldTakeCareOfPrisoner`
  or `CanReserveAndReach` in any giver that does not use a list. Vanilla's warden givers (chat, convert, interrogate, release, execution, feed, take to bed) and doctor givers (tend, feed patients, rescue) get the same: Harmony postfixes on
  their `PotentialWorkThingsGlobal` (`Core/VanillaGiverLists.cs`) swap in lists of the inmates that pass the giver's own tests that do not depend on who is asking, exactly (a prisoner left out is one the giver answers "no job" for, for every guard: an inmate in
  their own cell, in or at their bed, is not a candidate for the bed giver, whose bed search was most of what the staff scan cost). The doctors' lists keep every colonist and animal of vanilla's list and cut down only the inmates. Forced orders do not go through the lists.
- **The UI never touches the game's random numbers or its state**: `IntakeSystem.FreePrisonerBeds` shuffles (the order picks who gets which bed), so screens, gizmos and event conditions count with `FreeBedCount`, which is read only (it used to let go of a stale
  home bed, `PunishmentUtility.SyncHomeBed`, for every prisoner per call; that happens in each inmate's hourly update now). The staff list (`StaffCommute.AllStaff`, 36 callers) is built once a tick (and again when the colonists, the staff at home or anyone's role change), and a list given out
  is never changed afterwards. Nothing but the landing and the hourly pass sweeps the map for a shuttle site; readers use the cache.
- **The 250-tick pass runs on the beat only**: a dirty tick (a door mode, a lockdown, a zone painted) redoes the door verdicts and nothing else; a work-pass flip clears that pawn's reachability only
  (`Notify_WorkPassChanged(pawn)`), never the map's. Anything that must run once per 250 ticks (the K9 sniff rolls, the utilities) may count on that.
- **Regime changes are spread**: the hour's job interrupts (and a class starting) go through `PrisonMap.QueueRegimeInterrupt` and are drained `InterruptsPerTick` a tick, each only if the inmate is still on the
  job they had (compared by the job's number, `Job.loadID`: jobs are pooled and a reused object is not the job they had); an emergency (lockdown) still interrupts at once. The queue is not saved: the first pass of a session sends every idle inmate to look for what the
  regime has for them (`PrisonMap.RegimeSweep`). A bed built or taken away is listed at the next tick, not the next beat (`Notify_BedsChanged`).
- **Caches say what they are keyed on**: the canteen's meals by the thing lister's state hash and the zone version (exact, never stale), the cell doors' beds per room until the next `RefreshBeds`
  (checked in test runs against the live room: 0 of 36,000 verdicts differed), "is any instigator in the prison" 250 ticks, the coverage overlay until a guard moved 3 cells (at most every 250, at least every
  1000 ticks). A cache that can be stale within a tick has an `Invalidate`/`Reset` for tests. **Do not give inmates' searches a cooldown to save time** (tried for the free-time joy and commissary searches and
  taken out again: it saved about 0.1% of a tick, and how often an inmate looks for something to do is gameplay, not a cost to trade).
- Target: 300 inmates + 60 staff at 3x speed without our code showing in the profiler top 10.

## Facilities (the ready-made prisons)
"Open a new prison" offers three: the **inherited facility**, the **state penitentiary** and an empty **new facility**. The first two are built
finished (no blueprints) by `Testing/PrisonBuilder*.cs` from the scenario (`ScenPart_PrisonStart.starterFacility` + `facility`), which is also the
reference layout of the autotest. One partial class: `PrisonBuilder.cs` (size, footprint, the finishing pass, the self-check that logs sealed
rooms, Auto doors shut to inmates and beds inmates cannot reach), `PrisonBuilder.Kit.cs` (rooms with walls, floor and roof, doors, strict
furniture placement that records an error rather than overwrite a building, painting, the room table `Room(...)`, and the layout preview),
`PrisonBuilder.Inherited.cs` and `PrisonBuilder.Penitentiary.cs` (the layouts). Coordinates in a layout are local (x east, z north, the gate in the
south wall); `PrisonBuilder.Footprint` is the walled compound plus what lies outside its gate (`SouthMargin`), the test checks use it.

**The inherited facility** (`Cells` = 8, the starting size class): a wall round it with a gatehouse, a ring of buildings round a walled yard. Front:
reception and the holding room (west of the gate), the staff room (its door from the gatehouse and one to the yard), visitation (its own door
from outside). North: the cell block (cells on both sides of a corridor, south row minimum, north row medium), the hall with the guard station,
the canteen at the corridor's end and the kitchen behind it. West of the yard the common room, showers and library; east the classroom and
the workshop. A block takes eight cells; more cells (events 12, big 24, huge 48) mean more blocks with a hall between every two (from 16
cells the halls serve meals and have showers, so no bed is far from a counter), and from 24 a clerk's mailroom opens off the common room.
`PrisonBuilder.CanteenCell/WorkshopCell/CorridorCell` are the anchors the hostage checks use (a canteen aisle, the workshop floor, the room the
canteen's side door opens on).

**The state penitentiary** (120 x 98 inside the wall, 24 rows of road, landing pad and deliveries outside; needs a 200 plot): a wall with a tower
in each corner and a sterile strip; the gatehouse (sally port) at the foot of the entry court; reception and intake (holding hall and two cells,
search room, property room, issue room) and the staff wing (staff room, armoury, mailroom, offices, the parole hearing room) west of the court,
visitation (lobby and hall) and the infirmary and a classroom east of it; a central yard (weights pad, a sports court with hoops, horseshoes,
chess and cards, picnic tables, a garden, a guard post, payphones, a telescope) between the west block (kitchen, freezer and dry store, canteen,
showers, laundry, library) and the east block (canteen, kitchen, showers, workshop, classroom, chapel), each with a corridor of its own; the cross
corridor; and six housing wings round the control hub (minimum dormitory with a day area; medium; protective custody; maximum; segregation, whose far
half is the supermax unit (two cells a side, a shower room and a yard of its own behind a staff-only security door across the corridor); death row with its execution chamber), the hub holding the control room (camera monitors), a medical ward (the injured are carried to the nearest medical bed, and the infirmary is far from the wings) with the psych wing's two padded cells at its north end, records and the command suite.
Staff for every role are hired (without the hiring fee) and the guards are posted (their patrol zones); about forty inmates are admitted into
their wings and start in their cells; every bureaucracy project is done. The 9 x 7 landing area for the shuttle is `PrisonBuilder.ShuttlePadLocal`
(`ShuttlePadRect(map)`): paved and marked, ready for a pad building (the mod has none yet).
The layout is a table of rooms (`Room(name, rect, floor, zone).Door(...).With(furnish)`) built walls first, then doors, zones and furniture;
A quick look without playing: `tools/autotest.sh 1 -rimprison-plan=layout [-rimprison-facility=penitentiary]` writes the finished layout to the log and
`tools/layout-png.py <log> <png>` draws it. `tools/showcase-shots.sh [penitentiary|inherited]` takes the pictures of the prison itself
(windowed): overview, cell block, canteen at lunch, yard, reception, landing area, infirmary, chapel, library, the night lockdown.

## Testing
`-rimprison-test=<plan>`: starts a quick game with the Rim Prison scenario, builds a reference prison from code
(also the "inherited facility" option for players; plan `showcase`: the state penitentiary, with its own checks, see `Testing/ShowcaseChecks.cs`), runs N days at max speed, logs hourly metrics
(`[RimPrison:metrics]`) and a final report, then quits. Gameplay changes are checked against those numbers.
The test harness's own Harmony patches (the per-frame loop, the log and error taps, the headless autosave switch: classes marked `[TestOnly]`) are applied when a test is armed (`TestOnlyAttribute.InstallTestPatches`), not with the patches every game gets; `Testing/` is
still built into the DLL (the starter facility's builder lives there too). Performance: `Testing/PerfProbe.cs` times our hourly/daily/interval passes, work givers and think nodes and the vanilla calls under them (Harmony prefix/finalizer on ~650 methods, test runs
only, and only with `RIMPRISON_PROBE=1`: the timers change how a run plays out (a third to a half of the huge plan's food checks fail with a busy machine and probes on), so the plans run without them by default; test code and test patches are left out) and logs `[RimPrison:perf]` lines (inclusive ms, calls, per call; callers of the costliest vanilla searches) at the end of a run; the `mega` plan (96 cells, 96 inmates, staff in proportion;
its verdict is "no errors", it is a scale probe) shows what grows faster than the prison. At 48 inmates our code was about 4.5% of a tick (our own roots 3%, our Harmony patches 1.5%; 3.2% after the perf pass; 4.8% -> 3.3% at 96) and vanilla's pawn ticking
(needs and situational thoughts, job and path ticks) about 70%; compare `[RimPrison:perf]` before and after rather than TPS alone (the probes cost a tenth, and a second game on the machine moves vanilla's own numbers by 5-10%).

Flakes (what each is, and what the harness does about it). A failure that is on this list is read off its own text; one that is not is a real one.
- Fixed in the check (cause found): court "a witness who was already badly hurt was beaten again" (a forced-down witness can still be "fit"; the check hurts them until they are not), library reading (a religious
  real inmate is sent to pray by the lock-up activity, never reads; the readers are made not religious for the check), stowaway "with nobody on the pad" (boarding closes as the stay runs out and calls the stowaway off
  with it, the check's two 900-tick runs outlast a visit that landed early: the stay is stretched for the check), the mail clerk on the huge plan (the mailroom job is held while the inmate's tension is 35 or
  less, that prison averages 40, a clerk who breaks or runs past it is not replaced: only a clerk on shift with the post waiting, three hours or more, who sorts nothing fails the run; 658ed6a fails 2 runs in 6 on it too), the escort check (the escort job is casualInterruptible, so a staff break on the hour takes the guard off mid-walk: not tested that run, logged; the product's gap).
- Quarantined (`AutoTest.KnownFlakes`; a run with only these passes with `quarantined=name`, the check's text in a `QUARANTINED FLAKE` line): `hostage-ringleader-nearest-staff`, `parole-administrator-at-the-desk`
  (neither was seen in about 35 events runs of the gap analysis 4 work, so no cause could be read off a log; both have diagnostics in their text).
- Seen and not explained (not quarantined, diagnostics in the text): the programs class "closed by real ticks" check (the apprentice leaves for `Clean` or a `Goto` having heard about half of the class; the hour did not turn in the window, so that is not it), "shuttle release counters: the reoffence rolled", "no shuttle called" in the unloading checks.

## Milestones
M1 foundation: scenario/start flow, zones, door policy, regime + inmate AI, needs (hygiene/bladder), economy +
intake + HUD, autotest. M2 security & misconduct: tension, fights, guards, contraband, solitary, riots, escapes.
M3 staff & labor & programs & visitation. M4 events, grading, reputation, parole. M5 polish/UI/balance, then
innovate (gangs, informants, death row, corruption, legal system).

## Review amendments (Fable + Astra, 2026-10-16)
- `PawnCanOpen` runs on pathfinding worker threads (`PathGridDoorsBlockedJob`): the postfix only reads fields
  precomputed on the main thread (door verdict on `CompPrisonDoor`, active-inmate flag on `CompInmate`).
- Doors held open (`FreePassage`) let inmates through: tailgating/escape opportunity, by design.
- `SocialProperness.IsSociallyProper` is patched: inmates may use things in their room or in inmate zones. This is
  what makes canteens and yards work.
- Vanilla prison breaks off for managed inmates (`InitiatePrisonBreakMtbDays` → -1, jailbreak sparks off); riots and
  escapes come from our misconduct system with our own LordJobs.
- Inmate subtree always returns a job (wander fallback), so vanilla's prisoner branch below never runs.
- Beds are assigned after `SetGuestStatus` (status change unclaims beds). Joy: clear `colonistsOnly` and
  `neverOnPrisoner`, add `colonistAndPrisonersOnly`.
- Gameplay priorities raised: guard deployment/sectors, intel layer (you don't know who has the shiv), tunnels as
  hidden per-cell state, incident histories (causal chains in letters), grudges/protective relationships,
  informants. Deferred: laundry, staff strikes, bureaucracy until M5 (the exercise need came later, with the library).
- Tests assert behavior, not just metrics: no unauthorized exits, no starvation, inmates in the right zone per slot,
  job-failure counts, save/reload.

## Status vs Prison Architect (updated 2026-10-18)
| Area | PA | Rim Prison |
|---|---|---|
| Build & rooms | rooms, doors, fences | vanilla building + painted zones (canteen, yard, showers, workshop, classroom, visitation, kitchen, staff, holding, solitary, deliveries, exports, wings); door modes; security doors |
| Regime | hourly per category | hourly per security level (min/med/max/supermax/protective), lockdown, shakedown |
| Intake & payments | daily bus, grants per category | daily state shuttle (on a pad, or on clear ground) by policy and free wing beds, escorted to holding, intake grant + daily payment, reputation multiplier, funding events, transfers in/out |
| Needs | food, sleep, hygiene, bladder, family, recreation, freedom, comfort, ... | vanilla food/rest/joy/comfort/beauty/outdoors + hygiene, bladder, family, clean uniform, faith |
