| Staff | guards, cooks, workmen, janitors, doctors, psychologist, teachers, bureaucracy | guards (patrol, deployment, frisk, cell search, restrain), armed guards, dog handlers, cooks, workmen, janitors, doctors, gardeners, counsellors, psychologist (assessment, therapy), chaplain, administrators, riot police callout; staff eat off-site; corruption; morale (a demoralised guard misses things in searches and restraints, by their own break thresholds); training courses (paid, a day away, +2 in the job skill up to 16); burnout leave, debts, retirement, officer of the month |
| Security | guards, armed guards, dogs, CCTV, metal detectors, lockdown | guards, armed guards (response policy: hold fire / escapes / escapes + violence), K9 handlers (dog sniffs drugs and digging, bite-and-hold), guard towers (manned watch over walls), CCTV, metal detectors, lockdown/shakedown, riot police |
| Misconduct | fights, riots, escapes, tunnels, contraband, drugs | tension model with causes; insults, fights, vandalism, assaulting staff, escape attempts, riots, tunnels, contraband (visitors, workshop, garden, over the wall, corrupt staff), drug use, shivs, dealing, extortion; unrest events (hunger strikes, staff strike threats, blackouts); inmates' demands put by a named spokesperson (promise, break, refuse; riot deals made to the ringleader); good behaviour remission policy (clean days on the record take days off) |
| Intel | informants, contraband/danger maps | smuggling rings (a dealer's supply chain found link by link, then busted, waited on or turned), a map of where contraband was found, hidden reputations/gangs, informants (exposure risk), psychologist assessments, tension overlay, incident causes in letters, inmate prison file tab, needs report with top complaints |
| Reform | programs, parole, reoffending | 9 programs (induction, foundation + general education, rehab, anger mgmt, spiritual guidance, kitchen safety, a two-part tailoring course that certifies a trade), parole hearings, reoffending, report card; personalities with behaviour (dealer, extortionist, jailhouse lawyer) |
| Economy | grants, exports, shop | ledger, supplies by the state shuttle (population-scaled restocking; the daily shuttle or a cargo run between), pay-on-placement construction, exports, commissary, inmate wages and jobs (workshop, laundry, garden, cleaning), grants tree, utilities bill, winter jackets; export contracts for license plates (sized to recent sales, bonus or penalty over five export runs); debt crisis with a state loan repaid from payments; kitchen menu policy |
| Death row | appeals, execution chamber | death row level (wing-only, cell meals, all-day lockup), appeals that may commute to life (likelier for the hidden innocent), execution date, guards restrain and carry to the execution table, overdue fines, wrongful-execution lawsuits, prison-wide tension |
| Supermax | supermax wing | supermax level (wing-only, single cells, cell meals, locked in every hour, out only with a guard, whose pass opens the doors of the walk's path and no others, for a shower and an hour alone in a yard of its own, in several hours a day; fetched if left outside; no parole), sent there for killing staff, a murder conviction, a hostage crisis or the warden's order (paid at the maximum rate); a long stay lowers mood and raises the break threshold; a step-down letter after ten clean days |
| Psych ward | insane inmates, padded cells, medication | psychiatric care level (wing-only, padded beds only, no work hour; the psychologist moves an inmate there after two breaks in ten days or a fitting trait at the first assessment), padded cells (a break there harms neither the inmate nor the things in it), the doctor's medication rounds with an antipsychotic whose cover fades when a round is missed, and then a break comes; the project after psychology unlocks it |
| Bureaucracy | warden office research tree | administrators at office desks work through paid projects (foreman, commissary, visitation, reform, chaplaincy, psychology, security chief, tasers, riot gear, K9, max security, supermax, armed security, death row) that unlock buildings, zones, roles, inmate levels and features; grants appear once their requirements are unlocked |
| Laundry & faith | laundry, clothing need, chapel, spiritual need | clean-uniform need (swap at shower time from the stock), laundry job on washing machines, outsourced laundry bill at midnight for the shortfall; religious inmates (35%) with a faith need, prayer in the chapel in free time, chaplain services (2x, calming) |
| Hazards | fires, firemen, outbreaks | kitchen fires (staff fight them; a paid fire brigade puts out every fire), food poisoning from the kitchen (scrub, stand the crew down, or risk a second wave), flu outbreaks, medical emergencies (paramedics), weather strain (heat waves, cold snaps) |
| Life and story | (PA has little) | chronicle and weekly report; gang challenges, truces and initiations; informants; mentorships (an old hand and a newcomer); feuds, friendships, family news, birthdays, holidays, talent shows, big matches; innocence claims and exonerations; overdoses; inspections; celebrity inmates; staff lives (burnout, debts, retirement, rookies conned, officer of the month) |
| Courts and complaints | trials, complaints (PA has little) | murder trials (a charge, a prosecution built from who saw it, a camera, the weapon and the record, witnesses got at by the killer's gang, protective custody, a known informant who testifies and is exposed, a verdict on the strength of the case, by video from the hearing room or away at court) and inmates' complaints against officers (true or lied, an investigation that finds what the evidence shows, discipline that climbs, lawsuits, careers and morale) |
| Out of scope | escape mode | skipped (2026-10-17): the focus is the running prison, its events and its people |

## Roadmap (2026-10-17, from Fable's gap analysis, .work/gap-analysis-fable.md)
1. ~~Fear/safety need, vulnerable inmates and protective-custody requests; conduct-based reclassification
   (promotion/demotion between levels with payment consequences).~~ Done.
2. ~~Persistent suppression (punishment state that blocks reform), riots as a group (LordJob) that spreads, per-wing
   lockdown.~~ Done.
3. ~~Cell stash (contraband hidden in cells; cell searches and shakedowns find it), visitor reception and visitor
   searches, phone taps (intel).~~ Done.
4. Staff break need and staff room, ~~morale that changes guard quality~~ (done 2026-10-18), danger memories;
   ~~training~~ (done).
5. ~~Hazard events (kitchen fire, food poisoning, outbreaks), emergency callouts (paramedics, firemen)~~ (done), failure
   conditions (state takeover: open, the user's call), ~~a bailout~~ (the state loan, done).
6. Economic pressure: ~~grade-weighted payments~~ (pay by the grade), per-inmate upkeep, ~~credit~~ (the state loan), ~~export contracts~~
   (done 2026-10-18).
7. Prison life and story (priority from 2026-10-17, ahead of 4-6 where they overlap): more and better events
   driven by who is inside (feuds, friendships, news from home, gang power shifts, inspections, celebrity inmates,
   staff with lives of their own), each with causes, choices and consequences, and a prison history/chronicle that
   remembers them.
